\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}(E_{i})=2\,T^{+}_{\Phi,i}\wedge\!E_{i}\;,\!\quad\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}(T)=0\;,\!\quad\delta^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}(F_{i})=2\,T^{-}_{\Phi,i}\wedge\!F_{i}\;,\quad\forall\;i\in I\,,\,T\!\in{\mathfrak{h}}

where
\,\;T^{\pm}_{\Phi,i}=T^{\pm}_{i}\pm{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\overline{\phi_{kg}}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,H_{k}\;\,
for all
\,i\in I\,
.

In conclusion, one may consider the deformation
\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}
by the (Lie) twist
\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}\,
,  as well as the deformation
{\big(\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\big)}^{\mathcal{F}_{\Phi}}
of
U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})
by the (Hopf) twist
\mathcal{F}_{\Phi}\,
. By
[
GaGa2
, Theorem 6.2.2]
, we know that
{\big(\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\big)}^{\mathcal{F}_{\Phi}}
is a QUEA, whose semiclassical limit is
\,U\Big(\!{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}\Big)\,
:  indeed, we have
\;{\big(\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\big)}^{\mathcal{F}_{\Phi}}\cong\,U_{\!P_{\,\Phi},\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}_{\Phi}}({\mathfrak{g}})\;
and
\;{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{\overline{\mathfrak{J}}_{\Phi}}\cong\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\Phi}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}_{\Phi}}\;
.

3.2.
Deformations by 2-cocycle of QFSHA’s

We consider now deformations by 2-cocycle of QFSHA’s.
Due to the (linear) duality between the notions of QUEA and QFSHA,
and similarly for those of twist and 2-cocycle, the outcome we find is nothing but
the dual counterpart of Theorem
3.1.2
above
(and, consistently, it might be deduced
from the latter by duality).

Theorem 3.2.1
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA over the Poisson group
G
,
with tangent Lie bialgebra
\,{\mathfrak{g}}=\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}\,;\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
.
Let
\,\sigma
be a 2–cocycle for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
s.t.
\;\sigma\equiv\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\;\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,\Big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{\!*}\,\Big)\,
;  then

\,\varsigma:=\hbar^{-1}\log_{*}(\sigma)\in\Big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{\!*}\,
,  where “
\;\log_{*}
”
is the logarithm with respect to the convolution product, and

\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,
. Last, we set

\,\varsigma_{a}:=\varsigma-\,\varsigma_{2,1}\,
. Then:

(a)
\varsigma
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\varsigma_{2,1}=-\varsigma\,
, iff

\sigma
is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\sigma_{2,1}=\sigma^{-1}\,
;

(b)  the element

\,\overline{\varsigma_{a}}\,:=\,\varsigma_{a}\;\Big(\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,\Big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{\!*}\,\Big)\,

provides a well-defined element

\,\zeta\in{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{*}\!={\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\,

that is an

antisymmetric 2-cocycle

for the Lie bialgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
;

(c)  letting
\,\zeta\,
be as in claim (b),
the deformation
{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}
of
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
by the 2–cocycle

\sigma
is a QFSHA for the formal Poisson group
G_{\sigma}

with cotangent Lie bialgebra

{\textsl{Lie}\,(G_{\zeta})}^{*}\;=\;\,{(\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}_{\zeta}\,\;=\;\,\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,;\,{\big({[\,\ ,\ ]}_{*}\big)}_{\zeta}\,,\,\delta_{*}\,\big)

which is the deformation of
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
by the 2-cocycle
\zeta\,
; in short,

\;{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}\cong\,F_{\hbar}[[G_{\zeta}\hskip 0.5pt]]\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This is obvious, just by construction.

(b)
As a first step, we have to prove that

\,\overline{\varsigma_{a}}\,:=\,\varsigma_{a}\;\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,\Big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{\!*}\,\Big)\,

determines a uniquely defined element

\,\zeta\in{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{*}={\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\,
.
Hereafter we realize
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
as
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}={\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
where

\;{\mathfrak{m}}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F[[G\,]]}}\big)\;
;  then we have also

{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,\;=\;\,\Big({\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\Big)\otimes\Big({\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\Big)\,\;\cong\;\,\big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\big)\bigg/\Big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\Big)

thus in the end we have to prove that the function

\;\overline{\varsigma_{a}}\,:=\,\varsigma_{a}\;\big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,\big)\;

— defined from

\;{F[[G\,]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}}\;\big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,\big)\;
to
\Bbbk
— does vanish onto

\,{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\,
,   hence induces

\,\zeta\,
defined onto

\,{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{*}=\big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\big)\bigg/\Big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\Big)\,
by the canonical recipe

\;\zeta\,\big(\,\overline{u\otimes v}\,\big)\,:=\,\overline{\varsigma_{a}}\,(u\otimes v)\;

for each
\,u,v\in{\mathfrak{m}}\,
.
In fact, since
\,\varsigma_{a}\,
is antisymmetric it is enough to prove that

\;\overline{\varsigma_{a}}\,\big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\big)=0\;
;
in turn, this amounts to showing that — writing
\varsigma_{a}

as a bilinear function rather than a morphism on a tensor product module —
one has

\qquad\varsigma_{a}\big(a\,,b\,c\,\big)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,0\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;a,b,c\in J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)

(3.7)

For the given

\,a,b,c\in J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
,
the 2–cocycle nature of
\sigma
gives

\qquad\sigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,c_{(1)}\,\big)\,\sigma\big(a\,,b_{(2)}\,c_{(2)}\,\big)\,\;=\;\,\sigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\,\big)\,\sigma\big(a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,,c\,\big)

(3.8)

Now we expand
\sigma
as
(cf. §
2.3.1
for notation “
\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,
”)

\sigma\,\;=\;\,\exp_{*}\big(\,\hbar\,\varsigma\,\big)\,\;=\;\,\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=\;\,\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\,+\,\hbar\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}\otimes\varsigma^{\prime\prime}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

where we used sort of Sweedler’s-like notation

\;\varsigma\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}\otimes\varsigma^{\prime\prime}\;
to denote
\varsigma\,
;
plugging this into (
3.8
)
and expanding everything out, we end up with

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\epsilon(a)\,\epsilon(b)\,\epsilon(c)\,+\,\hbar\,\Big({\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(b\,c)\,+\,\epsilon(a)\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(b)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(c)\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\epsilon(a)\,\epsilon(b)\,\epsilon(c)\,+\,\hbar\,\Big({\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a\,b)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(c)\,+\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(b)\,\epsilon(c)\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\qquad

which implies — since
\,\epsilon(a)=0=\epsilon(c)\,

by assumption — also

\hbar\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(b\,c)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=\;\,\hbar\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a\,b)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(c)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

whence we argue

\qquad\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\;=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(b\,c\,)\,\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\varsigma}}\,\varsigma^{\prime}(a\,b)\,\varsigma^{\prime\prime}(c\,)\;=\;\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)

(3.9)

Recall also that
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is commutative modulo
\hbar\,
,  so that

\;x\,y\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,y\,x\,
for all
\,x,y\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Using this along with several instances of (
3.9
)
one gets

\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma(b\,a\,,c\,)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma(b\,,a\,c\,)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma(b\,,c\,a)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma(b\,c\,,a)

from which we eventually conclude that

\varsigma_{a}(a\,,b\,c\,)\;:=\;\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma(b\,c,\,a)\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\varsigma(b\,c,\,a)\,-\,\varsigma(b\,c,\,a)\;=\;0\quad,\qquad\text{q.e.d.}

As a second step, we note that
\,\zeta\,
is antisymmetric, by construction, since

\,\varsigma_{a}\,
is.

Third, we need to prove that

\,\zeta:{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk\,

satisfies the remaining condition of (
2.4
),
so that it is indeed a 2–cocycle. Now, expanding
\sigma
up to order 3, as

\sigma\,\;=\;\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\,\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,\;=\;\,\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\,+\,\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma^{*2}\big/2\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)

and plugging this into (
3.8
), we find, for all

\,a,b,c\in J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
again,

\hfil\displaystyle\bigg(\epsilon\big(b_{(1)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(c_{(1)}\big)\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,c_{(1)}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\Big({b_{(1)}}_{(1)},{c_{(1)}}_{(1)}\Big)\,\varsigma\Big({b_{(1)}}_{(2)},{c_{(1)}}_{(2)}\Big)\Big/2\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\bigg)\,\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\,\bigg(\epsilon(a)\,\epsilon\big(b_{(2)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\big(a\,,b_{(2)}\,c_{(2)}\big)\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\Big(a_{(1)}\,,{b_{(2)}}_{(1)}{c_{(2)}}_{(1)}\Big)\,\varsigma\Big(a_{(2)}\,,{b_{(2)}}_{(2)}{c_{(2)}}_{(2)}\Big)\Big/2\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\bigg)\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\bigg(\!\epsilon\big(a_{(1)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b_{(1)}\big)\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\Big(\!{a_{(1)}}_{(1)},{b_{(1)}}_{(1)}\!\Big)\,\varsigma\Big(\!{a_{(1)}}_{(2)},{b_{(1)}}_{(2)}\!\Big)\Big/2\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\!\bigg)\,\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\,\bigg(\epsilon\big(a_{(2)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b_{(2)}\big)\,\epsilon(c)\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,,c\big)\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\Big({a_{(2)}}_{(1)}{b_{(2)}}_{(1)},c\Big)\,\varsigma\Big({a_{(2)}}_{(2)}{b_{(2)}}_{(2)},c\Big)\Big/2\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\bigg)

and then performing multiplication and truncating at order 3 we get

\hfil\displaystyle\epsilon(a\,b\,c\,)\;+\;\hbar\,\big(\,\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\,+\,\epsilon(a)\,\varsigma(b\,,c\,)\big)\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;\hbar^{2}\,\Big(\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\big/2\,+\,\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,c_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a\,,b_{(2)}\,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\epsilon(a)\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,,c\,)\big/2\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\epsilon(a\,b\,c\,)\;+\;\hbar\,\big(\,\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)\,+\,\varsigma(a\,,b\,)\,\epsilon(c)\big)\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;\hbar^{2}\,\Big(\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,)\,\epsilon(c)\big/2\,+\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,,c\,\big)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,b\,,c\,)\big/2\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)

which in turn — since
\,\epsilon(a)=0=\epsilon(c)\,
by assumption —
simplifies into

\hfil\displaystyle\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\;+\;\hbar\,\Big(\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\big/2\,+\,\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,c_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a\,,b_{(2)}\,c_{(2)}\big)\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)\,+\,\hbar\,\Big(\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,,c\,\big)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,b\,,c\,)\big/2\,\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

that we eventually we re-write as

\displaystyle\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\;-\;\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)\;\;+

(3.10)

\displaystyle+\;\hbar\;\Big(\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\big/2\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,b\,,c\,)\big/2\;\;+

\displaystyle+\;\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,c_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a\,,b_{(2)}\,c_{(2)}\big)\,-\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,,c\,\big)\,\Big)\,\;\underset{\,\hbar^{2}}{\equiv}\;\,0

Now let
\,\Bbbk\big[\mathbb{S}_{3}\big]\,
act onto
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\otimes 3}

and consider in particular the action of the antisymmetrizer

\;\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}:=\big(\operatorname{id}-(1\,2)-(2\,3)-(3\,1)+(1\,2\,3)+(3\,2\,1)\big)\;

onto the equation in (
3.10
),
which yields a new equation: denoting equation
(
3.10
) by
\,\circledast=0\,
,  we will write

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)=0\,
for the newly found equation.
To see the latter explicitly, we compute the left-hand member

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)\,
,  starting by computing the action of

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\,
onto the
first line
in (
3.10
):
concretely, we find

\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\,\textsl{1${}^{\text{\,st}}$ line in\ }(\ref{eq: ident-sigma(a,b,c)})\big)\,\;=

(3.11)

\displaystyle=\;\,\varsigma(a\,,b\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma(b\,,a\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma(a\,,c\,b\,)\,-\,\varsigma(c\,,b\,a\,)\,+\,\varsigma(c\,,a\,b\,)\,+\,\varsigma(b\,,c\,a\,)\;-

\displaystyle-\;\varsigma(a\,b\,,c\,)\,+\,\varsigma(b\,a\,,c\,)\,+\,\varsigma(a\,c\,,b\,)\,+\,\varsigma(c\,b\,,a\,)\,-\,\varsigma(c\,a\,,b\,)\,-\,\varsigma(b\,c\,,a\,)\;=

\displaystyle=\;\,\varsigma_{a}\big(a\,,[b\,,c\,]\big)\;+\;\varsigma_{a}\big(b\,,[c\,,a\,]\big)\;+\;\varsigma_{a}\big(c\,,[a\,,b\,]\big)\,\;=\;\,\varsigma_{a}\big(a\,,[b\,,c\,]\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}

where standard notation
\,[\,u\,,v\,]:=u\,v-v\,u\,

is used to denote the usual commutator.
Modulo
\hbar\,
,  such a commutator in
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
yields the
Poisson bracket in
F[[G\,]]\,
,  hence we can write

\;[\,u\,,v\,]\,=\,\hbar\,\big\{\,\overline{u}\,,\overline{v}\,\big\}^{\prime}\;

where we use notation

\;\overline{z}:=\big(z\ \textrm{mod}\ \hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)\;

for each
\,z\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
and
\,\textrm{f\,}^{\prime}:=
some lift in
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
of any

\,\textrm{f}\in F[[G\,]]\,
,  i.e.
\,\overline{\textrm{f\,}^{\prime}}=\textrm{f}\,
;
note that
\textrm{f\,}^{\prime}
is only defined up to
\,\hbar^{2}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
yet that is enough for our purposes.
Thanks to this, (
3.11
) turns into

\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\textsl{1${}^{\text{\,st}}$ line in\ }(\ref{eq: ident-sigma(a,b,c)})\big)\,\;=\;\,\hbar\,\bigg(\,\varsigma_{a}\Big(a\,,\big\{\overline{b}\,,\overline{c}\,\big\}^{\prime}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\bigg)

(3.12)

Looking at (
3.10
), this entails that the
\hbar
–adic
expansion of
\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)\,
has zero term at order 0 ,
while at order 1 it also has a contribution coming from
(
3.12
).

Now we go and compute the contribution to

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)\,
issuing from the third line in
(
3.10
). Again, direct calculations give

\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\textsl{3${}^{\text{\,rd}}$ line in\ }(\ref{eq: ident-sigma(a,b,c)})\big)\,\;=\;\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,c\,,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}

(3.13)

Since one always has
\,x=\epsilon(x)+x_{+}\,
with

\,x_{+}:=\big(x-\epsilon(x)\big)\in\textsl{Ker}(\epsilon)\,
,
applying this to each element
\,x\in\{a\,,b\,,c\,\}\,

occurring in (
3.13
),
then expanding everything and taking into account that

\,\;\varsigma_{a}\big(J_{\hbar}\,,J_{\hbar}^{\,2}\big)\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,0\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,\varsigma_{a}\big(J_{\hbar}^{\,2},J_{\hbar}\big)\;\,

— cf. (
3.7
) — we obtain

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\textsl{3${}^{\text{\,rd}}$ line in\ }(\ref{eq: ident-sigma(a,b,c)})\big)\,\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,a\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,c\,,b_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,a_{(1)}\,,b\,\big)\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,c\,,a_{(2)}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,a\,,b^{\,\wedge}_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,c\,,b^{\,\wedge}_{(2)}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}

where we make use of short-hand notation

\;x^{\,\wedge}_{(1)}\otimes x^{\,\wedge}_{(2)}:=x_{(1)}\otimes x_{(2)}-x_{(2)}\otimes x_{(1)}\;
.

Finally, we go and compute the contribution to

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)\,
issuing from the second line in
(
3.10
). Dropping the coefficients

\,\hbar\,
and
\,1\big/2\,
we find the following:

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\textsl{2${}^{\text{\,nd}}$ line in\ }(\ref{eq: ident-sigma(a,b,c)})\big)\;=\;\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,,a\,c\,)\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,,a\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,c\,b\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(c\,,b\,a\,)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(c\,,a\,b\,)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,,c\,a\,)\;-

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad-\;\varsigma^{*2}(a\,b\,,c\,)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,a\,,c\,)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,c\,,b\,)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}(c\,b\,,a\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(c\,a\,,b\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,c\,,a\,)\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;\,\varsigma^{*2}\big(a\,,[b\,,c\,]\big)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}\big(b\,,[c\,,a\,]\big)\,+\,\varsigma^{*2}\big(c\,,[a\,,b\,]\big)\;-

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\;\varsigma^{*2}([b\,,c\,]\,,a\,\big)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}([c\,,a\,]\,,b\,\big)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}([a\,,b\,]\,,c\,\big)

which in turn implies

\,\;\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\big(\,\varsigma^{*2}(a\,,b\,c\,)\,-\,\varsigma^{*2}(b\,,a\,c\,)\big)\;=\;\mathcal{O}(\hbar)\;\,

— since
\,[\,u\,,v\,]=\mathcal{O}(\hbar)\,
for all
\,u,v\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
The outcome then is that the contribution to
\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}(\circledast)\,

given by the second line in (
3.10
) is trivial modulo

\hbar^{2}
.

Summing up, the outcome of the previous analysis is that

\varsigma_{a}\Big(a\,,\big\{\overline{b}\,,\overline{c}\,\big\}^{\prime}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\;+\;\varsigma_{a}\big(\,a\,,b^{\,\wedge}_{(1)}\big)\,\varsigma_{a}\big(\,c\,,b^{\,\wedge}_{(2)}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\,0

Taking the latter modulo
\,\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
we find

\varsigma_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}\,,\big\{\overline{b}\,,\overline{c}\,\big\}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\;+\;\varsigma_{a}\Big(\,\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}^{\,\wedge}_{(1)}\Big)\,\varsigma_{a}\Big(\,\overline{c}\,,\overline{b}^{\,\wedge}_{(2)}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\;\,0

for the elements
\,\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,,\overline{c}\in F[[G\,]]\,
.

Now recall that for
\,x\in J_{\hbar}\,
with

\,\overline{x}:=\big(\,x\text{\ mod\ }\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)\,
and

\,\text{x}:=\big(\,\overline{x}\text{\ mod\ }{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\big)\,

we have

\;\delta(\text{x})\,:=\,\text{x}^{\,\wedge}_{(1)}\otimes\text{x}^{\,\wedge}_{(2)}\;

for the induced Lie cobracket of
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}={\mathfrak{m}}\big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,

computed on
x
,
by definition; this means that, using our previously established notation

\;\delta(\text{x})\,:=\,\text{x}_{[1]}\otimes\text{x}_{[2]}\,
,
the last formula above yields

\zeta\big(\,\text{a}\,,[\,\text{b}\,,\text{c}\,]\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\;+\;\zeta\big(\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}_{[1]}\big)\,\zeta\big(\,\text{c}\,,\text{b}_{[2]}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\;\,0

(3.14)

Finally, the antisymmetry of
\zeta
gives

\,\;\zeta\big(\,\text{a}\,,[\,\text{b}\,,\text{c}\,]\big)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\;=\;-\zeta\big(\,[\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,]\,,\text{c}\,\big)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\;
,
while a straightforward check shows that

\,\;\zeta\big(\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}_{[1]}\big)\,\zeta\big(\,\text{c}\,,\text{b}_{[2]}\big)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\;=\;-{[[\,\zeta\,,\zeta\,]]}_{*}\;\,
. Therefore, (
3.14
)
is equivalent to

\zeta\big(\,[\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,]\,,\text{c}\,\big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\;+\;{[[\,\zeta\,,\zeta\,]]}_{*}\,\;=\;\,0

which means that
\,\zeta\,
is indeed a (strong type of) 2–cocycle for

{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
,  q.e.d.

(c)
Let us consider the deformed algebra

{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}\,
, which coincides with
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
as a

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module but is endowed with the deformed multiplication
“
\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}
” defined by

a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\;:=\;\sigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\sigma^{-1}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\qquad\qquad\forall\;a,b\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

(3.15)

As
\sigma
is of the form
\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,
,
in particular it is trivial modulo
\hbar\,
,  it follows from
(
3.15
) that the deformed multiplication
“
\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}
”
coincides with the old one modulo
\hbar\,
,
that is
\;a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\,\equiv\,a\,b\;\;\big(\,\text{mod\ }\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)\;
.
Therefore,
{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}
is again commutative modulo

\hbar\,
, hence it is (again) a QFSHA, as claimed, say

\,{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}=F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G_{(\sigma)}\big]\big]\,
.
Then, in order to prove that the newly found Poisson (formal) group
G_{(\sigma)}
is indeed

G_{\zeta}
as claimed it is enough to show that the
Lie bracket induced in
\,{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}\big/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}^{\,2}\,

— where
\,{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F[[G_{(\sigma)}]]}}\big)\,

— is indeed
\,{[\ \,,\ ]}_{\zeta}\,
.

Let us take
\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}\big/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}^{\,2}\,
;
then we can pick
\,a,b\in J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,

such that

\;\text{a}=a\;\Big(\text{mod\ }\big(\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}+J_{\hbar}^{\,2}\big)\Big)\;
and

\;\text{b}=b\;\Big(\text{mod\ }\big(\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}+J_{\hbar}^{\,2}\big)\Big)\;
.
Now, using the expansion

\,\;\sigma\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,=\,\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\,+\,\hbar\,\varsigma\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\;
,
formula (
3.15
) turns into

\hfil\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\,\big(\epsilon\big(a_{(1)}\big)\epsilon\big(b_{(1)}\big)+\hbar\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\big(\epsilon\big(a_{(3)}\big)\epsilon\big(b_{(3)}\big)+\hbar\,\varsigma\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\epsilon\big(a_{(1)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\epsilon\big(a_{(3)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b_{(3)}\big)\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\,\hbar\,\Big(\varsigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}b_{(2)}\,\epsilon\big(a_{(3)}\big)\epsilon\big(b_{(3)}\big)\,-\,\epsilon\big(a_{(1)}\big)\epsilon\big(b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}b_{(2)}\,\varsigma\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\Big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,a\,b\;+\;\hbar\,\Big(\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,-\,a_{(1)}\,b_{(1)}\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,,b_{(2)}\big)\Big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

where we took into account coassociativity and counitality properties.
Therefore, using “
\,{[\ \,,\ ]}_{\sigma}\,
” and “
\,[\ \,,\ ]\,
”
to denote the commutator with respect to the new (deformed) and the old
(undeformed) multiplication, we also have

\hfil\displaystyle{[a\,,b\,]}_{\sigma}\,\;:=\;\,a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\,-\,b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,a\,b\;+\;\hbar\,\Big(\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,-\,a_{(1)}\,b_{(1)}\,\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,,b_{(2)}\big)\Big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;-

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill-\;\,b\,a\;-\;\hbar\;\Big(\,\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,a_{(1)}\big)\,b_{(2)}\,a_{(2)}\,-\,b_{(1)}\,a_{(1)}\,\varsigma\big(b_{(2)}\,,a_{(2)}\big)\Big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,a\,b\,-\,b\,a\,\;+\;\,\hbar\;\Big(-\varsigma\big(a_{(2)}\,,b_{(2)}\big)\,a_{(1)}\,b_{(1)}\,+\,\varsigma\big(b_{(2)}\,,a_{(2)}\big)\,b_{(1)}\,a_{(1)}\,\;-\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\quad\qquad\quad-\;\,\varsigma\big(b_{(1)}\,,a_{(1)}\big)\,b_{(2)}\,a_{(2)}\,+\,\varsigma\big(a_{(1)}\,,b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\Big)\,\;+\;\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,[\,a\,,b\,]\,\;+\;\,\hbar\;\Big(-\varsigma_{a}\big(a_{(2)}\,,b_{(2)}\big)\,a_{(1)}\,b_{(1)}\,-\,\varsigma_{a}\big(b_{(1)}\,,a_{(1)}\big)\,b_{(2)}\,a_{(2)}\,\Big)\,\;+\;\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

where for the last step we used the fact that

\;a_{(s)}\,b_{(s)}\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,b_{(s)}\,a_{(s)}\;
.

Recall that

\;[\,a\,,b\,]\,=\,\hbar\,{\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}}^{\prime}\,
,
where hereafter we write

\,\overline{x}:=x\;\big(\,\text{mod\ }\hbar\,J_{\hbar}\big)\,
and

\,\text{f\,}^{\prime}\,
to denote any lift in
J_{\hbar}
of some given
f

in
J_{\hbar}\,
,  as we did before; similarly, we have

\;{[\,a\,,b\,]}_{\sigma}=\,\hbar\,{\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}}_{\sigma}^{\prime}\,
.
Then modulo
\,\hbar\,
our previous computations give

{\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}}_{\sigma}\,\;=\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\,\overline{a}_{(2)}

(3.16)

For each
\,x\in\big\{\,\overline{a}_{(s)}\,,\overline{b}_{(s)}\,\big|\,s=1,2\,\big\}\,
we have

\,x=\epsilon(x)+x^{+}\,
with

\,x^{+}:=\big(x-\epsilon(x)\big)\in J_{\hbar}\,
.
Using such expansions in either factor of the products

\,\overline{a}_{(s)}\,\overline{b}_{(s)}\,
and

\,\overline{b}_{(s)}\,\overline{a}_{(s)}\,

occurring within (
3.16
),
and noting that

\;\overline{a}_{(s)}^{\,+}\,\overline{b}_{(s)}^{\,+}\,\underset{\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}}{\equiv}\,0\,\underset{\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}}{\equiv}\,\overline{a}_{(s)}^{\,+}\,\overline{b}_{(s)}^{\,+}\,
,  we get
an equivalence modulo
\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}^{\,2}\,

(noting that
\,{\mathfrak{m}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}\,
as
\Bbbk
–modules), namely

\hfil\displaystyle{\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}}_{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\;=\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,\;\underset{\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}}{\equiv}\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\underset{\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}}{\equiv}\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\,\epsilon\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\big)\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\;-

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\,\epsilon\big(\overline{a}_{(2)}\big)\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}\,\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}_{(2)}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}\,\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}\,,\overline{a}_{(1)}\big)\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,,\overline{b}\,\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\;+\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\,,\overline{a}\big)\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\;-\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,,\overline{a}\,\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}\;+\;\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(1)}\,,\overline{b}\,\big)\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big\{\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\,\big\}\,-\,\big(\,\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(2)},\overline{b}\,\big)\,\overline{a}_{(1)}-\,\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{a}_{(1)},\overline{b}\,\big)\,\overline{a}_{(2)}\,\big)\,-\,\big(\,\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(1)},\overline{a}\,\big)\,\overline{b}_{(2)}-\,\overline{\varsigma}_{a}\big(\,\overline{b}_{(2)},\overline{a}\,\big)\,\overline{b}_{(1)}\,\big)

where the element in last line actually belongs to
\,{\mathfrak{m}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}\,
.
When we reduce all this modulo
\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}^{\,2}\,
,
and recalling the definition of the Lie bracket (for either Lie algebra structure)
as being induced by the Poisson bracket, and that of the Lie cobracket in

\,{\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\sigma}^{\,2}\,

— which coincide as Lie coalgebras — as being induced by

\,\Delta-\Delta^{\text{op}}\,
,  we eventually end up with

{[\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,]}_{(\sigma)}\,\;=\;\,{[\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,]}_{*}\;-\;\zeta\big(\text{a}_{[2]},\text{b}\big)\,\text{a}_{[1]}\;-\;\zeta\big(\,\text{b}_{[1]},\text{a}\big)\,\text{b}_{[2]}\,\;=:\;\,{\big({[\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,]}_{*}\big)}_{\zeta}

thus(cf. Definition
2.5
)
the Lie bracket we were looking for is just

\,{\big({[\ \,,\ ]}_{*}\big)}_{\zeta}\,
.
∎

Observation 3.2.2
.

We would better point out that the 2-cocycles

\sigma
considered in
Theorem
3.2.1

above are those of “trivial-modulo-
\hbar
-type”,
in that they are the identity modulo
\hbar\,
.
With this assumption, deforming
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

by such a
\sigma
does not affect the Hopf structure
of the semiclassical limit;
in particular, it still reads as

F\big[\big[\widetilde{G}\hskip 0.5pt\big]\big]\,
, with

\widetilde{G}
being the same formal group as
G

but with a different Poisson
structure. A more general 2-cocycle might be “unfit”,
in that the deformed Hopf algebra

{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}_{\sigma}
may no longer be a QFSHA,
in general.

Example 3.2.3
.

Let
\,G:=\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\,
be the general linear
group over
\Bbbk\,
,  and

\,{\mathfrak{g}}:=\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
its tangent Lie algebra.
It is well-known — cf.
[
Dr
]
,
[
CP
]
—
that a quantization of
{\mathfrak{g}}
is provided by the QUEA

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}=U_{\hbar}\big(\,\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\hskip-1.0pt\big)\,

defined as follows: it is the unital, associative,

\hbar
–adically complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra with
generators

F_{1}\,,F_{2}\,,\dots,F_{n-1}\,,\varGamma_{1}\,,\varGamma_{2}\,,\dots,\varGamma_{n-1}\,,\varGamma_{n}\,,E_{1}\,,E_{2}\,,\dots,E_{n-1}

and relations (for all
\,i,j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}\,
,

\,k,\ell\in\{1,\dots,n\}\,
)

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\varGamma_{k}\,,\varGamma_{\ell}\big]\,=\,0\;\;,\quad\big[\varGamma_{k}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,-\delta_{k,j}\,F_{j}\;\;,\quad[\varGamma_{k}\,,E_{j}]\,=\,+\delta_{k,j}\,E_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle\big[E_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,\delta_{i,j}\,{{\;\;e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i}-\varGamma_{i+1})}-e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i+1}-\varGamma_{i})}\;\;}\over{\;\;e^{+\hbar}-e^{-\hbar}\;\;}}

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]\,=\,0\;\;,\qquad\big[F_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|>1\,\phantom{.}\;

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill E_{i}^{2}\,E_{j}-\left(q+q^{-1}\right)E_{i}\,E_{j}\,E_{i}+E_{j}\,E_{i}^{2}\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|=1\,\phantom{.}\;

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill F_{i}^{2}\,F_{j}-\left(q+q^{-1}\right)F_{i}\,F_{j}\,F_{i}+F_{j}\,F_{i}^{2}\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|=1\,.\;

where
\,[X\,,Y]:=X\,Y-Y\,X\,
.
The (topological) Hopf algebra structure is given by

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta(F_{i})\,=\,F_{i}\otimes e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i+1}-\varGamma_{i})}+1\otimes F_{i}\;,\;\qquad S(F_{i})\,=\,-F_{i}\,e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i}-\varGamma_{i+1})}\;,\;\qquad\epsilon(F_{i})\,=\,0

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\,\Delta(\varGamma_{k})\,=\,\varGamma_{k}\otimes 1+1\otimes\varGamma_{k}\;,\qquad\qquad\qquad S(\varGamma_{k})\,=\,-\varGamma_{k}\;,\quad\quad\qquad\epsilon(\varGamma_{k})\,=\,0

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta(E_{i})\,=\,E_{i}\otimes 1+e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i}-\varGamma_{i+1})}\otimes E_{i}\;,\qquad S(E_{i})\,=\,-e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma_{i+1}-\varGamma_{i})}\,E_{i}\;,\qquad\epsilon(E_{i})\,=\,0

It is also well-known — cf.
[
Dr
]
,
[
CP
]

— that a quantization of
\,G:=\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\,

is provided by the QFSHA

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}=F_{\hbar}\big[\big[\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\big]\big]\,

defined as follows: it is the unital, associative,

I_{\hbar}
–adically complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra
generated by the elements of the set

\,\big\{x_{ij}\,\big|\,i,j=1,\ldots,n+1\big\}

arranged in a
q
–matrix, with

\,q:=\exp(\hbar)\,
,  with
I_{\hbar}

being the ideal generated by

\,\big\{\hbar\,,x_{1,1}\,,\dots,x_{n,n}\big\}\,
;
this is a quick way to say that the given generators
obey the relations

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill x_{ij}\,x_{ik}\,=\,q\,x_{ik}\,x_{ij}\;,\quad\quad x_{ik}\,x_{hk}\,=\,q\,x_{hk}\,x_{ik}\hfill\forall\;\;j<k\,,i<h\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill x_{il}\,x_{jk}\,=\,x_{jk}\,x_{il}\;,\quad\quad x_{ik}\,x_{jl}-x_{jl}\,x_{ik}\,=\,\left(q-q^{-1}\right)\,x_{il}\,x_{jk}\hfill\forall\;\;i<j\,,k<l\quad

whereas the comultiplication
\Delta\,
, the counit

\epsilon\,
,
and the antipode
S
are given by matrix formulation

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\Big(\!{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\Big)\,:=\,{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\otimes{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}

\hfil\displaystyle\epsilon\Big(\!{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\Big)\,:=\,{\big(\delta_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\quad,\qquad S\Big(\!{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\Big)\,:=\,{\Big(\!{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\Big)}^{-1}

which in down-to-earth terms read, for all

\,i,j=1,\dots,n\,
,

\Delta\big(x_{ij}\big)\,=\,{\textstyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}}x_{ik}\otimes x_{kj}\;\;,\qquad\epsilon\big(x_{ij}\big)\,=\,\delta_{ij}\;\;,\qquad S\big(x_{ij}\big)\,=\,{(-q)}^{j-i}D_{q}\!\left(\!{\big(x_{hk}\big)}_{h\neq j}^{k\neq i}\right)

where
D_{q}
denotes the so-called

quantum determinant
, defined on any

q
–matrix of (square) size
\ell
by

D_{q}\Big(\!{\big(x_{ij}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,\ell;}^{j=1,\dots,\ell;}\Big)\,:=\,{\textstyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{\ell}}}{(-q)}^{l(\sigma)}x_{1,\sigma(1)}\,x_{2,\sigma(2)}\cdots x_{\ell,\sigma(\ell)}

We have also explicit identifications

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\ast}\,
as well as

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
,
which can be described via the Hopf pairing

\;\langle\,\ ,\ \rangle:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\times{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

uniquely given by the following values on generators:

\big\langle x_{i,j}\,,\varGamma_{k}\big\rangle\,=\,\delta_{i,j}\,\delta_{i,k}\;\;,\quad\big\langle x_{i,j}\,,E_{t}\big\rangle\,=\,\delta_{i+1,j}\,\delta_{i,t}\;\;,\quad\big\langle x_{i,j}\,,F_{t}\big\rangle\,=\,\delta_{i,j+1}\,\delta_{t,j}

(3.17)

Now consider in
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

the element

\,\mathcal{F}:=\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{n}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\Big)\,

that is a twist for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
— just as in
Example
3.1.4
,
it trivially follows from the fact that the
\varGamma_{t}
’s
are primitive.
By Proposition
2.2.7
(a)
,
we can see this
\mathcal{F}
as a 2–cocycle

\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
simply given by
evaluation at
\mathcal{F}\,
, namely

\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\,:\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\times{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\quad,\qquad(\varphi\,,\psi)\,\mapsto\,\big\langle\,\varphi\otimes\psi\,,\,\mathcal{F}\,\big\rangle

(3.18)

Now, from (
3.18
) and (
3.17
), direct calculation gives

\hfil\displaystyle\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big(x_{i,\,r}\,,x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\;=\;\big\langle\,x_{i,\,r}\otimes x_{\ell,\,h}\,,\,\mathcal{F}\,\big\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,\left\langle\,x_{i,\,r}\otimes x_{\ell,\,h}\,,\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{m}\,\right\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,\left\langle\,\Delta^{(m-1)}\big(x_{i,\,r}\otimes x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\,,\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{\otimes m}\,\right\rangle

Let us stop and consider
\;\left\langle\,\Delta^{(m-1)}\big(x_{i,\,r}\otimes x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\,,\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{\otimes m}\,\right\rangle\;
.
Definitions give

\hfil\displaystyle\left\langle\,\Delta^{(m-1)}\big(x_{i,\,r}\otimes x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\,,\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{\otimes m}\,\right\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1},\dots,\,s_{m-1}=1\\
e_{1},\dots,\,e_{m-1}=1\end{subarray}}^{n}}\!\left\langle x_{i,\,s_{1}}\otimes x_{\ell,\,e_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{s_{m-1},\,r}\otimes x_{e_{m-1},\,h}\,,{\left({\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{\otimes m}\right\rangle\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1},\dots,\,s_{m-1}=1\\
e_{1},\dots,\,e_{m-1}=1\end{subarray}}^{n}}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{c=1}^{m}}\left\langle x_{s_{c-1},\,s_{c}}\otimes x_{e_{c-1},\,e_{c}}\,,\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\,\right\rangle\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1},\dots,\,s_{m-1}=1\\
e_{1},\dots,\,e_{m-1}=1\end{subarray}}^{n}}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{c=1}^{m}}\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\big\langle x_{s_{c-1},\,s_{c}}\,,\varGamma_{t}\big\rangle\,\big\langle x_{e_{c-1},\,e_{c}}\,,\varGamma_{k}\,\big\rangle

where we set
\,s_{0}:=i\,
,
\,s_{m}:=r\,
,
\,e_{0}:=\ell\,
,
\,e_{m}:=h\,
.
Now, the formulas in (
3.17
) guarantee that

\;\big\langle x_{s_{c-1},\,s_{c}}\,,\varGamma_{t}\big\rangle\,\big\langle x_{e_{c-1},\,e_{c}}\,,\varGamma_{k}\,\big\rangle\,=\,0\;

whenever
\,s_{c-1}\not=s_{c}\,
or
\,e_{c-1}\not=e_{c}\,
;
therefore, from the previous computation one eventually gets

\hfil\displaystyle\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big(x_{i,\,r}\,,x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,\left\langle\,\Delta^{(m-1)}\big(x_{i,r}\otimes x_{\ell,h}\big)\,,\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\varGamma_{t}\otimes\varGamma_{k}\right)}^{\otimes m}\,\right\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\quad=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\big\langle x_{i,\,i}\,,\varGamma_{t}\big\rangle\,\big\langle x_{\ell,\,\ell}\,,\varGamma_{k}\,\big\rangle\right)}^{m}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,{\left(\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\,k=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{t,k}\,\delta_{i,t}\,\delta_{\ell,k}\right)}^{m}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{m=0}^{+\infty}}{{\,\hbar^{m}\,2^{-m}\,}\over{\,m!\,}}\,{(\phi_{i,\ell})}^{m}\;=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,\exp\big(\hbar\,2^{-1}\phi_{i,\ell}\big)\;=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,e^{\hbar\,\phi_{i,\ell}/2}\hfill

in short

\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big(x_{i,\,r}\,,x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\;=\;\delta_{i,r}\,\delta_{\ell,h}\,e^{\hbar\,\phi_{i,\ell}/2}\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\,,r,\ell,h\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(3.19)

Using this formula, the deformed product in
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}

can be described as follows:

\hfil\displaystyle x_{i,\,j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\;\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}$}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\;:=\;\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big({(x_{i,\,j})}_{(1)}\,,\,{(x_{\ell,\,t})}_{(1)}\big)\;{(x_{i,\,j})}_{(2)}\,{(x_{\ell,\,t})}_{(2)}\;\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}^{-1}\big({(x_{i,\,j})}_{(3)}\,,\,{(x_{\ell,\,t})}_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{r,\,s,\,h,\,k=1}^{n}}\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big(x_{i,\,r}\,,\,x_{\ell,\,h}\big)\;x_{r,\,s}\,x_{h,\,k}\;\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}^{-1}\big(x_{s,\,j}\,,\,x_{k,\,t}\big)\;=\quad\qquad\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\big(x_{i,\,i}\,,\,x_{\ell,\,\ell}\big)\;x_{i,\,j}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\;\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}^{-1}\big(x_{j,\,j}\,,\,x_{t,\,t}\big)\;=\;e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2}\;x_{i,\,j}\,x_{\ell,\,t}

— where we used (
3.19
); in short, we get

x_{i,\,j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\;\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}$}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\;=\;e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2}\;x_{i,\,j}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\,,j,\ell,t\in\{1,\dots,n\}

(3.20)

Note that this formula shows how the new, deformed product is equivalent modulo

\,\hbar\,
to the old one: this is a general fact, due to the very construction,
namely because we are working with 2–cocycles of the form

\,\exp\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,
where
\,\varsigma\,

is some bilinear form on the QFSHA to be deformed. By this same reason,

any set of elements which generate, as an algebra, the QFSHA under exam,
will also generate it (as an algebra again) w.r.t. the new, deformed product
.
For this reason, the formula (
3.20
)
is enough to describe the deformed algebra
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}

as the latter is (again) generated — w.r.t. the new product —
by the
x_{i,\,j}
’s, just like
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
was (with the old product).

Let us now see how (
3.20
)
yields a modified Poisson bracket in the semiclassical limit of

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,
. Using such notation as

\;\overline{x}_{r,\,s}:=x_{r,\,s}\ \big(\,\text{mod}\,\ \hbar\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\big)\,
,  the Poisson bracket inherited from

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,
is given, by definition, by

{\big\{\overline{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big\}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,\;:=\;\;{{\;{\big[{x}_{i,\,j}\,,{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\;}\over{\;\hbar\;}}\ \ \Big(\,\text{mod}\,\ \hbar\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\Big)

Now

\hfil\displaystyle{\big[{x}_{i,\,j}\,,{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,=\;x_{i,\,j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\;\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}$}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\,-\,x_{\ell,\,t}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\;\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}$}\,x_{i,\,j}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2}\;x_{i,\,j}\,x_{\ell,\,t}\,-\,e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{j,t}\,-\,\phi_{i,\ell})/2}\;x_{\ell,\,t}\,x_{i,\,j}\;=\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2}\,\big[x_{i,\,j}\,,x_{\ell,\,t}\big]\,+\,\big(e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2}-e^{\hbar\,(\phi_{j,t}\,-\,\phi_{i,\ell})/2}\big)\,x_{\ell,\,t}\,x_{i,\,j}

hence expanding the exponentials we get

{\big[{x}_{i,\,j}\,,{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,=\;\big(1+\hbar\,(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t})/2\big)\,\big[x_{i,\,j}\,,x_{\ell,\,t}\big]\,+\,\hbar\,\big(\phi_{i,\ell}\,-\,\phi_{j,t}\big)\,x_{\ell,\,t}\,x_{i,\,j}\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)

from which we eventually get

{\big\{\overline{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big\}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,:=\;\big\{\overline{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big\}\,+\,\Big(\,\overline{\phi}_{i,\ell}\,-\,\overline{\phi}_{j,t}\Big)\,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\,\overline{x}_{i,\,j}

(3.21)

where
\,\big\{\overline{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big\}\,

denotes the old (undeformed) Poisson bracket and we took into account that

\;\big[\overline{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\overline{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]\,=\,0\;

and that the deformed and undeformed product do coincide modulo
\hbar\,
.

In addition, the formula (
3.21
)
for the Poisson bracket also induces, following the general recipe,
a concrete description of the modified Lie bracket in the cotangent Lie bialgebra

\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}:={\mathfrak{m}}\big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
,  where
{\mathfrak{m}}
is the augmentation ideal of

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,
. Indeed, the latter has as
\Bbbk
–basis the set of cosets

\,\big(\text{modulo\ }{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)

\Big\{\,\text{x}_{i,\,j}:=\big(\overline{x}_{i,\,j}-\delta_{i,\,j}\big)\,\ \text{mod}\,\ {\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\Big|\,i\,,j=1,\dots,n\,\Big\}

and for these elements from (
3.21
)
we deduce the deformed Lie bracket as given by

\hfil\displaystyle{\big[\text{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\;\big[\text{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]\;\;\;,\quad{\big[\text{x}_{i,\,i}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,\ell}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\;\big[\text{x}_{i,\,i}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,\ell}\big]\quad\qquad\forall\;\;i\not=j\,,\;\ell\not=t

\hfil\displaystyle{\big[\text{x}_{i,\,i}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,=\;\big[\text{x}_{i,\,i}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]\,+\,\Big(\,\overline{\phi}_{i,\ell}\,-\,\overline{\phi}_{i,t}\Big)\,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;\ell\not=t

\hfil\displaystyle{\big[\text{x}_{i,\,j}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,\ell}\big]}_{\sigma_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\,:=\;\big[\text{x}_{i,\,i}\,,\text{x}_{\ell,\,t}\big]\,+\,\Big(\,\overline{\phi}_{i,\ell}\,-\,\overline{\phi}_{j,\ell}\Big)\,\text{x}_{i,\,j}\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\not=j

3.3.
Deformations by polar 2–cocycle of QUEA’s

This subsection is dedicated to deformations by polar 2–cocycle of QUEA’s.
In this case the result that we achieve is somewhat surprising, in that we are indeed
“stretching the standard recipe”, as the 2-cocycles that we use to deform our Hopf

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebras are valued in the field
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
rather than in our ground ring
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
.
Therefore,
a priori
nothing even guarantees that the recipe would just work
and produce a new Hopf algebra over
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
;  nonetheless, we eventually find quite a
meaningful result, which also says that
the standard procedure of deformation by
twist for QUEA’s can be extended somewhat beyond its natural borders
.

We begin with two ancillary results.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
be any QUEA, and

\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{U_{\hbar}}\big)\,
. For every
\,z\in U_{\hbar}\,
,
there exists
\,N\in\mathbb{N}\,
such that

\;\delta_{n}(z)\,\in\,\hbar^{\,\max(n,N)-N}J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}\;
for every

\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
.

Proof.

As Drinfeld’s functors are inverse to each other
— cf. Theorem
2.4.2
(a)
— applying
{(\ )}^{\vee}
after
{(\ )}^{\prime}

to the QUEA
U_{\hbar}
we get
\,U_{\hbar}={\big(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{\!\vee}\,
:
letting
\,I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}+\textsl{Ker}\!\big(\epsilon_{U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}}\big)\,
, this last identity reads

U_{\hbar}\,=\,\hbar\text{-adic completion of\ }\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}\hbar^{-n}{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{n}\,=\,\hbar\text{-adic completion of\ }\bigcup\limits_{n\geq 0}\hbar^{-n}{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{n}

In particular, this implies that for our

\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
there exist some
\,N\in\mathbb{N}\,
and

\,z^{\prime}\in{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{N}\,
such that

\;z\,\equiv\hbar^{-N}z^{\prime}\,\big(\!\!\!\mod\hbar\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)\;
.
Now, given
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
we have

\,\delta_{n}\big(z^{\prime}\big)\in\hbar^{n}U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}\,

because

\,z^{\prime}\in{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{N}\subseteq U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
,
and also

\,\delta_{n}\big(z^{\prime}\big)\in\!\!{\textstyle\sum\limits_{s_{1}+\cdots+s_{n}=N}}\!\!\!\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\!{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{s_{i}}\,

because
I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}
is a Hopf ideal; moreover,

\,\textsl{Ker}\!\big(\epsilon_{U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}}\big)\subseteq\hbar\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
again by construction, hence

\,I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\subseteq\hbar\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
.
In the end, all this yields

\;\delta_{n}\big(z^{\prime}\big)\in\hbar^{\,\max(n,N)}\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}\,
,
therefore
\;\delta_{n}(z)\,\in\,\hbar^{\,\max(n,N)-N}J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}\;

as claimed.
∎

For the second, auxiliary result, we
fix some more notation: namely, hereafter by “
\log_{*}
” and “
\exp_{*}
”
we denote the logarithm and the exponential with respect to the
convolution product, whenever defined.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be any QUEA, and let
\chi
be a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear
form on
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
such that
\,\chi(z\,,1)=0=\chi(1\,,z)\,
for any

\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
;  denote also by the same symbol
\chi

the scalar extension of
\chi
to a
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–bilinear form for the

{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–vector space
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}:={\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\otimes_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Then:

(a)  the formal expression
\,\sigma:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,

uniquely provides a well-defined,
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–valued bilinear form for
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
;

(b)
\,\sigma(z\,,1)=\epsilon(z)=\sigma(1\,,z)\,
for any
\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
;

(c)
\sigma
is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\sigma_{2,1}\!=\sigma^{\,-1}
,
iff
\chi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\chi_{2,1}=-\chi\,
.

Proof.

(a)
Fix notation
\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
and

\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{U_{\hbar}}\big)\,
.
For any
\,z\in U_{\hbar}\,
, set

\hat{z}:=\epsilon(z)\,,\;\;\;z^{+}:=z-\epsilon(z)=z-\hat{z}\,\in\,J_{\hbar}\;,\quad\text{hence}\quad z=z^{+}+\hat{z}

(3.22)

The assumption
\,\chi(z\,,1)=0=\chi(1\,,z)\,
for
\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

implies (for all
\,u,v\in U_{\hbar}\,
)

\chi(u,v)\,=\,\chi\big(u^{+}\!+\hat{u}\,,v^{+}\!+\hat{v}\big)\,=\,\chi\big(u^{+},v^{+}\big)

(3.23)

Now, for any
\,a\,,b\in U_{\hbar}\,
, the formula

\;\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\,\big)\,=\,\sum_{n\,\geq 0}\hbar^{-n}\chi^{*\,n}\big/n!\;
gives

\displaystyle\sigma(a\,,b\,)\;=\,{\textstyle\sum_{n\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-n}\chi^{*\,n}(a\,,b\,)\Big/n!\,\;=

(3.24)

\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum_{n\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-n}{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a_{(i)},b_{(i)}\big)\Big/n!\;=\;{\textstyle\sum_{n\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-n}{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\Big/n!

where we took into account that
\,\chi^{*\,k}(u\,,v\,)\,=\,\prod_{s=1}^{k}\!\chi\big(u_{(s)},v_{(s)}\big)\,=\,\prod_{s=1}^{k}\!\chi\big(u^{+}_{(s)},v^{+}_{(s)}\big)\,
for each

\,u,v\in U_{\hbar}\,
,
\,k\in\mathbb{N}\,
, by definitions along with
(
3.23
). Now we notice that

\,\otimes_{i=1}^{n}a^{+}_{(i)}=\delta_{n}(a)\,
and

\,\otimes_{i=1}^{n}b^{+}_{(i)}=\delta_{n}(b)\,
,
hence Lemma
3.3.1
guarantees that

h^{-n}{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{-n+\,\max(n,A)-A+\max(n,B)-B}

[formula]

whence in particular

\displaystyle h^{-n}{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{-\min(A,B)}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\qquad\forall\;\;n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

(3.25)

\displaystyle h^{-n}{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{n-(A+B)}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\qquad\forall\;\;n\geq A+B

where
\,A\in\mathbb{N}\,
, resp.
\,B\in\mathbb{N}\,
,
plays for
a\,
, resp. for
b\,
, the role of
N
for
z
in
Lemma
3.3.1
above; by this, the formal expansion for

\sigma(a\,,b\,)
in (
3.24
) yields a well defined
element in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
, hence
\sigma
is a well-defined
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–bilinear
form of
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
as claimed.

(b–c)
Both claims are obvious, by construction,
as they follow from standard identities for formal exponentials.
∎

The previous result leads us to introduce the following notion:

Definition 3.3.3
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, and
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}:={\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\otimes_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
Note that
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
has a natural “Hopf algebra structure” of
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

induced by scalar extension from
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

— so that, in particular, the “coproduct” takes values in

\,{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\otimes_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\!\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)\,

rather than in
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\otimes_{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.

We call
polar 2-cocycle of

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
any
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–bilinear form

\,\sigma
of
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
which has the form

\,\sigma:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,
for some
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear
form
\,\chi\in{\Big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}2}\Big)}^{*}\,
of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
such that

\,\chi(z\,,1)=0=\chi(1\,,z)\,
for all
\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
and in addition enjoys the 2–cocycle properties with respect to the above
“Hopf algebra structure” of
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.

Remark 3.3.4
.

The notion of “polar 2-cocycle” for a QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

can also be cast in the following, equivalent shape.
Recall that
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
is a QFSHA
(cf. §
2.3.4
), and then

\,{\Big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}2}\Big)}^{*}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Given
\,\chi\in{\Big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}2}\Big)}^{*}\,
as in
Definition
3.3.3
above, the condition

\,\chi(z\,,1)=0=\chi(1\,,z)\,
for all
\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

means that
\,\chi\in J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\,
,
with
\,J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
,
hence we have

\;\chi\,\in\,\hbar^{2}\,{\big(J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\,\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\subseteq\,\hbar^{2}\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\;

where
\,J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\,\vee}:=\hbar^{-1}J_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\,
and
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}

is the QUEA defined in §
2.4
out of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Thus, it follows that

\;\hbar^{-1}\chi\,\in\,\hbar\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}
,
so
\,\sigma:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,

is a well-defined element in
{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,
.

Now, the requirement that
\,\sigma:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,

be a polar 2-cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
in the sense of
Definition
3.3.3
above is equivalent to the property of

\sigma
being a twist element for
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,

— which makes perfectly sense in sight of
Proposition
2.2.7
.

Clearly, every 2-cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is a polar 2–cocycle as well;
the converse, instead, is not true, in general (counterexamples do exist).
However, the key point is that
every polar 2–cocycle still provides
a well-defined deformation by 2-cocycle of
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

— in short, the construction of
deformations by 2–cocycle

does properly extend to
“deformations by
polar
2–cocycle”

as well: this is indeed our next result.

Theorem 3.3.5
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, and
\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,
a polar 2–cocycle for it, as in Definition
3.3.3
. Then the procedure of 2-cocycle deformation by
\,\sigma
applied to
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
actually restricts to
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
,  making the latter into a new QUEA.

Proof.

First of all, we have to explain the statement itself.
To begin with, note that, by definitions and by
Lemma
3.3.2
,
we can perform the deformation by the 2–cocycle
\sigma
onto the
“Hopf
\,{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–algebra”
\,{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}:={\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
Our statement then claims the resulting deformed Hopf structure onto
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

actually “restricts” to a deformation of
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
itself: in turn, this amounts to
claiming that
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is closed for the
\sigma
–deformed product in

{\big({\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}\,
— so we go and tackle this last problem.

Fix notation
\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(U_{\hbar}\big)\,
,

\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)\,
and

\,\widetilde{J_{\hbar}}:=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
, where

\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
is given in
Definition
2.4.1
(a)
.
As it was mentioned in the proof of Lemma
3.3.1
,
Theorem
2.4.2
(a)
implies that

\,U_{\hbar}={\big(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{\!\vee}\,
,  that is

U_{\hbar}\,=\,\hbar\text{-adic completion of\ }\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}\hbar^{-n}{\big(I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{n}

where
\,I_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}+\textsl{Ker}\!\big(\epsilon_{U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}}\big)=\hbar\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}+J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
; then a moment’s thought shows that the previous
expression of
U_{\hbar}
reads also

U_{\hbar}\,=\,\hbar\text{-adic completion of\ }{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}\hbar^{-n}{\big(J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)}^{n}\,=\,\hbar\text{-adic completion of\ }{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle n$}}

(3.26)

Note also that clearly
J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}
is a Hopf ideal in

U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}
, and moreover

\;J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,\subseteq\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}\;

(by construction); therefore for

\,z^{\prime}\in J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\,\scriptstyle N$}}\,

— with
\,N\in\mathbb{N}\,
—
acting like in the proof of Lemma
3.3.1
,
one sees that

\qquad\delta_{n}\big(z^{\prime}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{n}J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}\;{\textstyle\bigcap}\;\Big(\,{\textstyle\sum_{\sum_{i}N_{i}=N}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\,\scriptstyle N_{i}$}}\Big)\;\subseteq\;\hbar^{\,\max(n,N)}J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes n}

(3.27)

Again, for any
\,z\in U_{\hbar}\,
we retain notation as in (
3.22
)
above, that is

\hat{z}:=\epsilon(z)\,,\;\;\;z^{+}:=z-\epsilon(z)=z-\hat{z}\,\in\,J_{\hbar}\;,\quad\text{hence}\quad z=z^{+}+\hat{z}

(3.28)

and we recall also that for all
\,u,v\in U_{\hbar}\,
we have

\chi(u,v)\,=\,\chi\big(u^{+}\!+\hat{u}\,,v^{+}\!+\hat{v}\big)\,=\,\chi\big(u^{+},v^{+}\big)

(3.29)

Thanks to (
3.26
), in order to prove that

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}=:U_{\hbar}\,
is closed for the
\sigma
–deformed product

\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}

it is enough to show that

\;\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle A$}}\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\;\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}\;\subseteq{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle n$}}\;
for any
\,A\,,B\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
.

To begin with, we pick

\,a\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle A$}}=\hbar^{-A}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle A$}}\,
and

\,b\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}=\hbar^{-B}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}\,
;  by definition,

a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\;:=\;\sigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\sigma^{-1}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

whence expanding the formal formula

\;\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\,\big)\,=\,\sum_{n\,\geq 0}\hbar^{-n}\chi^{*\,n}\big/n!\;
— much like in the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
—
we get

\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\,\;=\;\,{\textstyle\sum_{t,\ell\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell)}\,{{(-1)}^{\ell}}\,{(t!\,\ell!)}^{-1}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\,\;=

(3.30)

\displaystyle=\;\,\epsilon\big(a^{\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b^{\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,a^{\prime}_{(2)}\,b^{\prime}_{(2)}\,\epsilon\big(a^{\prime}_{(3)}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b^{\prime}_{(3)}\big)\;+

\displaystyle+\,{\textstyle\sum_{t+\ell\,>0}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell)}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{(t!)}^{-1}{(\ell!)}^{-1}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\,\;=

\displaystyle=\;\,a\cdot b\,\;+

\displaystyle+\,{\textstyle\sum_{t+\ell\,>0}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell)}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{(t!)}^{-1}{(\ell!)}^{-1}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

where we took into account coassociativity and counitality properties.

Let us analyze each summand in the very last line in (
3.30
).
From the identities

\;\chi^{*\,k}(u\,,v\,)\,=\,\prod_{s=1}^{k}\chi\big(u_{(s)},v_{(s)}\big)\,=\,\ \prod_{s=1}^{k}\chi\big(u^{+}_{(s)},v^{+}_{(s)}\big)\;

— cf. (
3.29
) — we get

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a_{(i)},b_{(i)}\big)\,a_{(t+1)}\,b_{(t+1)}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\ell}}\,\chi\big(a_{(t+1+j)},b_{(t+1+j)}\big)\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\,a_{(t+1)}\,b_{(t+1)}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{j=1}^{\ell}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(t+1+j)},b^{+}_{(t+1+j)}\big)\quad

Consider the expansion of
a
as in (
3.28
).
Then, letting

\,j_{t+1}:U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(t+\ell)}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(t+\ell+1)}\,
be the map given by

\;\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{s=1}^{t+\ell}x_{s}\,\mapsto\,\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{s=1}^{t}x_{s}\Big)\otimes 1\otimes\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{s=t+1}^{t+\ell}x_{s}\Big)\;
,  we have

\hfil\displaystyle\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{i=1}^{t}\!a^{+}_{(i)}\Big)\otimes a^{+}_{(t+1)}\otimes\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{j=1}^{\ell}\!a^{+}_{(t+1+j)}\Big)\;=\;\delta_{t+\ell+1}(a)\,\;\in\;\,\hbar^{\,\max(t+\ell+1,A)\,-A}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes(t+\ell+1)}

\hfil\displaystyle\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{i=1}^{t}\!a^{+}_{(i)}\Big)\otimes\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\otimes\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{j=1}^{\ell}\!a^{+}_{(t+1+j)}\Big)\;=\;j_{t+1}\big(\delta_{t+\ell}(a)\big)\,\;\in\;\,\hbar^{\,\max(t+\ell,A)\,-A}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes(t+\ell+1)}

so that, summing up,

\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{i=1}^{t}\!a^{+}_{(i)}\!\Big)\otimes a_{\,t+1}\otimes\Big(\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{j=1}^{\ell}\!a^{+}_{(t+1+j)}\!\Big)=\delta_{t+\ell+1}(a)+j_{t+1}\big(\delta_{t+\ell}(a)\!\big)\in\hbar^{\,\max(t+\ell,A)\,-A}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes(t+\ell+1)}

— like in the proof of Lemma
3.3.1
—
and similarly with
b\,
, resp.
B\,
, replacing
a\,
, resp.
A\,
.
Eventually, for all
\,t+\ell>0\,
this gives

\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

(3.31)

\displaystyle=\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\,+\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

where for the two summands in second line, writing
\,n:=t+\ell\,
,
we have

\hfil\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,b_{(t+1)}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},b^{+}_{(k)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,\max(n,A)\,-A+\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes(n+1)}

\hfil\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\,a^{+}_{(t+1)}\,b_{(t+1)}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},b^{+}_{(k)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,\max(n+1,A)\,-A+\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes(n+1)}

Let us now assume that

\,A:=1\,
,  so that
\,n:=t+\ell>0\,

implies
\,n:=t+\ell\geq 1=A\,
. Then the last estimates read

\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,n-1+\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,n+\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}

The term in the second line, when plugged in (
3.31
)
and then in (
3.30
), yields a contribution of the form

{{\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,}\over{\,t!\,\ell!\,}}\;\hbar^{-n}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}

that belongs to
\,\hbar^{\,\max(n,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}\,
,
thus for growing
n
these elements sum up to a convergent series in

U_{\hbar}\,
,  and we are done.

As to the term in the first line, we split it into

\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=

(3.33)

\displaystyle=\;\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,\widehat{b}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\,+\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

Then for the first summand we have (almost by definition, or acting as before)

\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,\widehat{b}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\;\chi^{*\,(t+\ell\,)}(a\,,b\,)

so when we plug every such term in (
3.31
)
and then in (
3.30
), overall they sum up
to give the contribution

\hfil\displaystyle\quad{\textstyle\sum_{t+\ell\,>0}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell\,)}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{(t!)}^{-1}{(\ell!)}^{-1}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,\widehat{b}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,>\,0}}\;{\textstyle\sum_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell\,)}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{(t!)}^{-1}{(\ell!)}^{-1}\,\chi^{*\,(t+\ell)}(a\,,b\,)\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,>\,0}}\;{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}\,\bigg(\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\!\bigg)\;\chi^{*\,n}(a\,,b\,)\;=\;\,0\quad

just because of the combinatorial identity

\,\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell=n}}{(-1)}^{\ell}\,\displaystyle{n\choose\ell}\;=\;0\;\,
.

Finally, we have to dispose of the summands of type

\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

(3.34)

for which the analogue of the first identity in (
3.32
)
holds true, namely

\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{\,n-1+\,\max(n+1,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}

(3.35)

where
\,n:=t+\ell\,
, taking into account that

\,\delta_{n+1}(b)\,\in\,\hbar^{\,\max(n+1,B)\,-B}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}\;
.

Then we have to distinguish two cases, depending on
\,n:=t+\ell\,
.

First we assume
\;n:=t+\ell\geq B\;
. Then

\;n-1+\,\max(n+1,B)\,-B\,\geq\,n\;
,
hence the first identity in (
3.32
) yields

\,\;\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\,\in\,\hbar^{\,n}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}\;
,
and then, when plugged in (
3.33
),
and subsequently in (
3.31
) and in
(
3.30
), this provides to the expansion of

\;a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\;

a contribution of the form

\hbar^{-n}\,{{\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,}\over{\,t!\,\ell!\,}}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{-n}\,\hbar^{\,n}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}\;=\;U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(n+1)}

— which is fair! — hence we are done with it.

Then we are left with the case
\;n:=t+\ell\leq B-1\;
.
Tracking backwards our construction,
all these case provide to (
3.30
)
a contribution of the form

\displaystyle\textstyle{\displaystyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,\hbar^{-(t+\ell)}\,{{\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,}\over{\,t!\,\ell!\,}}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=

(3.36)

\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

With no loss of generality,
we can assume that

\;a\not\equiv 0\,
,
\,b\not\equiv 0\;\big(\,\text{mod}\ \hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\;
. Then for their corresponding cosets

\;\overline{a}\,,\overline{b}\in U_{\hbar}\Big/\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\cong{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;

we have
\,\overline{a}\in{{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{1}\,
and

\,\overline{b}\in{{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\!B}\,
,  where

\,{\big\{{{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{n}\big\}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\,

is the standard, coradical filtration of
{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  and also

\,\delta_{1}(\,\overline{a}\,)\not=0\,
as well as

\,\delta_{n}\big(\,\overline{b}\,\big)\not=0\,
for
\,1\leq n\leq B\,

— cf.
[
Ga1
]
, Lemma 3.3. Moreover, we recall that

\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

is cocommutative modulo

\,\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,
,  as it is a QUEA: in particular, this implies that

\,\delta_{n}\big(\,\overline{b}\,\big)\,

is a
symmetric
tensor

— for
\,1\leq n\leq B\,
— hence we can write
\delta_{n}(b\,)

in the form

\delta_{n}(b\,)\;=\;b^{+}_{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes b^{+}_{(n)}\;=\;\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\!\beta_{\langle n\rangle}\,+\,\mathcal{O}_{n}\big(\hbar^{1}\big)

(3.37)

(for
\,1\leq n\leq B\,
) where

\,\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\beta_{\langle n\rangle}\,

— using some
\sigma
–notation of sort, as usual
— is some
symmetric
tensor in
U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,n}
and

hereafter
\mathcal{O}_{n}\big(\hbar^{s}\big)
stands for some element in

\,\hbar^{s}\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,n}\,
,  for every
\,s,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
.
Then plugging (
3.37
) in (
3.34
)
we find

\hfil\displaystyle\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,b^{+}_{(t+1)}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{t+\ell+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},b^{+}_{(k)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{t+\ell+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}_{1}\big(\hbar^{\,t+\ell\,}\big)

for all
\,t+\ell\leq B-1\,
,  with

{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{t+\ell+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\,\;\in\;\,\hbar^{\,t+\ell-1}\,U_{\hbar}

Therefore, the contribution to (
3.30
) given in
(
3.36
) now reads

\hfil\displaystyle{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\,\chi^{*\,t}\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(2)}\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,\chi^{*\,\ell}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\,{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\,\;+

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;\,\mathcal{O}_{1}\big(\hbar^{0}\big)

where in the last formula we have

{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}\!{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\!{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\,\in\,\hbar^{-1}\,U_{\hbar}

Now, observe that, setting
\,n:=t+\ell\,
, we can re-write

{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\!\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\,=\,\varPhi\big(\delta_{n}(a)\otimes\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\beta_{\langle n+1\rangle}\big)

(3.38)

with
\;\varPhi:U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,2(n+1)}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\hbar}\;
being the map given by the composition

\varPhi\;:=\;\mu\circ\big(\,\chi^{\otimes\,t}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{U_{\hbar}}^{\,\otimes 2}\otimes\chi^{\otimes(n-t)}\big)\circ\varsigma_{\,n+1}

where

(1)

\;\varsigma_{\,n+1}:U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(2n+1)}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes\,(2n+1)}\;
is the “shuffle” map

x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\otimes y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{n}\otimes y_{n+1}\;\mapsto\;x_{1}\otimes y_{1}\otimes x_{2}\otimes y_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\otimes y_{n}\otimes y_{n+1}

and, considering
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
as embedded into
U_{\hbar}
via the unit map,

(2)

\;\mu:U_{\hbar}^{\otimes\,n}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\hbar}\;
is the obvious (
n
–fold iterated) multiplication by scalars.

Now recall that
\,\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\beta_{\langle n+1\rangle}\,
represents a tensor in
\sigma
–notation,
so more explicitly we might write

\,\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\beta_{\langle n+1\rangle}\,=\,\sum_{s=1}^{N}\beta_{s,1}\otimes\beta_{s,\,n+1}\,
;  so in the formula we are dealing with what is written as a product

{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)

is actually a sum of several products as

\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{s,\,i}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{s,\,t+1}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{s,\,k}\big)\;
.
But then recall that this tensor

\,\beta_{\langle 1\rangle}\otimes\cdots\otimes\beta_{\langle n+1\rangle}\,=\,\sum_{s=1}^{N}\beta_{s,1}\otimes\beta_{s,\,n+1}\,

is
symmetric
, therefore, the various products

\;{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{s,\,i}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{s,\,t+1}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{s,\,k}\big)\;

actually all
coincide
:
letting
C_{n}
be their “common value”, we deduce that

\hfil\displaystyle{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}\!{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\!{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{t}}\,\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},\beta_{\langle i\rangle}\big)\,\widehat{a}_{(t+1)}\,\beta_{\langle t+1\rangle}\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{k=t+2}^{n+1}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(k)},\beta_{\langle k\rangle}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\,=\,1}^{B-1}}\,{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\hbar^{-n}\bigg(\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell\,=\,n}}\!{(-1)}^{\ell}\,{n\choose\ell}\!\bigg)\,C_{n}\,\;=\;\,0

again because of the identity

\,\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{t+\ell=n}}{(-1)}^{\ell}\,\displaystyle{n\choose\ell}\;=\;0\;\,
.

Thus, also the last contributions to (
3.30
) given in
(
3.36
) actually belong to
U_{\hbar}\,
.

To sum up,
we have proved that

\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\;=\;a\cdot b\,+\,z\qquad\qquad\textsl{with \ }\;z\in J_{\hbar}

(3.39)

\displaystyle\forall\;\;\;a\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle 1$}}=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,,\;\;b\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}=\hbar^{-B}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}

and similarly — just switching roles of
a
and
b
— also

\displaystyle b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\;=\;b\cdot a\,+\,x\qquad\qquad\textsl{with \ }\;x\in J_{\hbar}

(3.40)

\displaystyle\forall\;\;\;a\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle 1$}}=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,,\;\;b\in\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}=\hbar^{-B}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime\!\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\;\scriptstyle B$}}

Let
\,{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\,

the unital
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
