–subalgebra of

\,{\big({\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\!\sigma}:={\big({\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\otimes_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}U_{\hbar}\big)}_{\!\sigma}\,

generated by
\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\,
.
Now recall that
\,\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}:=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
with

\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)\,
;  then

\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}=J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\oplus{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\cdot 1\,
,  which implies

\;\Delta\big(J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\subseteq\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\otimes 1+J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\otimes J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}+1\otimes J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\;
.
Then we get also

\;\Delta\big(\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big)\,\subseteq\,\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\otimes 1+\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\otimes\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}+1\otimes\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\;
.
Since the coalgebra structure is the same in
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and

{\big({\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\!\sigma}\,
, it follows eventually from this that

\,{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\,

is a Hopf
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–subalgebra inside

{\big({\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\!\sigma}\,
.

By repeated use of (
3.39
) or
(
3.40
) alike, we find that

\;{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\subseteq U_{\hbar}={\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;
.

Now observe that the original product “
\,\cdot\,
” in
\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

and
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
can be obtained from
“
\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}
’’
through deformation via the inverse
2
–cocycle
\sigma^{-1}\,
.
Thanks to this, we can reverse the roles of

\,U_{\hbar}={\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
and

\,{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\,

in the previous construction (with some care), thus eventually achieving the
converse inclusion

\;{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\subseteq{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\;
.
Therefore
\;{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\subseteq{\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\sigma}\;
,  which in particular implies
that
\;U_{\hbar}={\big\langle\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}\big\rangle}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;
is closed for the

\sigma
–product,  q.e.d.
∎

Definition 3.3.6
.

With assumptions as in Theorem
3.3.5
,
the new QUEA obtained from
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
through the process of 2–cocycle
deformation by
\sigma
of
{\mathbb{U}_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
followed by restriction will be called

the polar 2–cocycle deformation of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
by
\sigma
,
and it will be denoted by
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}\,
.

To complete our analysis, next result sheds light onto the new, polar 2–cocycle deformed QUEA
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}\,
, describing in detail its semiclassical limit:

Theorem 3.3.7
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA over the Lie bialgebra

\,{\mathfrak{g}}=\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}\,;\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
.
Let
\sigma
be a polar 2–cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, so

\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,
for some

\,\chi\in{\Big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)}^{\!*}\,
with

\,\chi(z\,,1)=0=\chi(1\,,z)\,
for
\,z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Set also

\;\chi_{a}:=\chi-\chi_{2,1}\;
.
Then:

(a)
\chi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\chi_{2,1}=-\chi\,
,
iff
\sigma

is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\sigma_{2,1}=\sigma^{-1}\,
;

(b)  the
\Bbbk
–linear map
\,\gamma\,:=\,\chi_{a}\;\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\big)}^{\!*}\,\Big)\Big|_{{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}}\;

from
\,{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\,
to
\Bbbk
is

antisymmetric 2–cocycle
for the Lie bialgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
;

(c)  the polar 2–cocycle deformation
\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}
of

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is a QUEA for the Lie bialgebra

\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{\gamma}=\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}\,;\,{[\,\ ,\ ]}_{\gamma}\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,

which is the deformation of
\,{\mathfrak{g}}
by the 2–cocycle
\gamma\,
;
in a nutshell, we have

\;{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}\cong\,U_{\hbar}\big({\mathfrak{g}}_{\gamma}\big)\;
.

In particular, if
\sigma
is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–valued
— i.e., it is an ordinary 2–cocycle for the Hopf
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
— or equivalently

\,\chi\in\hbar\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\big)}^{\!*}\,
,
then we have just
\,\gamma=0\,
and

\;{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}\cong\,U_{\hbar}\big({\mathfrak{g}}_{\gamma}\big)={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This follows from claim
(c)
in
Lemma
3.3.2
.

(b)
We are interested in the restriction to
\,{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\,

of the specialization of
\sigma
modulo
\hbar\,
. So we start with

\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\,,\text{c}\in{\mathfrak{g}}\,
,  that we realize as

\;\text{a}=a\;\big(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\;
,

\;\text{b}=b\;\big(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\;
and

\;\text{c}=c\;\big(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\;

for some “lifts”
\,a,b,c\in U_{\hbar}\,
. By the identity

\,U_{\hbar}={\big(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\vee}\,

and by Lemma 3.3 in
[
Ga1
]
,
we can choose the lifts

a
,
b
and
c
belong to

\,\widetilde{J}_{\hbar}:=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
,  so that

\,a^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,a\,
,
\,b^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,b\,
and

\,c^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,c\,
belong to
J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\,
.

As
\sigma
is a normalized Hopf 2–cocycle for
U_{\hbar}\,
,
it must obey the equality

\sigma\big(b^{\,\prime}_{(1)},c^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}_{(2)}c^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\big)\;=\;\sigma\big(a^{\,\prime}_{(1)},b^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a^{\,\prime}_{(2)}b^{\,\prime}_{(2)},c^{\,\prime}\,\big)

(3.41)

Let us focus on the left hand side of
(
3.41
). Expanding the exponential we get

\displaystyle\sigma\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\sigma\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\,\sum_{n,m\geq 0}\frac{\hbar^{-(n+m)}}{n!\,m!}\,\chi^{\ast\,n}\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi^{\ast\,m}\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\;=

\displaystyle\hskip 7.0pt=\;\epsilon\big(a^{\,\prime}\big)\,\epsilon\big(b^{\,\prime}\big)\,\epsilon\big(c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{-1}\,\chi\big(b^{\,\prime},c^{\,\prime}\big)\,\epsilon\big(a^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{-1}\,\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+

\displaystyle\quad+\,\hbar^{-2}\,\chi\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{-2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi^{\ast\,2}\big(b^{\,\prime},c^{\,\prime}\big)\,\epsilon\big(a^{\,\prime}\big)\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\,\hbar^{-2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi^{\ast\,2}\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\sum_{n+m\geq 3}\frac{\hbar^{-(n+m)}}{n!\,m!}\,\chi^{\ast\,n}\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi^{\ast\,m}\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)

Then, noting that
\,\epsilon\big(a^{\,\prime}\big)=\epsilon\big(b^{\,\prime}\big)=\epsilon\big(c^{\,\prime}\big)=0\,

by construction, and analyzing all other summands as in the proof of claim
Theorem
3.3.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\sigma\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\;\hbar^{-1}\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+

(3.42)

\displaystyle+\,\hbar^{-2}\chi\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{-2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi^{\ast\,2}\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+

\displaystyle+\,\big(\,\textsl{sum of all terms with\ }n+m\geq 3\,\big)

Writing
\,z^{\prime}=z^{\prime+}+\epsilon\big(z^{\prime}\big)\,
and using that

\,\chi(z,1)=0=\chi(1,z)\,
and

\chi\big(x_{(1)}^{\,\prime},y_{(1)}^{\,\prime}z_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(x_{(2)}^{\,\prime},y_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\,\epsilon\big(z_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\big)\,=\,\chi\big(x_{(1)}^{\,\prime},y_{(1)}^{\,\prime}z^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(x_{(2)}^{\,\prime},y_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)

we have that

\hfil\displaystyle\enskip\chi^{\ast\,2}\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\;\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime},b_{(1)}^{\,\prime}c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hskip 17.0pt=\;\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}c_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hskip 29.0pt+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},b^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},b^{\,\prime}c_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},c_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},b^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},c^{\,\prime}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(1)}^{\,\prime+}c^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime+}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)}^{\,\prime+},c^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)}^{\,\prime+},b^{\,\prime}\big)

Now, taking into account that
\,z^{\prime}=\hbar\,z\,
and

\,z_{(i)}^{\prime\ +}\in\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,
,
we may re-write the expression above as

\chi^{\ast\,2}\big(a^{\,\prime},b^{\,\prime}c^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\;\,\hbar^{4}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\big)\,+\,\hbar^{4}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},c\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},b\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{5}\big)

Performing a similar analysis on the term

\,\chi\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,

we get

\chi\big(b_{(1)}^{\,\prime},c_{(1)}^{\,\prime}\big)\,\chi\big(a^{\,\prime},b_{(2)}^{\,\prime}c_{(2)}^{\,\prime}\big)\;=\;\,\hbar^{4}\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{4}\,\chi\big(b_{(1)},c\big)\,\chi\big(a,b_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{5}\big)

Moreover, with a similar (yet easier) analysis one finds also that

\chi^{*n}\big(b^{\prime}_{(1)},c^{\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\chi^{*m}\big(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}_{(2)}c^{\prime}_{(2)}\big)\,\in\,\hbar^{+2(n+m)}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}

for all
\,n+m\geq 3\,
,  so that the (last) summand
“
\big(\,\textsl{sum of all terms with\ }n+m\geq 3\,\big)
”
in (
3.42
) is of type

\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{n+m}\big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)\,
.
Putting all together in (
3.42
) we find

\displaystyle\hbar^{3}\,\sigma\big(b_{(1)},c_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a,b_{(2)}c_{(2)}\big)\;=

\displaystyle=\,\hbar^{2}\,\chi(a,bc)+\hbar^{2}\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)+\hbar^{2}\,\chi\big(b_{(1)},c\big)\,\chi\big(a,b_{(2)}\big)\,+

\displaystyle+\,\hbar^{2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\big)\,+\,\hbar^{2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},c\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},b\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)

An analogous treatment of the right hand side of
(
3.41
) yields

\displaystyle\hbar^{3}\,\sigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a_{(2)}b_{(2)},c\,\big)\;=

\displaystyle=\,\hbar^{2}\,\chi(ab,c\,)+\hbar^{2}\,\chi\big(a,b_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b_{(2)},c\,\big)+\hbar^{2}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\,\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\,\big)\,+

\displaystyle+\,\hbar^{2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\hbar^{2}\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}\big(\hbar^{3}\big)

Altogether, this implies that

\displaystyle\chi(a,bc\,)\,+\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(b_{(1)},c\,\big)\,\chi\big(a,b_{(2)}\big)\,+

\displaystyle+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\,\big)\,+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},c\,\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},b\,\big)\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}

\displaystyle\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\chi(ab,c\,)\,+\,\chi\big(a,b_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b_{(2)},c\,\big)\,+\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\,\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\,\big)\,+

\displaystyle+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)

where (again)
\,\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\,
stands for “congruent modulo

\,\hbar\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
”,
that we re-write as

\displaystyle\chi(a,bc\,)\,+\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\chi\big(b_{(1)},c\,\big)\,\chi\big(a,b_{(2)}\big)\,+

(3.43)

\displaystyle+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\,\big)\,+\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},c\,\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},b\,\big)\;-

\displaystyle-\;\chi(ab,c\,)\,-\,\chi\big(a,b_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b_{(2)},c\,\big)\,-\,\chi\big(a_{(1)},b\,\big)\,\chi\big(a_{(2)},c\,\big)\,-

\displaystyle-\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(a,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b,c_{(2)}\big)\,-\,2^{-1}\,\chi\big(b,c_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a,c_{(2)}\big)\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;0

Consider now the action of the group algebra

\,\Bbbk\big[\mathbb{S}_{3}\big]\,
of the symmetric group

\mathbb{S}_{3}\,
on
\big(U_{\hbar}^{\widehat{\otimes}3}\big)^{*}\,

given by

\,\sigma\,.\,\varphi(a,b,c):=\varphi\big(\sigma^{-1}\,.\,(a,b,c)\big)\,
,
where the action of
\,\Bbbk\big[\mathbb{S}_{3}\big]\,
on

\,U_{\hbar}^{\widehat{\otimes}3}\,
is the natural one that permutes
the tensor factors. Then we let the antisymmetrizer
\textsl{Alt}_{3}

act on both sides of (
3.43
): using that

\;\gamma\,:=\,\chi_{a}\;\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\big)}^{\!*}\,\Big)\Big|_{{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}}\;

and that

\;a\;\big(\hskip-7.0pt\mod\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\big)=\text{a}\;
,

\;b\;\big(\hskip-7.0pt\mod\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\big)=\text{b}\;

and

\;c\;\big(\hskip-7.0pt\mod\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\big)=\text{c}\;
,
a straightforward calculation eventually yields

\partial_{*}(\gamma)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\,+\,{[[\gamma\,,\gamma\,]]}_{*}\,\;=\;\,0

This means exactly that
\,\gamma\,
is a 2-cocycle for the Lie bialgebra

{\mathfrak{g}}
— according to Definition
2.1.5
—
that is obviously antisymmetric (by construction), q.e.d.

(c)
First of all, we start by noting that

\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}:={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}\,
is equal to
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

as a counital
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–coalgebra (by construction), but with the new product defined by

m_{\sigma}(a,b\,)\,=\,a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b\,=\,\sigma(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\sigma^{-1}(a_{(3)},b_{(3)})

[formula]

In particular, the
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

is still topologically free, so that
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}
is again a Hopf algebra in

\mathcal{T}_{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\,
, cf. §
2.3.1
.
Moreover, its semiclassical limit

\,\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}\Big/\hbar\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}\,

as a coalgebra is the same as that of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
;
hence it is again cocommutative connected (as these properties are not affected
by
2
–cocycle deformations). Thus by Milnor-Moore Theorem we have

\,\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}=U\big(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\,
, where

\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}=\operatorname{Prim}\big(\,\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}\,\big)\,

is the space of primitive elements in
\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}\,
,
and as such it coincides with

\,\operatorname{Prim}\big(\,\overline{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}\,\big)=\operatorname{Prim}\big(U({\mathfrak{g}})\big)={\mathfrak{g}}\,

as a Lie coalgebra; its Lie algebra structure, on the other hand, does depend on

\sigma\,
. Altogether, this shows that
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}
is indeed a QUEA,
whose semiclassical limit is
U\big(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\,
;
then we are only left to prove that the Lie bracket on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}

coincides with that of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\gamma}\,
,  while also proving that
\gamma

is an antisymmetric 2–cocycle for the Lie bialgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
.

The Lie bracket in
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is given by the commutator inside

\,U\big(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)=\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}\,
,
so we denote it by

\,\;{[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\sigma}=\text{a}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{b}-\text{b}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{a}\;\,
(for all
\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\in{\mathfrak{g}}\,
), where

\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;

is the product in
\,U\big(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)=\overline{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}}\,

induced by the (
\sigma
–deformed) product in
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}_{\sigma}\,
.
Therefore, we will compute such a commutator as the coset modulo

\,\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,
of a commutator in
U_{\hbar}\,
,  namely

\,\;{[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\sigma}=\text{a}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{b}-\text{b}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{a}\;=\;a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b-b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\;\;\big(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\;
,
where
a
and
b\,
, like in the proof of claim
(c)
,
are lifts of
a
and
b
—
i.e.,
\,a\;\big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)=\text{\rm a}\,

and
\,b\;\big(\,\text{\rm mod}\,\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)=\text{\rm b}\,

— such that
\;a^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,a\,\in J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\;
and

\;b^{\,\prime}:=\hbar\,b\,\in J_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}\;
.

We re-start back from (
3.30
), which now gives
(taking into account all the analysis carried out there, with
\,A=1=B\,
)

\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b-b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\;\;\underset{\hbar}{\equiv}\;\,a\cdot b\;-\;b\cdot a\,\;+

\displaystyle\hskip 3.0pt+\,\hbar^{-3}\,\Big(\,\chi\big(a^{\prime+}_{(1)},b^{\prime+}_{(1)}\big)\big(\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\,b^{\prime+}_{(2)}+a^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\big)\,-\,\chi\big(b^{\prime+}_{(1)},a^{\prime+}_{(1)}\big)\big(\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\,a^{\prime+}_{(2)}+b^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\big)\!\Big)\;-

\displaystyle\hskip 6.0pt-\,\hbar^{-3}\,\Big(\!\big(\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\,b^{\prime+}_{(1)}+a^{\prime+}_{(1)}\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(a^{\prime+}_{(2)},b^{\prime+}_{(2)}\big)\,-\,\big(\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\,a^{\prime+}_{(1)}+b^{\prime+}_{(1)}\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\chi\big(b^{\prime+}_{(2)},a^{\prime+}_{(2)}\big)\!\Big)

Second, letting

\;\chi_{a}:=\chi-\chi_{\,2,1}\;
,
the previous formula greatly simplifies into

\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b-b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\;\;=\;\,a\cdot b\,-\,b\cdot a\;+\;\hbar^{-3}\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{\prime+}_{(1)},b^{\prime+}_{(1)}\big)\big(\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\,b^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,+\,a^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(2)}\big)\,\;+

\displaystyle\hskip 113.0pt+\;\,\hbar^{-3}\,\big(\,\widehat{b}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\,a^{\prime+}_{(1)}+b^{\prime+}_{(1)}\,\widehat{a}^{\,\prime}_{(1)}\big)\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{\prime+}_{(2)},a^{\prime+}_{(2)}\big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}(\hbar)\,\;=

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt=\;\,a\cdot b\,-\,b\cdot a\;+\;\hbar^{-3}\,\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{\prime+}_{(1)},b^{\prime}\big)\,a^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{\prime+}_{(2)},b^{\prime}\big)\,a^{\prime+}_{(1)}\,\Big)\,\;-

\displaystyle\hskip 126.0pt-\,\hbar^{-3}\,\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{\prime+}_{(1)},a^{\prime}\big)\,b^{\prime+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{\prime+}_{(2)},a^{\prime}\big)\,b^{\prime+}_{(1)}\Big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}_{1\!}(\hbar)

Now, let us write
\;z^{\prime}\,=\,\hbar\,z\;
for all

\;z\,\in\,\{a\,,b\,\}\;
:  then the last formula turns into

\displaystyle a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b-b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a\;\;=\;\,a\cdot b

\displaystyle-\;b\cdot a\,\;+\;\hbar^{-1}\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{+}_{(1)},b\,\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{+}_{(2)},b\,\big)\,a^{+}_{(1)}\Big)\,\;-

(3.44)

\displaystyle-\;\hbar^{-1}\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{+}_{(1)},a\,\big)\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{+}_{(2)},a\,\big)\,b^{+}_{(1)}\Big)\;+\;\mathcal{O}_{1\!}(\hbar)

Here we recall that, working with a QUEA, for
\,c\in\{a\,,b\,\}\,

we have

\Delta(c\,)\,\;=\;\,c\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes c\,+\,c^{+}_{(1)}\otimes c^{+}_{(2)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}_{2\!}\big(\hbar^{2}\big)\;\;,\qquad c^{+}_{(1)}\otimes c^{+}_{(2)}\,\in\,\hbar\,U_{h}^{\,\widehat{\otimes}2}

and moreover — for every
\,c\in\{a\,,b\,\}\,
and

\,\text{c}\in\{\text{a}\,,\text{b}\}\,
,
so that
c
is a lift of
c
—

\overline{\hbar^{-1}\big(c^{+}_{(1)}\!\otimes c^{+}_{(2)}-c^{+}_{(2)}\!\otimes c^{+}_{(1)}\big)}\;=\;\delta(\text{c})\;=:\;\text{c}_{[1]}\otimes\text{c}_{[2]}

(3.45)

where hereafter any “overlined” object stands for
“its coset modulo
\hbar\,
”;
in addition, we recall also that
\chi_{a}
is antisymmetric.
Then (
3.44
)
and (
3.45
) altogether yield

\displaystyle{[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\sigma}=\text{a}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{b}-\text{b}\;\overline{\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\,\sigma}$}}\;\text{a}\;=\;\overline{a\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,b-b\,\raisebox{-5.0pt}{$\dot{\scriptstyle\sigma}$}\,a}\,\;=

\displaystyle\hskip 35.0pt=\,\overline{a}\cdot\overline{b}-\overline{b}\cdot\overline{a}\,\;+\;\overline{\hbar^{-1}\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{+}_{(1)},b\,\big)\,a^{+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(a^{+}_{(2)},b\,\big)\,a^{+}_{(1)}\Big)}\,\;-

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt-\;\overline{\hbar^{-1}\Big(\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{+}_{(1)},a\,\big)\,b^{+}_{(2)}\,-\,\chi_{a}\big(b^{+}_{(2)},a\,\big)\,b^{+}_{(1)}\Big)}\,\;=

\displaystyle\hskip 105.0pt=\;\,[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]\,+\,\gamma\big(\text{a}_{[1]},\text{b}\big)\,\text{a}_{[2]}\,-\,\gamma\big(\text{b}_{[1]},\text{a}\big)\,\text{b}_{[2]}\,\;=

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt=\;\,[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]\,-\,\gamma\big(\text{a}_{[2]},\text{b}\big)\,\text{a}_{[1]}\,-\,\gamma\big(\text{b}_{[1]},\text{a}\big)\,\text{b}_{[2]}\,\;=:\;\,{[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\gamma}

hence
\;{[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\sigma}={[\text{a}\,,\text{b}]}_{\gamma}\;

for all
\,\text{a}\,,\text{b}\in{\mathfrak{g}}\,
,  in the sense of (
2.5
),
and we are done.

Finally, if in particular
\sigma
is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–valued
— i.e., it is an ordinary 2–cocycle for the Hopf
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
—
then we have

\;\chi\,=\,\hbar\,\log_{*}(\sigma)\,\in\,\hbar\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\big)}^{\!*}\,
,  hence we have just

\,\gamma=0\,
and

\;{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}_{\sigma}\cong\,U_{\hbar}\big({\mathfrak{g}}_{\gamma}\big)={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;
.
∎

Example 3.3.8
.

Some concrete examples of polar 2–cocycles and deformation by them are treated
in full depth in
[
GaGa2
, Section 5.2]
: they concern the wide family of

formal multiparameter QUEAs
that we already treated in
Example
3.1.4
.
We then resume notations and formulas from there.

Fix
\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
and
\,I:=\{1,\ldots,n\}\,
.
We choose a multiparameter matrix

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
of Cartan type,
with associated Cartan matrix
A\,
, a realization

\;\mathcal{R}\,:=\,\big(\,{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;
of it and the
(topological) Hopf algebra
U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\,
.
Let
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
be a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis in
{\mathfrak{h}}
,
where
\mathcal{G}
is an index set with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}({\mathfrak{h}})=t\,
.

We consider special polar 2–cocycles of
\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\,
, called “toral” as they are induced from the quantum torus. Fix an antisymmetric,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map
\,\chi:{\mathfrak{h}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,  that corresponds to
\,X={\big(\,\chi_{g{}\gamma}=\chi(H_{g}\,,H_{\gamma})\big)}_{g,\gamma\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
. Any such map
\chi
also induces uniquely an antisymmetric,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,:\,U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\times U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}

as follows. By definition,
U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})
is an
\hbar
–adically
complete topologically free Hopf algebra isomorphic to

\,\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}({\mathfrak{h}}):=\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,n}({\mathfrak{h}})}\,
,  the

\hbar
–adic completion of the symmetric algebra

\,S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}({\mathfrak{h}})=\bigoplus\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,n}({\mathfrak{h}})\,
. Then,

\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
is defined as the unique
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear
(hence
\hbar
–adically continuous) map

\;U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\otimes U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}}}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(z,1):=\epsilon(z)=:\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(1,z)\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;z\in\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}({\mathfrak{h}})

(3.46)

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=\chi(x,y)\qquad\hskip 55.0pt\qquad\forall\;\;x,y\in S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,1}({\mathfrak{h}})

\displaystyle\hfill\qquad\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=0\quad\qquad\forall\;\;x\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,r}({\mathfrak{h}})\,,\;y\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,s}({\mathfrak{h}})\,:\,r,s\geq 1\,,\;r+s>2\enskip

By construction,
\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
is a normalized Hochschild 2–cocycle on
U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\,
,  that is

\epsilon(x)\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(y,z)\,-\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(xy,z)\,+\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,yz)\,-\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y)\,\epsilon(z)\;=\;0\qquad\forall\;\;x,y,z\in U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})

By
[
GaGa2
, Lemma 5.2.3]
, the convolution powers of
\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
satisfy the following property: for all
\,H_{+},H_{-}\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
and
\,k,\ell,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
,  we have

{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}^{\,\ast\,m}\big(H_{+}^{\,k},H_{-}^{\,\ell}\,\big)\;=\;\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{lr}\delta_{k,m}\,\delta_{\ell,m}\,{\big(m!\big)}^{2}\,\chi(H_{+},H_{-})^{m}&\quad\text{for}\;\;\;\;m\geq 1\;,\\
\delta_{k,0}\,\delta_{\ell,0}&\quad\text{for}\;\;\;\;m=0\;.\end{array}\end{cases}

This allows one to define a polar 2–cocycle
\,\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
as the unique
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear
map from
\,U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\!\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\!U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\,
to
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
given by the exponentiation of

\,\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
, i.e.

\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\;:=\;e^{\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}\;=\;{\textstyle\sum_{m\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-m}\,{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}^{\,\ast\,m}\!\Big/2^{m}\,m!

By
[
GaGa2
, Lemma 5.2.2]
, this
\,\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
is in fact a well-defined
polar 2–cocycle
for
\,U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{h}})\,
, in the sense of Definition
3.3.3
. Moreover, one has, for all
H_{+}\,,H_{-}\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
, and setting
\,K_{\pm}:=e^{\,\hbar\,H_{\pm}}\,
,

\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}^{\pm 1}(H_{+},H_{-})\,=\,\pm\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\chi(H_{+},H_{-})\quad,\qquad\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(K_{+},K_{-})\,=\,e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}\chi(H_{+},H_{-})}

Assume now that
\,\chi
satisfies the additional requirement

\;\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\,=\,\chi(\,-\,,S_{i})\;
for all
\,i\in I\,
,
where
\,S_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)\,
for all
\,i\in I\,
.
In particular, one has that

\;\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+}\,,\,T\,\big)\,=\,\chi\big(-T_{i}^{-}\,,\,T\,\big)\;
and

\;\chi\big(\,T\,,T_{i}^{+}\big)\,=\,\chi\big(\,T\,,-T_{i}^{-}\big)\;

for all
\,i\in I\,
and
\,T\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
. Then
\chi
canonically induces a

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\;\overline{\chi}:\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;
,
where

\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}:={\mathfrak{h}}\big/\mathfrak{s}\,
with

\,\mathfrak{s}:=\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\{\,S_{i}\,\}}_{i\in I}\big)\,
,  given by

\overline{\chi}\,\big(\,T^{\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,,T^{\prime\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,\big)\,:=\,\chi\big(\,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\big)\qquad\forall\;\;T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathfrak{h}}

Now, replaying the construction above with
\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}
and

\overline{\chi}
replacing
{\mathfrak{h}}
and
\chi\,
,
we can construct a normalized Hopf polar
2
–cocycle

\,\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\!:U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\times U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\,
.
Moreover, since

\,U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\cong\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\,
,
there exists a unique Hopf algebra epimorphism

\;\pi:U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\;
given by

\,\pi(E_{i}):=0\,
,
\,\pi(F_{i}):=0\,
— for
\,i\in I\,
— and

\,\pi(T):=(T+\mathfrak{s})\,\in\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\subseteq U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\,

— for
\,T\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
. Then we consider

\sigma_{\chi}\,:=\,\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\,:\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\times U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}

which is
automatically
a normalized,
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–valued
Hopf polar
2
–cocycle on
U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\,
.

By Theorem
3.3.7
, one may define a
“deformed product” on
U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})
using
\sigma_{\chi}
hereafter denoted by

\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}\,
. Write

\,X^{(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=X\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\cdots\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}X\,
for the
n
–th
power of any
\,X\in U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\,
with respect to this deformed product.

Directly from definitions, sheer computation yields the following formulas,
relating the deformed product with the old one (for all
\;T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime},T\in{\mathfrak{h}}\;
,

\;i\,,\,j\in I\,
):

\hfil\displaystyle T^{\prime}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T^{\prime\prime}\,=\;T^{\prime}\,T^{\prime\prime}\quad,\qquad E_{i}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\;=\,E_{i}\,F_{j}\quad,\qquad F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}\;=\,F_{j}\,E_{i}

\hfil\displaystyle T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\;=\;T\,E_{j}\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T,T_{j}^{+}\big)\,E_{j}\quad,\quad E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\;=\;E_{j}\,T\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T_{j}^{+},T\big)\big)\,E_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\;=\;T\,F_{j}\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T,T_{j}^{-}\big)\,F_{j}\quad,\quad F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\;=\;F_{j}\,T\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T_{j}^{-},T\big)\,F_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\;=\;E_{i}^{\,m}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,m}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,n\,T_{j}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,n\,2^{-1}\mathring{\chi}_{ij}}E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,E_{j}\,\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,E_{k}^{\,(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=\,\Big({\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}\Big)\!\Big)\Big({\textstyle\prod_{t=1}^{n-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,t\,T_{k}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{k}^{+}}\Big)\!\Big)\,\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\cdot\;\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{j}^{+}}\Big)\,\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,(m\,T_{i}^{+}+T_{j}^{+})}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,n\,T_{k}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}\,E_{k}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\;=\;F_{i}^{\,m}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,m}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,n\,T_{j}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,n\,2^{-1}\mathring{\chi}_{ij}}F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,F_{j}\,\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,F_{k}^{\,(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=\Big({\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\Big)\!\Big)\Big({\textstyle\prod_{t=1}^{n-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,t\,T_{k}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{k}^{-}}\Big)\!\Big)\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\cdot\;\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{j}^{-}}\Big)\,\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,(m\,T_{i}^{-}+T_{j}^{-})}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,n\,T_{k}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}\,F_{k}^{\,n}

Fix now
\,\mathring{X}:=\!{\Big(\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}=\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+},T_{j}^{+}\big)\!\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
and define the multiparameter matrix

P_{(\chi)}:=\,P\,+\,\mathring{X}\,=\,{\Big(\,p^{(\chi)}_{i{}j}:=\,p_{ij}+\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}\Big)}_{\!i,j\in I}\;\,,\!\quad\Pi_{(\chi)}:=\,{\Big\{\,\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}:=\,\alpha_{i}\pm\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{\pm}\big)\Big\}}_{i\in I}

It turns out that
P_{(\chi)}
is a matrix of Cartan type — the same of
P
indeed —
and
\,\mathcal{R}_{(\chi)}\,=\,\big(\,{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

is a realization of it. Moreover, by
[
GaGa2
, Theorem 5.2.12]
,
there exists an isomorphism of topological Hopf algebras

{\big(U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\;\cong\;\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}_{(\chi)}}({\mathfrak{g}})

which is the identity on generators.
In short, every toral polar 2–cocycle deformation of a FoMpQUEA is another FoMpQUEA,
whose multiparameter
\,P_{(\chi)}
and realization
\mathcal{R}_{(\chi)}

depend on the original
P
and
\mathcal{R}\,
, as well as on
\chi\,
.
Moreover, under some mild restrictions on the realizations, one proves that the FoMpQUEA
U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})
is isomorphic to a toral polar 2–cocycle deformation of the Drinfeld’s standard double QUEA, see
[
GaGa2
, Theorem 5.2.14]
.

About the semiclassical limit, we have the following. Taking everything modulo
\hbar\,
,
the map
\,\chi:{\mathfrak{h}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,

defines a similar antisymmetric,
\Bbbk
–bilinear map

\;\gamma:=\big(\,\chi\!\mod\hbar\,\big):{\mathfrak{h}}_{0}\times{\mathfrak{h}}_{0}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk\;
— where

\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{0}:={\mathfrak{h}}\Big/\hbar\,{\mathfrak{h}}\,=\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,
.
Out of
\gamma
one constructs a toral
2
–cocycle
\gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}

for the Lie bialgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\,
,  and out of it the

2
–cocycle deformed Lie bialgebra

\,{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}\,
.
Similarly as above, out of
\gamma
we get the multiparameter matrix

P_{(\gamma)}
and its realization
\,\mathcal{R}_{(\gamma)}\,
:
then by construction
\,P_{(\gamma)}=\bar{P}_{(\chi)}\,
and

\,\mathcal{R}_{(\gamma)}=\bar{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\,
. Attached to these we have

\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,\mathcal{R}_{(\chi)}}({\mathfrak{g}})\,
and

\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}_{(\gamma)}}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{(\chi)}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}_{(\chi)}}\,
,
again connected via quantization/specialization, and

\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}_{(\gamma)}}\cong\,{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}\,

as Lie bialgebras. Actually, one has that

“deformation by (polar) 2–cocycle commutes with specialization”
,
see
[
GaGa2
, Theorem 6.2.4]
: with assumptions as above, we have that

{\big(\,U^{\,\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})\big)}_{\!\sigma_{\chi}}
is a quantized universal enveloping algebra,
with semiclassical limit

\,U\Big(\!{{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}}\Big)\,\cong\,U\Big({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R}}_{(\gamma)}}\Big)\,
.

Remark 3.3.9
.

It is important to stress that
our notion of
polar 2–cocycle
did not
come out of the blue
, but rather was suggested by the previous example.
Indeed, the authors first “met” these objects when studying polynomial-type QUEAs

U_{q}({\mathfrak{g}})
— i.e., QUEAs “à la Jimbo-Lusztig”, defined over

\Bbbk\big[q\,,q^{-1}\big]\,
: these are standard Hopf algebras (no topology is involved),
to which one can apply deformation by 2–cocycles and then obtain some
“multiparameter QUEAs” — cf.
[
GaGa1
]
, §4.2. Every such
polynomial

U_{q}({\mathfrak{g}})
can be realized as a Hopf subalgebra of a
formal

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
hence it makes sense to try and extend the 2–cocycle and the associated deformation
procedure used for
U_{q}({\mathfrak{g}})
to the larger Hopf algebra
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
When we fulfilled this task — in
[
GaGa2
]
—
what we actually found was that the unique extension of the 2–cocycle of
U_{q}({\mathfrak{g}})
to

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
actually is a
polar
2–cocycle (and not a 2–cocycle any more),
yet despite this the deformation procedure does extend from
U_{q}({\mathfrak{g}})
to the whole

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Thus the very notion of “polar 2–cocycle” and the associated deformation
procedure showed up as something real from this concrete example.

3.4.
Deformations by polar twist of QFSHA’s

In this subsection we consider deformations by twist of QFSHA’s,
but again “stretching the standard recipe”,
much like in §
3.3
:
in fact, rather than twists in the usual sense we
consider some special twist elements belonging to the scalar extension from

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
to
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
of our QFSHA.
For these elements — that we call “polar twists” — nothing ensures

a priori
that the deformation recipe would properly work on the given
QFSHA — as it is defined on
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
;  nevertheless, we eventually
find that this is indeed the case. In other words, we prove
— with a parallel result to Theorem
3.3.5

— that
the standard procedure of deformation by twist for QFSHA’s
can be extended (beyond its natural borders) to the case of polar twist elements
.

We begin with a couple of technical lemmas:

Lemma 3.4.1
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}

the associated QUEA defined in §
2.4
.
Let
\,\varphi\in J_{\hbar}^{\,2}\,
,  with

\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
. Then:

(a)
F:=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)\,

is a well-defined element in
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\;
;

(b)
\,\operatorname{Ad}(F)\big(f\big):=F\cdot f\cdot F^{-1}\,\in\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,

for all
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, so that the adjoint action of
\,F
onto

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
actually restricts to
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
The assumption
\,\varphi\in J_{\hbar}^{\;2}\,

implies
\;\varphi\,\in\,\hbar^{2}\,{\big(J_{\hbar}^{\,\vee}\big)}^{2}\subseteq\,\hbar^{2}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,  where

\;J_{\hbar}^{\,\vee}:=\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}\,\subseteq\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
.
Therefore
\;\hbar^{-1}\varphi\,\in\,\hbar\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,  hence

\,F:=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)\,
is indeed a well-defined element in

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,  q.e.d.

(b)
We compute
\,\operatorname{Ad}(F)\big(f\big)\,
,
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
:
using the identity
\;\operatorname{Ad}\!\big(\!\exp(X)\big)(Y)\,=\,\exp\!\big(\!\operatorname{ad}(X)\big)(Y)\;
and expanding the exponential into a
power series we get

\operatorname{Ad}(F)\big(f\big)\;=\;\operatorname{Ad}\!\big(\!\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)\big)(f)\;=\;\exp\!\big(\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)\big)(f)\;=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,n!\,}\;{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)}^{n}(f)

Now Lemma
2.3.5
(c)
and the assumption

\,\varphi\in J_{\!F_{\hbar}}^{\;2}\,
together guarantee that

{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)}^{n}(f)\,=\,{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)}^{n}\big(f_{+}\big)\,\in\,(1-\delta_{s,0})\,J_{\!F_{\hbar}}^{\;n+s}

[formula]

with
\,s\in\mathbb{N}\,
such that
\,f\in J_{\!F_{\hbar}}^{\;s}\,
,  hence

\;\operatorname{Ad}(F)\big(f\big)={\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,n!\,}\,{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varphi\big)}^{n}(f)\;
is indeed a well-defined element
— a
convergent
series! — of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
∎

Lemma 3.4.2
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
the associated
QUEA defined in §
2.4
. Let
\,\phi\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,

be such that
\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\epsilon)(\phi)=0=(\epsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})(\phi)\,
. Then:

(a)
\mathcal{F}=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
is a well-defined element in

\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\;
;

(b)
\,\mathcal{F}\cdot(x\otimes y)\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,\in\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\;

for all
\,x,y\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, so that the adjoint action of
\,\mathcal{F}
onto

\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}
actually restricts to

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\;
;

(c)
(\operatorname{id}\otimes\epsilon)(\mathcal{F}\,)=1=(\epsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})(\mathcal{F}\,)\;
;

(d)
\mathcal{F}
is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\mathcal{F}_{2,1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,
,  iff
\phi

is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\phi_{\,2,1}=-\phi\,
;

Proof.

(a)–(b)
The assumption

\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\epsilon)(\phi)=0=(\epsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})(\phi)\,
means that

\,\phi\in J_{\hbar}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
where

\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
.
Now note that

\,F_{\hbar}[[G\times G\hskip 1.5pt]]:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,

is in turn a QFSHA, for the group
\,G\times G\,
;
furthermore, its augmentation ideal is

\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{F_{\hbar}[[G\times G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)\;=\;\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\otimes{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,+\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\otimes\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)

so that
\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\subseteq{\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{F_{\hbar}[[G\times G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{2}\,
,
and finally, we have also

{F_{\hbar}[[G\times G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\;=\;\,{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{\vee}\;=\;\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}

Therefore, applying Lemma
3.4.1
above to the QFSHA

F_{\hbar}[[G\times G\hskip 1.5pt]]
and to
\,\varphi:=\phi\,

we get both claims
(a)
and
(b)
.

(c)–(d)
Both these claims follow at once from definitions,
along with the assumption that

\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\epsilon)(\phi)=0=(\epsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})(\phi)\,
.
∎

The previous result leads us to introduce the notion of “polar twist”, as follows:

Definition 3.4.3
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
as in

2.4.1
(b)
. We call
polar twist (element) of

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

any element in

\,\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,\big)^{\!\vee}={\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,
of the form

\,\mathcal{F}:=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
— for some

\,\phi\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
such that

\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\epsilon)(\phi)=0=(\epsilon\otimes\operatorname{id})(\phi)\,
—
which have the property of a twist element for the QUEA
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
.

Of course, every twist for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is a polar twist too; the converse, instead,
in general, is false (counterexamples do exist).
However,
every polar twist still provides a well-defined deformation by twist of

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
— in other words, the construction of
deformations by twist

properly extends to
“deformations by
polar
twist”
as well:

Theorem 3.4.4
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,

a polar twist for it, as in Definition
3.4.3
.
Then the procedure of twist deformation by
\mathcal{F}
applied to the QUEA

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
restricts to
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, making the latter into a new QFSHA.

Proof.

When deforming
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
by the twist
\mathcal{F}
one introduces on
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
the new coproduct
\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}
given by

\,\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}:=\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\circ\Delta\,
.
Then Lemma
3.4.2
(b)

ensures that
\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}
restricts to
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
in that it maps the latter into
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
.
The antipode can be dealt with similarly,
whence we conclude that the (deformed) Hopf structure of

{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee\,}\big)}^{\mathcal{F}}
does restrict to
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  q.e.d.
∎

Definition 3.4.5
.

With assumptions as in Theorem
3.4.4
,
the new QFSHA obtained from
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
through the process of twist deformation
(by
\mathcal{F}\,
) of
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
followed by restriction will be called

the polar twist deformation of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
by
\mathcal{F}
, and denoted by

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\mathcal{F}}\,
.

Finally, the next result describes in detail what exactly is the nature of the new,
polar twist deformed QFSHA
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\mathcal{F}}
, shedding light onto its semiclassical limit:

Theorem 3.4.6
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA over the Lie bialgebra

\,{\mathfrak{g}}=\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}\,;\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
. Set
\,{\mathfrak{m}}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
,
so
\;{\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\cong\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,

and
\;\big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\big)\Big/\big({\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)\,\cong\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,

as Lie bialgebras. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a polar twist for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, of the form
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
for some
\,\phi\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
,   and set also
\;\phi_{a}:=\phi-\phi_{\,2,1}\;
. Then:

(a)
\phi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\phi_{\,2,1}=-\phi\,
,  iff
\mathcal{F}
is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\mathcal{F}_{2,1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,
;

(b)  the element
\,c\,:=\,\bigg(\,\phi_{a}\,\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2\,}\Big)\!\!\mod\big({\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)\bigg)\;
in
\;\big({\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\big)\Big/\big({\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}+{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)\,\cong\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,
is an
antisymmetric twist
for
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,
;

(c)  the polar twist deformation
\,{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{\mathcal{F}}
of
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is a QFSHA for the Poisson group
G^{\,c}
whose cotangent Lie bialgebra is

\,\;{\textsl{Lie}\,\big(G^{\,c}\big)}^{*}=\,{\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{c}\,=\,\big(\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,;\,{[\,\ ,\ ]}_{*}\,,\,\delta_{*}^{\,c}\,\big)\;\,

that is the deformation of
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
by the twist
c\,
; in short,
\;{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{\mathcal{F}}\cong\,F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{\,c}\big]\big]\;
.

In particular, if
\mathcal{F}
is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–valued — i.e., it is an ordinary twist for the Hopf
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
— or equivalently
\,\phi\in\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
,  then we have just
\,c=0\,
and
\;{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{c}\cong\,F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{\,c}\big]\big]={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This is a special case of Lemma
3.4.2
(d)
.

(b)
We start from the twist identity

\,\;\mathcal{F}_{1{}2}\,\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\;=\,\mathcal{F}_{2{}3}\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\;\,

that we re-write in the equivalent form

\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\,{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)}^{-1}\;=\;\,\mathcal{F}_{1{}2}^{\,-1}\,\mathcal{F}_{2{}3}

(3.47)

Replacing
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
, we find

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\big)\cdot{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)}^{-1}\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\big)\cdot{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)\big(\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\big)}^{-1}\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\exp\big(\,\hbar^{-1}\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\big)\cdot\exp\big(\!-\hbar^{-1}\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\big)\quad

Now we recall the
Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff’s formula
,
that is the formal identity

\exp(X)\cdot\exp(Y)\;=\;\exp\big(\mathcal{B\,C\,H}(X,Y\,)\big)

(3.48)

which allows to express the product of two exponential as a single exponential: in it,

\,\mathcal{B\,C\,H}(X,Y\,)\,
is an explicit formal series given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B\,C\,H}(X,Y\,)\,\;:=\;\,\log\big(\exp(X)\,\exp(Y\,)\big)\,\;=

(3.49)

\displaystyle\,\;=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\;{(-1)}^{n-1}\;}{\;n\;}\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{i}+s_{i}>0\\
1\leq i\leq n\end{subarray}}}\frac{\;\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}X^{\bullet r_{2}}Y^{\bullet s_{2}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\;}{\;\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(r_{i}+s_{i})\big)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{n}r_{i}!\,s_{i}!\;}

where we use notation

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\,\;:=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big[\underbrace{X,\big[X,\cdots\big[X}_{r_{1}},\big[\underbrace{Y,\big[Y,\cdots\big[Y}_{s_{1}},\cdots,\big[\underbrace{X,\big[X,\cdots\big[X}_{r_{n}},\big[\underbrace{Y,\big[Y,\cdots,Y}_{s_{n}}\big]\big]\big]\cdots\big]\big]\cdots\big]\big]

with the silent assumption that the Lie monomial

\,\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\,

is just
X
, respectively
Y
, when
\,n=1\,
and
\,s_{1}=0\,
,
respectively
\,r_{1}=0\,
, while it is zero whenever
\,s_{n}>1\,
or

\,s_{n}=0\,
and
\,r_{n}>1\,
. In words, when

\,S:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(r_{i}+s_{i})>1\,
the Lie monomial

\,\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\,

is the composition of several operators
{\operatorname{ad}(X)}^{r_{i}}
or
{\operatorname{ad}(Y)}^{s_{i}}
to
Y

— when
\,s_{n}=1\,
— or to
X
— when
\,s_{n}=0\,
and
\,r_{n}=1\,
.
Looking up to second order, (
3.49
) reads

\mathcal{B\,C\,H}(X,Y\,)\,\;:=\;\,X\,+\,Y\,+\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,2\,}\,[X,Y\,]\,+\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}}(3)

(3.50)

where
\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{L}}(3)\,
denotes a (formal) infinite linear combination
of Lie monomials in
X
and
Y
of degree at least 3.

Setting now
\,X:=\hbar^{-1}\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,
and

\,Y:=-\hbar^{-1}\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\,
,
the above analysis yields, rewriting (
3.48
),

\displaystyle\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)

\displaystyle\cdot{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)}^{-1}\,\;=

(3.51)

\displaystyle=\;\,\exp\Big(\,\mathcal{B\,C\,H}\Big(\,\hbar^{-1}\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,,\,-\hbar^{-1}\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\Big)\Big)

where the BCH series has to be expanded as in (
3.49
).
To this end, writing
\,\phi=\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\,
(a sum being tacitly intended) with

\,\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in J_{\hbar}\,

(by Lemma
3.1.1
) we have

\hfil\displaystyle\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,=\,\Delta\big(\phi_{1}\big)\otimes\phi_{2}\,=\,\phi_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,1\otimes\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(1)}\big)}^{+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(2)}\big)}^{+}\otimes\phi_{2}\,=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\phi_{1,3}\,+\,\phi_{2,3}\,+\,{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(1)}\big)}^{+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(2)}\big)}^{+}\otimes\phi_{2}

where we expanded
\,\Delta\big(\phi_{1}\big)\,
as in
Lemma
2.3.5
(d)
and we used that

\,\epsilon\big(\phi_{1}\big)=0\,
. Note that in the expansion of

\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)
we have

\big(\phi_{1,3}\,+\,\phi_{2,3}\big)\,\in\,J_{\hbar}^{(\otimes 3|2)}\;\;,\qquad{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(1)}\big)}^{\!+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{1})}_{(2)}\big)}^{\!+}\otimes\phi_{2}\,\in\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes 3}

(3.52)

where we introduced the notation

\;J_{\hbar}^{(\otimes 3|N)}:=\sum_{\!\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\,\geqslant\,0\\
a+b+c\,\geqslant\,N\end{subarray}}\!J_{\hbar}^{\,a}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,J_{\hbar}^{\,b}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,J_{\hbar}^{\,c}\;
(for
\,N\in\mathbb{N}\,
).

A similar analysis for
\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\,
,
just switching the roles of
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}\,
,  yields

\hfil\displaystyle\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\,=\,\phi_{1}\otimes\Delta\big(\phi_{2}\big)\,=\,\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\otimes 1\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(1)}\big)}^{+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(2)}\big)}^{+}\,=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\phi_{1,2}\,+\,\phi_{1,3}\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(1)}\big)}^{+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(2)}\big)}^{+}

with

\big(\phi_{1,2}\,+\,\phi_{1,3}\big)\,\in\,J_{\hbar}^{(\otimes 3|2)}\;\;,\qquad\phi_{1}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(1)}\big)}^{\!+}\otimes{\big({(\phi_{2})}_{(2)}\big)}^{\!+}\,\in\,J_{\hbar}^{\,\otimes 3}

(3.53)

Now, thanks to Lemma
2.3.5
(c)
,
from (
3.52
) and
(
3.53
) we get

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\big[\hbar\,X,\hbar\,Y\big]\;=\;\big[\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\big]\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,\big[\,\phi_{1,3}+\phi_{2,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,2}+\phi_{1,3}\,\big]\;+\;\hbar\cdot\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{(\otimes 3|4)}\Big)\,\;=\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big[\,\phi_{1,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,2}\,\big]+\big[\,\phi_{2,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,2}\,\big]+\big[\,\phi_{2,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,3}\,\big]\;+\;\hbar\cdot\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\Big)\quad

hence

\!\!\!\big[X,Y\big]\,=\,\hbar^{-1}\Big(\hbar^{-1}\big[\phi_{1,3},\phi_{1,2}\big]+\hbar^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3},\phi_{1,2}\big]+\hbar^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3},\phi_{1,3}\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\Big)\Big)

(3.54)

for some element
\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\Big)\in J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\,
,
where hereafter we use notation

J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|N]}\,:=\hskip-7.0pt\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\,\geqslant\,1\\
a+b+c\,\geqslant\,N\end{subarray}}\hskip-11.0ptJ_{\hbar}^{\otimes a}\otimes J_{\hbar}^{\otimes b}\otimes J_{\hbar}^{\otimes c}\;\subseteq\;{\textsl{Ker}\Big(\epsilon_{{}_{{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\otimes 3}}}\Big)}^{N}\qquad\forall\;\;N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

(3.55)

Pushing the analysis further on, we find easily that

\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\;\in\;\hbar^{-1}\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|S+1]}\qquad\forall\;\;S:={\textstyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}}\,(r_{i}+s_{i})>1\,

(3.56)

looking at (
3.49
), this tells us that the expansion of the
BCH series occurring in (
3.51
), when expanded as in
(
3.49
), is actually given by
\hbar^{-1}
multiplied by

a truly convergent series inside

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\otimes 3}\,
.
In other words, tiding everything up
— from (
3.51
), (
3.49
),
(
3.50
), (
3.54
) and
(
3.56
) altogether —
we find that there exists some

\,\mathcal{Z}\in J_{\hbar}^{\otimes 3}\subseteq{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\otimes 3}\,
such that

\;\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\cdot{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)}^{-1}=\,\exp\big(\,\hbar^{-1}\mathcal{Z}\,\big)\;

Even more, by (
3.49
) and (
3.50
)
and the previous analysis we do know the expansion of this
\mathcal{Z}

up to second order, whence we find

\displaystyle\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)\cdot{\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\mathcal{F}\,)}^{-1}\;=

(3.57)

\displaystyle=\;\,\exp\Big(\,\hbar^{-1}\,\Big(\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,-\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\;-

\displaystyle-\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{1,3},\phi_{1,2}\big]-\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3},\phi_{1,2}\big]-\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3},\phi_{1,3}\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes\,3|4]}\Big)\!\Big)\!\Big)

Now we go and work instead on the right-hand side of (
3.47
).
Again, replacing
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1{}2}^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2{}3}\;=\;\exp\!\big(\!-\!\hbar^{-1}\phi\otimes 1\big)\cdot\exp\!\big(\,\hbar^{-1}1\otimes\phi\big)\,\;=

\displaystyle=\;\exp\!\big(\!-\!\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1,2}\big)\cdot\exp\!\big(\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2,3}\big)\;=\;\exp\!\Big(\mathcal{B\,C\,H}\big(\!-\!\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\,\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{2,3}\,\big)\Big)

Now for the computation
of
\;\mathcal{B\,C\,H}\big(\!-\!\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{2,3}\big)\;
;
to avoid possible confusion, we denote the second, right-hand instance of
\phi
by

\phi^{\prime}\,
. We begin noting that

\big[\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\phi^{\prime}_{2,3}\,\big]\,\;=\;\,\big[\,\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\otimes 1\,,1\otimes\phi^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\phi^{\prime}_{2}\,\big]\,\;=\;\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\phi_{2}\,,\phi^{\prime}_{1}\,\big]\otimes\phi^{\prime}_{2}\,\;\in\;\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|3]}

so that for
\;X:=-\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{1,2}\;
and
\;Y:=\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{2,3}\;

we get, using Lemma
2.3.5
(c)
,

\big[X,Y\big]\;=\;\big[-\!\hbar^{-1}\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\hbar^{-1}\,\phi^{\prime}_{2,3}\,\big]\;=\;-\!\hbar^{-2}\big[\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\phi^{\prime}_{2,3}\,\big]\;\in\;\hbar^{-2}\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}^{\otimes 3}\;=\;\hbar^{-1}J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|3]}

A second, similar step gives
(with obvious notation
\phi\,
,
\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi^{\prime\prime}\,
)

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\big[\,\phi^{\prime}_{1,2}\,,\phi^{\prime\prime}_{2,3}\,\big]\big]\,\;=\;\,\big[\,\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\otimes 1\,,\phi^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\big[\,\phi^{\prime}_{2}\,,\phi^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\big]\otimes\phi^{\prime\prime}_{2}\,\big]\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big[\,\phi_{1}\,,\phi^{\prime}_{1}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\cdot\big[\,\phi^{\prime}_{2}\,,\phi^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\big]\otimes\phi^{\prime\prime}_{2}\,+\,\phi_{1}\cdot\phi^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\big[\,\phi_{2}\,,\big[\,\phi^{\prime}_{2}\,,\phi^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\big]\big]\otimes\phi^{\prime\prime}_{2}\,\;\in\;\,\hbar^{2}\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}

so that

\;\big[X,\big[X,Y\,\big]\big]\,\in\,\hbar^{-3}\,\hbar^{2}\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\,=\,\hbar^{-1}\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 3|4]}\;
. More in general, iteration yields

\big[\,X^{\bullet r_{1}}Y^{\bullet s_{1}}\cdots X^{\bullet r_{n}}Y^{\bullet s_{n}}\big]\;\in\;\hbar^{-S}\,\hbar^{S-1}\,J_{\hbar}^{\,[\otimes 3|S+1]}\;=\;\hbar^{-1}\,J_{\hbar}^{\,[\otimes 3|S+1]}

(3.58)

with notation as before, still using Lemma
2.3.5
(c)
.
Tiding everything up we find that there exists

\,\mathcal{W}\in J_{\hbar}^{\otimes 3}\subseteq{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}\,

such that

\;\mathcal{F}_{1{}2}^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2{}3}\,=\,\exp\big(\,\hbar^{-1}\mathcal{W}\,\big)\;
;
moreover, by (
3.49
) and (
3.50
)
along with the previous analysis we can write

\mathcal{F}_{1,2}^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2,3}\;=\;\exp\Big(\,\hbar^{-1}\Big(\!-\phi_{1,2}\,+\,\phi_{2,3}\,-\,\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\big[\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\,\phi_{2,3}\,\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes\,3|4]}\Big)\Big)

(3.59)

Finally, comparing (
3.57
),
(
3.59
) and (
3.47
) we get the identity in

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}

\hfil\displaystyle\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)(\phi)\,-\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)(\phi)\;-\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hskip 11.0pt-\,\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{1,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,2}\big]-\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,2}\big]-\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\big[\phi_{2,3}\,,\,\phi_{1,3}\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes\,3|4]}\Big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,-\phi_{1,2}\,+\,\phi_{2,3}\,-\,\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\big[\,\phi_{1,2}\,,\,\phi_{2,3}\,\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}\Big(J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes\,3|4]}\Big)

that in turn, through simplification and reduction modulo

\,\hbar\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}\,
,  yields the following identity inside

{{F[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}\,

\displaystyle\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}\,\big)\,-\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}\,\big)\,+\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,-\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\;+

(3.60)

\displaystyle+\;2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\,+\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\,+\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\;\underset{{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes\,3\,|4]}}{\equiv}\;0

where hereafter we adopt the notation for which
\,\overline{\varphi}\,

denotes the coset modulo
\,\hbar\,
of any element

\,\varphi\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}\,
with
\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
.

Now let
\,\Bbbk\big[\mathbb{S}_{3}\big]\,
act onto
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,3}

and consider in particular the action of the antisymmetrizer

\;\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}:=\big(\operatorname{id}-(1\,2)-(2\,3)-(3\,1)+(1\,2\,3)+(3\,2\,1)\big)\;

onto the above identity: this in turn yields a new identity.
Within the latter, we have a first contribution of the form

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}\,\big)\,-\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}\,\big)\Big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big(\,\nabla\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}\,\big)-\big(\,\nabla\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi}_{2,1}\big)\;+\;\text{c.p}\,\;=\;\,\big(\,\nabla\otimes\text{id}\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,\big)\;+\;\text{c.p}

and a second contribution of the form

\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\big(\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,-\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big)\,\;=\;\,0

The third and last contribution is

\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,+\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,+\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)

We go and compute the first summand, as follows:

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,\;=\;\,\big(\operatorname{id}-(2\,3)\big).\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\;-\;2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,\big\}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\;\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\;+\;\text{c.p.}

A similar analysis applies to the third summand, which yields

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,\;=\;\,\big(\operatorname{id}-(1\,2)\big).\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\;-\;2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\;\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\;+\;\text{c.p.}

whereas for the second summand instead we get

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,\;=\;\,\big(\operatorname{id}-(1\,3)\big).\Big(\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\;+\;\text{c.p.}\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\;-\;\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{3,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,1}\,\big\}\;+\;\text{c.p.}

Putting all these together we find

\hfil\displaystyle\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,+\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,+\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3\,}.\Big(\,2^{-1}\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\,\big\}\Big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\big\}\,+\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\big\}\,-\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{3,2}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,1}\big\}\,+\,\big\{\,\overline{\phi}_{1,3}\,,\,\overline{\phi}_{2,3}\big\}\,+\,\text{c.p.}\;=\;\big\{\!\big\{\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,,\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,\big\}\!\big\}

where the very last identity follows from a routine calculation.
Joint with the previously found identities,
the latter gives yet the following, last one, which is the result of letting

\,\textsl{Alt}_{\,3}\,
act onto the congruence (
3.60
):

\Big(\!\big(\,\nabla\otimes\operatorname{id}\,\big)\big(\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,\big)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\Big)\,+\,\big\{\!\big\{\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,,\,\overline{\phi_{a}}\,\big\}\!\big\}\,\;\underset{{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes\,4]}}{\equiv}\;\,0

At last, recalling that
\,c:=\overline{\phi_{a}}\,\big(\,\text{mod\ }{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\big)\,
in
\,{\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,
, and that in the latter Lie bialgebra the Lie cobracket, resp. the Lie bracket, is given by
\,\nabla\,
, resp. by
\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,
, reduced modulo
{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
, the last formula above — in
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,3}
— implies

\Big(\!\big(\,\delta\otimes\operatorname{id}\,\big)(c\,)\,+\,\text{c.p.}\Big)\;+\;[[\,c\,,c\,]]\,\;=\;\,0

within
{\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{\otimes 3}
,  which implies exactly that

\,c\,
— which is antisymmetric by construction —
is an antisymmetric twist for the Lie bialgebra

\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}={\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
,  q.e.d.

(c)
We adopt the following notational convention: any element in
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
will be denoted by an italic letter, say
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
; then its coset modulo
\,\hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
will be denoted with a line over that letter, say
\,\overline{f}:=\big(\,f\ \text{mod}\ \hbar\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\big)\,
, and finally the coset of the latter modulo
\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
will be denoted by the corresponding letter in roman font, say
\,\text{f}:=\big(\,\overline{f}\ \text{mod}\ {\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)\,
. Note also that every element in
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}={\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,
can be written as such an
\;\text{f}=\big(\,\overline{f}\ \text{mod}\ {\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\,\big)\,
for some
\,f\in J_{\hbar}\in\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{}_{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}\big)\,
.

Similar notation will be used for elements in
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}
and their coset modulo
\hbar
and (further on) modulo
\,{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}:={\mathfrak{m}}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{m}}\,
.

Recall that the Lie cobracket induced on
\,{\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,
by the deformed quantization is defined by

\delta^{\,\mathcal{F}}(\,\text{f}\,)\;:=\;\Big(\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}-{\big(\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}\big)}^{2{}1}\Big)\big(\,\overline{f}\,\big)\ \ \text{mod}\ {\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}\;=\;\overline{\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}(f)}-\overline{{\big(\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}\big)}^{\text{op}}(f)}\,\ \ \text{mod}\ {\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}

so we start computing
\,\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}(f)\,
. Definitions give

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}(f)\,\;=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}(\,\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\Delta(f)\big)\,=\,\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\Big(\!\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\cdot\big(\,1\otimes f_{(2)}\big)\!\Big)\,\;=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\cdot\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,1\otimes f_{(2)}\big)

In the last product, we focus on the first factor: thus we get

\hfil\displaystyle\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\big)\,\;=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}\big(\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\big)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\,\;=\;\,\exp\big(\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\big)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,n!\,}\;{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)}^{n}\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\,\;=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,n!\,}\;{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\big)}^{n}\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\frac{\,1\,}{\,n!\,}\;{\operatorname{ad}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\big)}^{n}\big(\,f_{(1)}\big)\otimes\phi_{2}^{\;n}\,\;=\;\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f_{(1)}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)

that is in short

\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\big)\,\;=\;\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f_{(1)}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)

where hereafter
\,\mathcal{O}(2)\,
denotes any element in
\,J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}\,
. A similar calculation yields

\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,1\otimes f_{(2)}\big)\,\;=\;\,1\otimes f_{(2)}\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f_{(2)}\,\big]\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)

Pasting together the last two identities we find

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}(f)\,\;=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}\big)\,\;=\;\,\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1\,\big)\cdot\operatorname{Ad}(\mathcal{F}\,)\big(\,1\otimes f_{(2)}\big)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\,\big(\,f_{(1)}\otimes 1+\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f_{(1)}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}+\mathcal{O}(2)\big)\cdot\big(\,1\otimes f_{(2)}+\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f_{(2)}\,\big]+\mathcal{O}(2)\big)\,=

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}\,+\,f_{(1)}\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f_{(2)}\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f_{(1)}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,f_{(2)}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}\,+\,\epsilon\big(f_{(1)}\big)\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f_{(2)}\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f_{(1)}\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,\epsilon\big(f_{(2)}\big)\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)\,\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\Delta(f)\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,+\,\mathcal{O}(2)

so that, eventually, we get in short

\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}(f)\,\;\underset{J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}}{\equiv}\;\,\Delta(f)\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}

Therefore, for
\,\nabla_{\!\mathcal{F}}:=\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}-{\big(\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}\big)}^{2{}1}\,
we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{\!\mathcal{F}}(f)

\displaystyle\underset{J_{\hbar}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}}{\equiv}\;\,\Delta(f)\,+\,\phi_{1}\otimes\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f\,\big]\,+\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f\,\big]\otimes\phi_{2}\,-

(3.61)

\displaystyle-\,\Delta^{\text{op}}\!(f)\,-\,\big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{2}\,,f\,\big]\otimes\phi_{1}\,-\,\phi_{2}\otimes\Big[\,\hbar^{-1}\phi_{1}\,,f\,\Big]\,\;=

\displaystyle=\;\,\nabla(f)\,+\,\phi_{1}^{(a)}\otimes\hbar^{-1}\Big[\,\phi_{2}^{(a)},\,f\,\Big]\,+\,\hbar^{-1}\Big[\,\phi_{1}^{(a)},\,f\,\Big]\otimes\phi_{2}^{(a)}\;=

\displaystyle=\;\,\nabla(f)\,-\,\phi_{1}^{(a)}\otimes\hbar^{-1}\Big[\,f\,,\,\phi_{2}^{(a)}\Big]\,-\,\hbar^{-1}\Big[\,f\,,\,\phi_{1}^{(a)}\Big]\otimes\phi_{2}^{(a)}

where we used notation
\;\phi_{a}:=\phi-\phi_{\,2{}1}=\phi_{1}^{(a)}\otimes\phi_{2}^{(a)}\;
.
When we reduce the last identity in (
3.61
) modulo

\,\hbar\,J_{\hbar}^{\otimes 2}\,
we end up with

\nabla_{\!\mathcal{F}}\big(\,\overline{f}\,\big)\,\underset{{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}}{\equiv}\,\nabla\big(\,\overline{f}\,\big)\,-\,\overline{\phi_{1}^{(a)}}\otimes\Big\{\,\overline{f}\,,\,\overline{\phi_{2}^{(a)}}\,\Big\}\,-\,\Big\{\,\overline{f}\,,\,\overline{\phi_{1}^{(a)}}\,\Big\}\otimes\overline{\phi_{2}^{(a)}}

hence reducing the latter modulo
{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}
we find in

\;{\mathfrak{m}}^{\otimes 2}\!\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 3]}={\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,

the identity

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\Big(\,\nabla_{\!\mathcal{F}}\text{\ mod\ }{\mathfrak{m}}^{[\otimes 2\,|3]}\Big)(\,\text{f}\,)\,\;=\;\,\delta(\,\text{f}\,)\,-\,c_{\,1}\otimes\big[\,\text{f}\,,\,c_{\,2}\big]\,-\,\big[\,\text{f}\,,\,c_{\,1}\big]\otimes c_{\,2}\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,\delta(\,\text{f}\,)\,-\,\big(\,\operatorname{ad}(\,\text{f}\,)\big)(c)\,\;=\;\,\big(\,\delta-\partial_{c}\big)(\,\text{f}\,)\,\;=\;\,\delta^{\,c}(\,\text{f}\,)\quad

which means that the induced Lie cobracket on

\,{\mathfrak{m}}\Big/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}={\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\,
is just
\delta^{\,c}
,  q.e.d.
∎

Example 3.4.7
.

Let
\,G:=\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\,
be the general linear group over
\Bbbk\,
,
and
\,{\mathfrak{g}}:=\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
. We consider the QUEA

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}=U_{\hbar}\big(\,\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\hskip-1.0pt\big)\,
and the QFSHA

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}=F_{\hbar}\big[\big[\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\big]\big]\,
introduced in
Example
3.2.3
. Letting
{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}
and

{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}
be the Borel Lie subalgebras in
{\mathfrak{g}}
of lower triangular and upper
triangular matrices, respectively, the subalgebra
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}
of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

generated by the
F_{i}
’s and the
\varGamma_{k}
’s is a QUEA for
{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}\,
,
while the subalgebra
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}
generated by the
E_{i}
’s and the

\varGamma_{k}
’s is a QUEA for
{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}\,

— both being also Hopf subalgebras of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, indeed. Dually, the QFSHA

\,{F_{\hbar}[[B^{-}\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}}^{*}\,
identifies with the Hopf algebra quotient of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

obtained by modding out the ideal generated by the
x_{i,\,j}
’s with
\,i<j\,
;
similarly, the QFSHA
\,{F_{\hbar}[[B^{+}\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}}^{*}\,
identifies with the Hopf algebra quotient of

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
obtained by modding out the ideal generated by the
x_{i,\,j}
’s with

\,i>j\,
. Therefore, from the presentation of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
in
Example
3.2.3
one deduces the following
presentations for these quotient Hopf algebras:
{F_{\hbar}[[B^{-}\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is generated by the entries
of the “lower triangular
q
–matrix”

\,{\big(x^{-}_{i,\,j}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\,
with
\,x^{-}_{i,\,j}:=x_{i,\,j}\,

for all
\,i\geq j\,
and
\,x^{-}_{i,\,j}:=0\,
for all
\,i<j\,
,  and similarly

{F_{\hbar}[[B^{+}\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is generated by the entries of the “upper triangular
q
–matrix”

\,{\big(x^{+}_{i,\,j}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\,
where

\,x^{+}_{i,\,j}:=x_{i,\,j}\,
when
\,i\leq j\,
and
\,x^{+}_{i,\,j}:=0\,
for

\,i>j\,
.

Now we consider a new group
\,G\,
which is “double version” of

\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\,
, in that it is a Manin double of
B^{-}
and
B^{+}\,
;
