its tangent Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
then is the Manin double of
{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}
and

{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}\,
; in particular,
\,G=B^{-}\times B^{+}\,
as algebraic varieties
(not as groups), with
B^{-}
and
B^{+}
being embedded as subgroups,
whereas
\,{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{b}}^{-}\oplus{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}\,
as vector spaces, with
{\mathfrak{b}}^{-}
and

{\mathfrak{b}}^{+}
being embedded as Lie subalgebras (this case is explained in detail in

[
GaGa2
]
when
G
is a “double version” of a semisimple (connected) group:

\,G=\textit{SL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)\,
is such an example, and
\textit{GL}_{\,n}(\Bbbk)

is just a very slight variation of that).

For these new
G
and
{\mathfrak{g}}
, a QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is defined as follows:
it is the unital, associative,
\hbar
–adically complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra with generators

F_{1}\,,F_{2}\,,\dots,F_{n-1}\,,\varGamma^{-}_{1}\,,\varGamma^{-}_{2}\,,\dots,\varGamma^{-}_{n-1}\,,\varGamma^{-}_{n}\,,\varGamma^{+}_{1}\,,\varGamma^{+}_{2}\,,\dots,\varGamma^{+}_{n-1}\,,\varGamma^{+}_{n}\,,E_{1}\,,E_{2}\,,\dots,E_{n-1}

and relations

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\,,\varGamma^{\pm}_{\ell}\big]=0\;,\quad\big[\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\,,F_{j}\big]=-\delta_{k,j}\,F_{j}\;,\quad[\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\,,E_{j}]=+\delta_{k,j}\,E_{j}\;,\quad\big[\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\,,\varGamma^{\mp}_{\ell}\big]=0

\hfil\displaystyle\big[E_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,\delta_{i,j}\,{{\;\;e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{+}_{i}-\varGamma^{+}_{i+1})}-e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{-}_{i+1}-\varGamma^{-}_{i})}\;\;}\over{\;\;e^{+\hbar}-e^{-\hbar}\;\;}}

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]\,=\,0\;\;,\qquad\big[F_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|>1\,\phantom{.}\;

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill E_{i}^{2}\,E_{j}-\left(q+q^{-1}\right)E_{i}\,E_{j}\,E_{i}+E_{j}\,E_{i}^{2}\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|=1\,\phantom{.}\;

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill F_{i}^{2}\,F_{j}-\left(q+q^{-1}\right)F_{i}\,F_{j}\,F_{i}+F_{j}\,F_{i}^{2}\,=\,0\hfill\forall\;\;i\,,j\;\colon|i-j|=1\,.\;

where
\,[X\,,Y]:=X\,Y-Y\,X\,
again.
The Hopf structure then is given by

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta(F_{i})\,=\,F_{i}\otimes e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{-}_{i+1}-\varGamma^{-}_{i})}+1\otimes F_{i}\;,\;\;\quad S(F_{i})\,=\,-F_{i}\,e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{-}_{i}-\varGamma^{-}_{i+1})}\;,\;\;\;\quad\epsilon(F_{i})\,=\,0

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\,\Delta\big(\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\big)\,=\,\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\otimes 1+1\otimes\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\;,\;\quad\qquad\qquad S\big(\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\big)\,=\,-\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\;,\;\;\quad\qquad\epsilon\big(\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}\big)\,=\,0

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta(E_{i})\,=\,E_{i}\otimes 1+e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{+}_{i}-\varGamma^{+}_{i+1})}\otimes E_{i}\;,\;\;\quad S(E_{i})\,=\,-e^{\hbar\,(\varGamma^{+}_{i+1}-\varGamma^{+}_{i})}\,E_{i}\;,\;\;\quad\epsilon(E_{i})\,=\,0

In fact, this
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
can be realized as a
quantum double
of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}
and

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}\,
:  in particular, then, we have a decomposition

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}\,
as coalgebras.
Dually, the latter implies that for the QFSHA
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,

we have an identification
as algebras

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,=\,{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}\big)}^{*}\,=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{-})}}^{*}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{b}}^{+})}}^{*}\,=\,{F_{\hbar}[[B^{-}\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[B^{+}\hskip 1.5pt]]}

As a consequence, exploiting the presentations of
{F_{\hbar}[[B^{-}\hskip 1.5pt]]}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[B^{+}\hskip 1.5pt]]}

given above, we find in a nutshell a presentation for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

as being the algebra generated by the entries of the “
q
–matrix in blocks”

\begin{pmatrix}\!\;X^{+}&\!\!\mathbf{0}_{n\times n}\,\\
\ \mathbf{0}_{n\times n}&\!\!X^{-}\,\end{pmatrix}\qquad\text{with}\quad X^{\pm}:={\big(x^{\pm}_{i,\,j}\big)}_{i=1,\dots,n;}^{j=1,\dots,n;}\text{\ \ \ as defined above (triangular).}

Moreover, explicit identifications
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\ast}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,

can be encoded in the Hopf pairing

\;\langle\,\ ,\ \rangle:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\times{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

given on generators by the following values:

\hskip-3.0pt\begin{aligned} \hskip-11.0pt\Big\langle x^{-}_{i,\,j}\,,{\textstyle\prod_{k=1}^{n}}{\big(\varGamma^{+}_{k}\big)}^{g_{k}}\Big\rangle=0=\big\langle x^{-}_{i,\,j}\,,E_{t}\big\rangle\;\;,\!\!\quad\big\langle x^{+}_{i,\,j}\,,F_{t}\big\rangle=0=\Big\langle x^{+}_{i,\,j}\,,{\textstyle\prod_{k=1}^{n}}{\big(\varGamma^{-}_{k}\big)}^{g_{k}}\Big\rangle\hskip-15.0pt\\
\big\langle x^{-}_{i,\,j}\,,F_{t}\big\rangle=\delta_{i,j+1}\,\delta_{t,j}\qquad,\qquad\qquad\big\langle x^{+}_{i,\,j}\,,E_{t}\big\rangle=\delta_{i+1,j}\,\delta_{i,t}\hskip 39.0pt\\
\Big\langle x^{+}_{i,\,j}\,,{\textstyle\prod_{k=1}^{n}}{\big(\varGamma^{+}_{k}\big)}^{g_{k}}\Big\rangle\,=\,\delta_{i,j}\,(1-\delta_{g_{i},0})\,{\textstyle\prod_{k\not=i}}\delta_{g_{k},0}\,=\,\Big\langle x^{-}_{i,\,j}\,,{\textstyle\prod_{k=1}^{n}}{\big(\varGamma^{-}_{k}\big)}^{g_{k}}\Big\rangle\end{aligned}

(3.62)

In particular, from the first line in (
3.62
) note that if

\underline{\varGamma}_{1}
and
\underline{\varGamma}_{2}

are two monomials in the
\varGamma^{\pm}_{k}
’s, then for all
\,i=1,\dots,n\,

we have

\Big\langle x^{\pm}_{i,\,i}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}_{1}\cdot\underline{\varGamma}_{2}\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle x^{\pm}_{i,\,i}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}_{1}\Big\rangle\cdot\Big\langle x^{\pm}_{i,\,i}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}_{2}\Big\rangle

(3.63)

Thanks to Proposition
4.1.2
later on, any polar twist for

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
can be seen as a polar 2–cocycle for
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
.
Now, some examples of the latter were introduced in
Example
3.3.8
above for a large class of QUEA,
including that for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
. The same procedure can be
applied to the present case, which is a slight variation of that case applied to

\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\Bbbk)
instead of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
, as follows.

Let
{\mathfrak{h}}
be the
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–span of

\,B_{\varGamma}:=\big\{\,\varGamma^{+}_{k},\varGamma^{-}_{k}\,\big|\,k=1,\dots,n\,\big\}\,
.
Then fix an antisymmetric,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\,\chi:{\mathfrak{h}}\times{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,

whose matrix of values on pairs of elements in the
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
B_{\varGamma}
is

\,X={\Big(\,\chi^{\varepsilon,\,\eta}_{k,\,t}=\chi\big(\varGamma^{\varepsilon}_{k}\,,\varGamma^{\eta}_{t}\,\big)\!\Big)}_{k,t=1,\dots,n;}^{\varepsilon,\,\eta\in\{+\,,-\}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{2n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
. Any such map
\chi
also induces uniquely an
antisymmetric,

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map
\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
on

\,U_{\hbar}({\mathfrak{h}})=\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}({\mathfrak{h}}):=\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,n}({\mathfrak{h}})}\,
with values in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,
by setting

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(z,1):=\epsilon(z)=:\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(1,z)\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;z\in\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}({\mathfrak{h}})

(3.64)

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=\chi(x,y)\qquad\hskip 55.0pt\qquad\forall\;\;x,y\in S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,1}({\mathfrak{h}})

\displaystyle\hfill\qquad\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=0\quad\qquad\forall\;\;x\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,r}({\mathfrak{h}})\,,\;y\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,s}({\mathfrak{h}})\,:\,r,s\geq 1\,,\;r+s>2\enskip

Then we can define a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear map

\;\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}:=e^{\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{{}_{U}}}={\textstyle\sum_{m\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-m}\,{\tilde{\chi}_{{}_{U}}}^{\,\ast\,m}\!\Big/2^{m}\,m!\;

from
\,U_{\hbar}({\mathfrak{h}})\mathop{\widehat{\otimes}}\limits_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}U_{\hbar}({\mathfrak{h}})\,

to
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
,  which — like in
[
GaGa2
, Lemma 5.2.2]
—
happens to be a well-defined
polar 2–cocycle
for

\,U_{\hbar}({\mathfrak{h}})\,
.

Assume now that
\,\chi
satisfies the additional constraint

\;\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\,=\,\chi(\,-\,,S_{i})
for all

\,i\in I:=\{1,\dots,n-1\}\,
,  where

\,S_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,\varGamma^{+}_{i}-\varGamma^{+}_{i+1}+\varGamma^{-}_{i}-\varGamma^{-}_{i+1}\big)\,
for all
\,i\in I\,
;
note that requiring
\;\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\;
for all
\,i\in I\,

is enough (by antisymmetry), and the latter in turn is equivalent to requiring

\;\chi^{+,\,\eta}_{i,\,t}-\chi^{+,\,\eta}_{i+1,\,t}+\chi^{-,\,\eta}_{i,\,t}-\chi^{-,\,\eta}_{i+1,\,t}=0\;
for all
\,i\in I\,
, all
\,t=1,\dots,n-1\,

and all
\,\eta\in\{+\,,-\}\,
. Then
\chi
induces a unique
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear
map
\;\overline{\chi}:\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;
,  where

\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}:={\mathfrak{h}}\big/\mathfrak{s}\,
with

\,\mathfrak{s}:=\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\{\,S_{i}\,\}}_{i=1,\dots,n-1;}\big)\,
,
given by

\;\overline{\chi}\,\big(H^{\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,,H^{\prime\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,\big)\,:=\,\chi\big(H^{\prime},H^{\prime\prime}\big)\;

for all
\,H^{\prime},H^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
.

Now repeat the above construction with
\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}
and

\overline{\chi}
replacing
{\mathfrak{h}}
and
\chi\,
:  this yields a
polar
2
–cocycle
\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
for

U_{\hbar}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\,
. But now the additional assumption on

\chi
implies that there exists a
Hopf algebra epimorphism

\;\pi:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\cong U_{\hbar}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\;

given by
\,\pi(E_{i}):=0\,
,
\,\pi(F_{i}):=0\,
— for
\,i=1,\dots,n-1\,

— and
\,\pi(T):=(T+\mathfrak{s})\,\in\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\subseteq U_{\hbar}\big(\,\overline{{\mathfrak{h}}}\,\big)\,
— for
\,T\in{\mathfrak{h}}\,
. Finally, we set

\;\sigma_{\chi}:=\overline{\chi}_{{}_{U}}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\,
,
which is a well-defined polar
2
–cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
again in the sense of Definition
3.3.3
. Note that

\sigma_{\chi}:=\overline{\chi}_{{}_{U}}\circ(\pi\times\pi)=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\widetilde{\overline{\chi}}_{{}_{U}}\big)\circ(\pi\times\pi)=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\widetilde{\overline{\chi}}_{{}_{U}}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\big)

Now let us re-think the polar 2–cocycle
\sigma_{\chi}
for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

as a polar twist for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
. First of all,
comparing (
3.63
) and (
3.64
)
we deduce that the form
\,\widetilde{\overline{\chi}}_{{}_{U}}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\,

in
{\Big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)}^{*}
identifies with

\;\varPhi_{\chi}\,:=\,\sum\limits_{k,\,t=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{\varepsilon,\,\eta\in\{+,-\}}\!\chi_{k,\,t}^{\,\varepsilon,\,\eta}\,y^{\,\varepsilon}_{k,\,k}\otimes y^{\,\eta}_{t,\,t}\;

in
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
,  where

\,y^{\,\varsigma}_{\ell,\ell}:=\log\big(x^{\,\varsigma}_{\ell,\ell}\big)\,

is a well-defined element in
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.

Then, exponentiating yields

\mathcal{F}_{\chi}\;:=\;\sigma_{\chi}\;=\;\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\widetilde{\overline{\chi}}_{{}_{U}}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\big)\;=\;\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\varPhi_{\chi}\big)

which is exactly the polar twist of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
we were looking for.

We can also check directly that this
\mathcal{F}_{\chi}

is indeed a polar twist by an explicit computation.
We see this in the simplest case, when
\,n=2\,
;
the other cases are quite similar,
but require quite another shot of calculations.

We need to compute the coproduct of the
x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}
’s in
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
which is defined (by construction) by the condition

\;\big\langle\,\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big),A\otimes Z\,\big\rangle\,=\,\big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,A\cdot Z\,\big\rangle\;
for all
\,A\,,Z\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
;  since
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
admits the PBW-type basis

\mathcal{B}\;:=\;\Big\{\,F^{f}\,{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{g_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{g_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{g_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{+}\big)}^{g_{2}^{+}}E^{e}\,\Big|\,f,g_{1}^{-},g_{2}^{-},g_{1}^{+},g_{2}^{+},e\in\mathbb{N}\,\Big\}

we can replace
A
and
Z
with any two PBW monomials from
\mathcal{B}\,
. Now, let us say that a PBW monomial of the form
\,\mathcal{M}=F^{f}\,{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{g_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{g_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{g_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{g_{2}^{+}}E^{e}\,
belongs to the root space
\,(e-\!f)\,\alpha\,
. Then root/weight considerations easily show that
\;\big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\mathcal{M}\,\big\rangle\neq 0\;
only for PBW monomials
\,\mathcal{M}\,
in the root space 0, i.e. such that
\,e=f\,
. A straightforward computation gives

E^{e}\cdot F^{f}\;=\;\sum\limits_{s=0}^{e\wedge f}{\big([s]_{q}!\big)}^{2}\,{\bigg[{e\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,{\bigg[{f\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,F^{f-s}\,K_{e,f}(s)\,E^{e-s}

where
\,q:=\exp(\hbar)\,
,
\,[r]_{q}:=\displaystyle{{\,q^{r}-q^{-r}\,}\over{\,q-q^{-1}\,}}\,
,
\,[m]_{q}!:=\prod\limits_{r=1}^{m}[r]_{q}\,
,
\,\displaystyle{{\bigg[{\ell\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}:={{\,[\ell]_{q}!\,}\over{\,[s]_{q}!\,[\ell-s]_{q}!\,}}}\,
and

K_{e,f}(s):=\prod\limits_{r=1}^{s}{{\,\;q^{\,2s-e-f+1-r}K_{+}^{+1}-q^{\,r-1-2s+e+f\,}K_{-}^{-1}\;\,}\over{\,q^{\,r}-q^{\,-r}\,}}

with
\,K_{+}^{+1}:=1\otimes{\exp\big(\!+\!\hbar\,\big(\varGamma_{i}^{+}-\varGamma_{i+1}^{+}\big)\big)}^{\pm 1}\,
and

\,K_{-}^{-1}:={\exp\big(\!-\!\hbar\,\big(\varGamma_{i}^{-}-\varGamma_{i+1}^{-}\big)\big)}^{\pm 1}\otimes 1\,
.
Then the product of two PBW monomials expands into

\hfil\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;F^{f^{\prime}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{+}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{2}^{+}}E^{e^{\prime}}\,\cdot\,F^{f^{\prime\prime}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{+}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{2}^{+}}E^{e^{\prime\prime}}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\sum\limits_{s=0}^{e^{\prime}\wedge f^{\prime\prime}}{\big([s]_{q}!\big)}^{2}\,{\bigg[{e\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,{\bigg[{f\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,F^{f^{\prime}}\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\,F^{f^{\prime\prime}-s}\,K_{e^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime}}(s)\,E^{e^{\prime}-s}\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\,E^{e^{\prime\prime}}\;=\quad\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\sum\limits_{s=0}^{e^{\prime}\wedge f^{\prime\prime}}q^{\,\mathcal{E}(\,\dot{g},\,f^{\prime\prime},\,s\,,e^{\prime},\,\ddot{g})}\,{\big([s]_{q}!\big)}^{2}\,{\bigg[{e^{\prime}\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,{\bigg[{f^{\prime\prime}\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q}\,F^{f^{\prime}+f^{\prime\prime}-s}\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\,K_{e^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime}}(s)\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\,E^{e^{\prime}-s+e^{\prime\prime}}

where

\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}:={\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{+}\big)}^{\dot{g}_{2}^{+}}\,
,

\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}:={\big(\varGamma_{1}^{-}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{1}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{-}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{2}^{-}}{\big(\varGamma_{1}^{+}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{1}^{+}}{\big(\varGamma_{2}^{+}\big)}^{\ddot{g}_{2}^{+}}\,

while
\,\mathcal{E}\big(\,\dot{g}\,,f^{\prime\prime},s\,,e^{\prime},\ddot{g}\big)\in\mathbb{Z}\,

is a suitable exponent. When we expand the last expression w.r.t. the PBW basis

\mathcal{B}\,
, the part given by a linear combination of PBW monomials
in the root space 0 is

{\big(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\big)}_{0}\;=\;\delta_{f^{\prime},\,0}\,\delta_{f^{\prime\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\,\delta_{e^{\prime\prime},\,0}\,q^{\,\mathcal{E}(\,\dot{g},\,e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime},e^{\prime},\,\ddot{g})}\,{\big([e^{\prime}]_{q}!\big)}^{2}\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\,K_{e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\big(e^{\prime}\big)\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}

Eventually, tiding everything up we find

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\Big\langle\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,{\big(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\big)}_{0}\,\Big\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\delta_{f^{\prime},\,0}\,\delta_{f^{\prime\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\,\delta_{e^{\prime\prime},\,0}\,q^{\,\mathcal{E}(\,\dot{g},\,e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime},\,\ddot{g})}\,{\big([e^{\prime}]_{q}!\big)}^{2}\,\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\,K_{e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\big(e^{\prime}\big)\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\,\Big\rangle\quad

Now, a similar analysis yields (notation being obvious, hopefully)

\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\,K_{e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\big(e^{\prime}\big)\,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\,\Big\rangle\;=\;{\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}\,\Big\rangle}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\cdot\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,K_{e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\big(e^{\prime}\big)\Big\rangle\cdot{\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,\underline{\varGamma}\,\Big\rangle}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}

and finally direct computation gives

\;\Big\langle\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\,K_{e^{\prime},\,e^{\prime}}\big(e^{\prime}\big)\Big\rangle\,=\,\delta_{e^{\prime},0}\;
,  which is a direct consequence of the assumption

\;\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\,=\,\chi(\,-\,,S_{i})\;
for
\,i=1,\dots,n-1\,
.
Therefore, we end up with

\Big\langle\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\Big\rangle\;\not=\;0

only for monomials with
\,\big(f^{\prime},e^{\prime}\big)=(0,0)=\big(f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\big)\,
,
and for them we have

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\Big\langle\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\Big\rangle\,=\,\Big\langle\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\otimes\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\Big\rangle\,=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\,\Big\langle x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\cdot\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\Big\rangle\,=\,\Big\langle x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\dot{g}}}\Big\rangle\cdot\Big\langle x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\underline{\varGamma}^{\,\underline{\ddot{g}}}\Big\rangle\,=\,\Big\langle x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\Big\rangle\cdot\Big\langle x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\,,\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\Big\rangle\quad

which in short means
\;\Delta\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,=\,x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\otimes x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\;
,  i.e.
x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}
is group-like. Therefore, for

\,y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}:=\log\big(x^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,

instead we have

\;\Delta\big(y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\big)\,=\,y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\otimes 1+1\otimes y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}\;
,
i.e.
y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}
is primitive.

Eventually, as all the
y^{\,\varepsilon}_{t,\,t}
’s are primitive,
a trivial computation shows that
\mathcal{F}_{\chi}
does obey condition
(
2.7
), hence it is indeed a polar twist, as claimed.

3.5.
Duality issues

The procedures of deformation by twist or by
2
–cocycle,
both for Lie bialgebras and for Hopf algebras, are dual
to each other, in the sense of Proposition
2.1.7

and Proposition
2.2.7
.
Because of this, we are lead to compare these two procedures
before

and
after
specialization, as follows.

Proposition 3.5.1
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QUEA and a QFSHA which are dual to each other,
that is
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
.
Then let
\mathcal{F}
be a twist for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, and
\sigma
be a

2
–cocycle for

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
. Assume that both
\mathcal{F}
and
\sigma
are trivial modulo

\hbar\,
,  so that there exists a corresponding twist
\,c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}

for

\,{\mathfrak{g}}

(induced by
\mathcal{F}
via Theorem
3.1.2
) and a corresponding

2
–cocycle
\,\zeta_{\sigma}
for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
(induced by
\sigma
via
Theorem
3.2.1
). Finally, we identify twists for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

and
2
–cocycles for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
via Proposition
2.2.7
,
and similarly twists for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}
and
2
–cocycles for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}

via Proposition
2.1.7
.

Then the following holds: if
\,\mathcal{F}=\sigma\,
,  then

\,c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\zeta_{\sigma}\,
.
\square

The
proof
of the above claim is trivial
— just track the whole construction of both
\,c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and

\,\zeta_{\sigma}\,
,  and compare the outcomes.

A similar duality result holds for deformations by polar
2
–cocycles and deformations by polar twists.
Indeed, let us first notice that, by the very definitions, if
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
are a QUEA and a QFSHA in duality — i.e.,
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
— then
any polar
2
–cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is automatically a polar twist for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  and viceversa
— see Proposition
4.1.2
later on.

Once this is settled, next result (which mirrors Proposition
3.5.1
above) holds too, whose proof again follows by direct comparison of the two deformation procedures (just tracking the whole construction of
\,\gamma_{\sigma}
and
\,c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\,
,  and comparing the outcomes):

Proposition 3.5.2
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QUEA and a QFSHA which are dual to each other,
that is

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
.
Then let
\sigma
be a polar
2
–cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, and
\mathcal{F}
be a polar twist for

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Let
\,\gamma_{\sigma}
be the
2
–cocycle for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}

induced by
\sigma

via Theorem
3.3.7
, and let

\,c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}

be the twist for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
induced by
\mathcal{F}
via
Theorem
3.4.6
.
Finally, we identify polar
2
–cocycles for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and polar twists
for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
as mentioned above, and similarly we identify twists for

\,{\mathfrak{g}}
and

2
–cocycles for
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}

via Proposition
2.1.7
.

Then the following holds: if
\,\sigma=\mathcal{F}\,
,  then

\,\gamma_{\sigma}=c_{{}_{\mathcal{F}}}\,
.
\square

4.
Deformations vs. QDP

When we interchange (contravariantly) QUEA’s and QFSHA’s via linear duality,
the interaction of such a process with deformation processes (either by twist
or by 2-cocycle) is clear. By Proposition
2.2.7
,
any twist, resp. 2-cocycle, for a given quantum algebra
(a QUEA or QFSHA, say) is automatically a 2-cocycle, resp. a twist,
for its dual (a QFSHA or a QUEA, respectively),
and the corresponding deformation processes “commute” with dualization.

On the other hand, in this section we investigate how the deformation procedures
interact when we interchange (covariantly) QUEA’s and QFSHA’s via Drinfeld’s
functors, as in Theorem
2.4.2
. In other words, we analyze how deformation
procedures behave with respect to the Quantum Duality Principle.

4.1.
Some auxiliary results

We begin with a key observation, which shows a crucial fact:
our “polar 2–cocycles” for any QUEA and “polar twists”
for any QFSHA are actually
standard

2–cocycles and twists, respectively, for the QFSHA and
for the QUEA that are associated with the original quantum
group via Drinfeld’s functors from the QDP.

Here is the precise result:

Lemma 4.1.1
.

(a)  Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, and let
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}

be its associated QFSHA following Theorem
2.4.2
.
Let
\,\sigma:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,
be a

polar 2–cocycle
for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
as in Definition
3.3.3
.
Then the restriction
\sigma{\big|}_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\times{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}
of

\,\sigma
to
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\times{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
is a well-defined,

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–valued bilinear form on
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
, of the form

\;\sigma^{\prime}=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\chi^{\prime}\big)\,
with

\,\chi^{\prime}:={\big(\hbar^{-2}\,\chi\big)}{\big|}_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\times{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\,
,
and this
\,\sigma^{\prime}:=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\chi^{\prime}\big)\,
is a

2-cocycle
for
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
.

(b)  Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and let
{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}

be its associated QUEA following Theorem
2.4.2
. Let

\,\mathcal{F}:=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,
be a

polar twist
for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

as in Definition
3.4.3
.
Then
\,\mathcal{F}:=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\phi^{\vee}\big)\,
with

\,\phi^{\vee}:=\hbar^{-2}\phi\in{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,
,
and, in these terms,

\,\mathcal{F}^{\vee}:=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\phi^{\vee}\big)\,

is a
twist
for
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
.

Proof.

(a)
We retain notation from the proof of
Lemma
3.3.2
,
and we proceed along the same lines.
Thus we set
\,U_{\hbar}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
and

\,J_{\hbar}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(U_{\hbar}\big)\,
,  and we write

\hat{z}:=\epsilon(z)\,,\;\;\;z^{+}:=z-\epsilon(z)=z-\hat{z}\,\in\,J_{\hbar}\;,\quad\text{hence}\quad z=z^{+}+\hat{z}\qquad\forall\;\;z\in U_{\hbar}\;\;

We already saw that

\;\chi(u,v)\,=\,\chi\big(u^{+},v^{+}\big)\;
for all
\,u,v\in U_{\hbar}\,
,
and then for any
\,a\,,b\in U_{\hbar}\,
we have

\sigma(a\,,b\,)\;=\;{\textstyle\sum_{n\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-n}\,{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\chi\big(a^{+}_{(i)},b^{+}_{(i)}\big)\Big/n!

(4.1)

where
\,\otimes_{i=1}^{n}a^{+}_{(i)}=\delta_{n}(a)\,
and

\,\otimes_{i=1}^{n}b^{+}_{(i)}=\delta_{n}(b)\,
.

Now, restricting to
U_{\hbar}^{\;\prime}
we get that

\,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in U_{\hbar}^{\;\prime}\,
yields

\,\delta_{n}\big(a^{\prime}\big),\delta_{n}\big(b^{\prime}\big)\in\hbar^{n}\,{U_{\hbar}}^{\!\!\widehat{\otimes}\,n}\,
;
also, in the sequel we can clearly assume

\,\epsilon\big(a^{\prime}\big)=0=\epsilon\big(b^{\prime}\big)\,
. Then we get

{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\chi^{\prime}\big(a^{\prime\,+}_{(i)},b^{\prime\,+}_{(i)}\big)\,=\,{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\hbar^{-2}\chi\big(a^{\prime\,+}_{(i)},b^{\prime\,+}_{(i)}\big)\;\in\;\hbar^{-2n}\,\hbar^{2n}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;=\;{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;\;\;\;

[formula]

whence, just like in (
4.1
),
we eventually get

\sigma^{\prime}\big(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\,\big)\;=\;{\textstyle\sum_{n\,\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{+n}\,{\textstyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}}\chi^{\prime}\big(a^{\prime\,+}_{(i)},b^{\prime\,+}_{(i)}\big)\Big/n!\;\in\;{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\qquad\forall\;a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in U_{\hbar}^{\;\prime}

which proves the claim.

(b)
This follows directly from
Definition
3.4.3
.
∎

As a direct consequence, we have the following significant result:

Proposition 4.1.2
.

Let
U_{\hbar}
be a QUEA and
F_{\hbar}
be a QFSHA that are dual to each other, i.e. such that
\,F_{\hbar}={(U_{\hbar})}^{\ast}\,
and
\,U_{\hbar}={(F_{\hbar})}^{\star}\,
. Then:

(a)
\sigma
is a polar 2–cocycle for

U_{\hbar}

\,\iff\,

\sigma

is a polar twist for
F_{\hbar}\;
;

(b)
\mathcal{F}
is a polar twist for

F_{\hbar}

\,\iff\,

\mathcal{F}

is a polar 2–cocycle for
U_{\hbar}\;
.

Proof.

The proof follows directly from the very definitions
of polar 2–cocycle and polar twist, along with the observation that

\,F_{\hbar}={(U_{\hbar})}^{\ast}\,
and

\,U_{\hbar}={(F_{\hbar})}^{\star}\,
imply

\,F_{\hbar}^{\,\vee}={(U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime})}^{\star}\,

and
\,U_{\hbar}^{\,\prime}={\big(F_{\hbar}^{\,\vee}\big)}^{\ast}\,
,
by (
2.18
).
∎

4.2.
Drinfeld’s functors and “polar deformations”

In this subsection we analyze the interaction between the
process of “polar deformation” and the action of a Drinfeld’s functor on
some quantum group.

We begin with deformations by polar twist of a QFSHA and the application
to the latter of Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\vee}\,
;
then we shall look at deformation by polar 2–cocycle of a QUEA and the
application to the latter of Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
.

4.2.1
.

Deformations by polar twist under
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\mapsto{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
.

We look now what happens with deformations by polar twist for a QFSHA
when the latter is acted upon by the functor
{(\ )}^{\vee}
which associates with it a
QUEA. Here is our result:

Theorem 4.2.2
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA. Let
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi\big)\,

be a polar twist for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, with
\,\phi\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,

(cf. Definition
3.4.3
).
Set
\,\phi^{\vee}:=\hbar^{-1}\,\log(\mathcal{F}\,)=\hbar^{-2}\phi\,
,
\,\phi_{a}:=\phi-\phi_{\,2,1}\;
and
\;\phi^{\vee}_{a}:=\phi^{\vee}-\phi^{\vee}_{\,2,1}\;
.
Then we have:

(a)
\phi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\phi_{\,2,1}=-\phi\,
,  iff
\mathcal{F}

is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\mathcal{F}_{2,1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,
,  iff
\phi^{\vee}

is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\phi^{\vee}_{\,2,1}=-\phi^{\vee}\,
;

(b)
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\!\big(\hbar\,\phi^{\vee}\big)\,

is a twist element for the QUEA
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
.

(c)  Let
\,c\,
be the antisymmetric twist of
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}

corresponding to
\mathcal{F}
as provided by
Theorem
3.4.6
, and let
\,c^{\vee}

be the similar twist provided by Theorem
3.1.2

along with claim (b) above. Then
\;c=c^{\vee}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This follows directly by construction.

(b)
This is granted by
Lemma
4.1.1
(b)
.

(c)
This follows by a careful — yet entirely straightforward —
check, just tracking both constructions involved (of
c

and of
c^{\vee}
alike).
∎

4.2.3
.

Deformations by polar 2–cocycle under
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\mapsto{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
.

Given a QUEA, we can apply on it Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
;
we now see what happens when a deformation by polar 2–cocycle is performed.
Our result reads as follows:

Theorem 4.2.4
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA. Let
\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\chi\big)\,

be a polar 2–cocycle for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, with

\,\chi\in{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,\big)}^{*}\,

(cf. Definition
3.3.3
).
Set
\,\chi^{\prime}:=\hbar^{-1}\,\log_{*}(\sigma)=\hbar^{-2}\,\chi\,
,

\,\chi_{a}:=\chi-\chi_{\,2,1}\;
and
\;\chi^{\prime}_{a}:=\chi^{\prime}-\chi^{\prime}_{2,1}\;
.
Then we have:

(a)
\chi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\chi_{\,2,1}=-\chi\,
,  iff
\sigma

is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\sigma_{2,1}=\sigma^{-1}\,
,  iff
\chi^{\prime}

is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\chi^{\prime}_{2,1}=-\chi^{\prime}\,
;

(b)
\,\sigma=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar\,\chi^{\prime}\big)\,
is a 2-cocycle for the QFSHA

\,F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{*}\big]\big]:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
.

(c)  Let
\,\gamma\,
be the antisymmetric 2-cocycle of

{\mathfrak{g}}
corresponding to
\sigma
as provided by
Theorem
3.3.5
, and let

\,\gamma^{\prime}
be the similar 2-cocycle provided by
Theorem
3.2.1
along with claim (b) above
— using the identification
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{**}={\mathfrak{g}}\,
.
Then
\;\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This follows directly by construction.

(b)
This is true because of
Lemma
4.1.1
(a)
.

(c)
Much like for
Theorem
4.2.2
(c)
,
this follows again by a straightforward check, just carefully tracking both
constructions involved (of
\gamma

and of
\gamma
alike).
∎

Remark 4.2.5
.

Recall that the notions of twist and that of 2-cocycle are dual to each other
(cf. Proposition
2.2.7
),
and the same holds for those of polar twist and polar 2–cocycle
(cf. §
3.5
). Moreover, Drinfeld’s functors are also
dual to each other, in the sense of (
2.18
).
Taking all this into account, it turns out easily that
Theorem
4.2.2
and
Theorem
4.2.4

above are also “dual to each other”, in that either one of these two statements
can be deduced from the other by a duality argument.

4.3.
Drinfeld’s functors and (standard) deformations

We analyze now the interaction between the process of deformation
— in the standard sense — and the action of a Drinfeld’s functor on some
quantum group.

We begin with deformations by twist of a QUEA and the application to the latter of
Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
;  then we shall look at deformation by 2-cocycle of any
QUEA, to which we apply Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
. In both cases (obviously
related by duality) we have to adopt a slightly stronger assumption, namely that the
given twist, resp. 2-cocycle, is additionally an
R
–matrix, resp. a

\varrho
–comatrix.

4.3.1
.

Deformations by twist under
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\mapsto{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
.

As a first step, we look what happens for deformations by twist of a QUEA
when the latter is acted upon by the functor
{(\ )}^{\prime}
which associates with
it a QFSHA. It turns out that we find a relevant result when we make the stronger
assumption that the given twist is in fact a
(quantum)
R
–matrix twist
,
as in Definition
2.2.1
(d)
. Here is our result:

Theorem 4.3.2
.

Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, and let
\mathcal{F}
be a twist for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
s.t.
\;\phi\equiv 1\;\big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,\big)
;
then
\,\phi:=\hbar^{-1}\log(\mathcal{F}\,)\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,
,  and

\,\mathcal{F}=\exp(\hbar\,\phi)\,
. Set also

\,\phi^{\prime}:=\hbar^{-2}\log(\mathcal{F}\,)=\hbar^{-2}\phi\,
,
\;\phi_{a}:=\phi-\phi_{2,1}\;

and
\;\phi^{\prime}_{a}:=\phi^{\prime}-\phi^{\prime}_{2,1}\;
.
Assume in addition that
\mathcal{F}
is indeed a
(quantum)
R
–matrix twist
,
as in Definition
2.2.1
(d).
Then we have:

(a)
\phi
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\phi_{2,1}=-\phi\,
,  iff
\mathcal{F}

is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\mathcal{F}_{2,1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,
,  iff
\phi^{\prime}
is antisymmetric,
i.e.
\,\phi^{\prime}_{2,1}=-\phi^{\prime}\,
;

(b)
\,\mathcal{F}=\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\phi^{\prime}\big)\,
is a polar twist for the QFSHA

\,F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{*}\big]\big]:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
.

(c)  Let
\,c\,
be the antisymmetric twist of
{\mathfrak{g}}
corresponding to

\mathcal{F}
as provided by Theorem
3.1.2
, and let

\,c^{\,\prime}
be the similar twist provided by
Theorem
3.4.6
along with claim
(b) above — using the identification
\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{**}={\mathfrak{g}}\,
.
Then
\;c=c^{\,\prime}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This follows directly by construction.

(b)
This is proved in
[
EH
, Theorem 0.1]
.
Note that the overall assumption there is that
\mathcal{R}
be an
R
–matrix,
in the standard sense — so that
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is quasitriangular.

Nevertheless
,
all the arguments used there to
prove the main result only apply the defining properties of an
“
R
–matrix” in the sense of Definition
2.2.1
,
namely (
2.8
) and the right-hand side of
(
2.7
); the assumption (
2.10
), instead,
is
never
used
. Therefore, the same arguments,
and the whole proof, used in
[
EH
]
to prove Theorem 0.1
actually do prove also the present statement, that is actually stronger.

(c)
Here again, the proof follows from a straightforward,
careful checking procedure, keeping track of both constructions involved
(of
c
and of
c^{\,\prime}
alike), much like for
Theorem
4.2.2
(c)

and for
Theorem
4.2.4
(c)
.
∎

4.3.3
.

Deformations by 2-cocycle under
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\mapsto{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
.

As a second step, we look what happens to deformations of a QFSHA by 2-cocycle when we apply Drinfeld’s functor
{(\ )}^{\vee}\,
. Here again, we get a relevant result under the stronger assumption that the given 2-cocycle is in fact a
(quantum)
\varrho
–comatrix 2-cocycle
, as in Definition
2.2.4
(d)
. Our result reads as follows:

Theorem 4.3.4
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be any QFSHA, and let
\sigma
be a 2-cocycle for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}

such that
\;\sigma\equiv 1
\;\Big(\,\text{\rm mod}\;\hbar\,{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,\big)}^{\!\star}\,\Big)\,
;  then

\,\varsigma:=\hbar^{-1}\log(\sigma)\in{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,\big)}^{\!\star}\,
,  and

\,\sigma=\exp\!\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,
. Set also

\,\varsigma^{\vee}:=\hbar\,\log(\sigma)=\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\,
,

\;\varsigma_{a}:=\varsigma-\varsigma_{\,2,1}\;
and

\;\varsigma^{\vee}_{a}:=\varsigma^{\vee}-\varsigma^{\vee}_{\,2,1}\;
.
Assume in addition that
\sigma
is a
(quantum)
\varrho
–comatrix
2-cocycle
, as in Definition
2.2.4
(d).

Then the following holds true:

(a)
\varsigma
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\varsigma_{\,2,1}=-\varsigma\,
,
iff
\sigma
is orthogonal, i.e.
\,\sigma_{2,1}=\sigma^{-1}\,
,
iff
\varsigma^{\vee}
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,\varsigma^{\vee}_{\,2,1}=-\varsigma^{\vee}\,
;

(b)
\,\sigma=\exp\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varsigma^{\vee}\big)\,

is a polar 2–cocycle for the QUEA
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
;

(c)  Let
\,\gamma\,
be the antisymmetric 2-cocycle of
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}
corresponding to
\sigma
as provided by Theorem
3.2.1
, and let
\,\gamma^{\vee}
be the similar 2-cocycle provided by
3.3.5
along with claim (b) above. Then
\;\gamma=\gamma^{\vee}\;
.

Proof.

(a)
This is obvious, by standard identities for formal exponentials.

(b)

This claim is the dual to
Theorem
4.3.2
(b)
,
so it follows from that one via a duality argument — involving the results in
§
4.1
, in particular
Proposition
4.1.2
.

(c)
Once more, as in previous cases, the claim follows from direct
checking, keeping track of the two involved 2-cocycles —
\gamma

and of
\gamma^{\vee}
— were constructed.
∎

Remark 4.3.5
.

Much like as we did in Remark
4.2.5
,
we notice here as well that — by the same reasons as before —
Theorem
4.3.2
and
Theorem
4.3.4

above are once more “dual to each other”, in that either one of these two statements
can be deduced from the other by a duality argument.

5.
Morphisms in the “(co)quasitriangular” case

In this section we focus onto
R
–matrices
and
\varrho
–comatrices
.

First, we investigate what happens with
R
–matrices
and

\varrho
–comatrices
with respect to the Quantum Duality Principle,
namely when Drinfeld’s functors are applied. This leads us to introduce the weaker notions of “polar
R
–matrix” and “polar
\rho
–comatrix” which eventually will prove quite significant.

Second, we consider the standard constructions providing morphisms between a
Hopf algebra
H
and its dual when an
R
–matrix or a
\varrho
–comatrix
is available, looking at how this applies to QUEAs and to QFSHAs.
It turns out that the construction then improves, in a way that involves Drinfeld’s
functors, again; moreover, we prove that it can also be extended to the case when

R
–matrices or
\rho
–comatrices are replaced with their “polar” counterparts.

5.1.
R
–matrices and
\varrho
–comatrices w.r.t. QDP:
polar (co)matrices

In next two results, we explain how
R
–matrices and
\rho
–comatrices
“behave well” with respect to Drinfeld’s functors and the Quantum Duality Principle.
In fact, this leads us to introduce the notions of
“polar
R
–matrix”
and
of
“polar
\rho
–comatrix”
, which are straight analogue of the notions of
“polar twist”and of “polar 2–cocycle”.

We begin introducing some more bare definitions:

Definition 5.1.1
.

(a)
Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA. We call
“polar
R
–matrix”
of

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
any
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}
for
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
such that

\;\mathcal{R}\,\equiv\,1^{\otimes 2}\;\;\text{mod}\,\Big(\hbar\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\Big)\;
.

(b)
Let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA. We call
“polar
\rho
–comatrix”

of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
any
\varrho
–comatrix
\rho\,
for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,

such that
\;\rho\,\equiv\,\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\;\;\text{mod}\,\Big(\hbar\,{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)\;
.

Remark 5.1.2
.

In the same spirit of Proposition
2.2.7
and (
2.18
), it is clear that the two notions of “polar
R
–matrix” and of “polar
\varrho
–comatrix” are dual to each other. Namely, let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QUEA and a QFSHA which are dual to each other, i.e.
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
. Then, through the identification
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}^{*}\,
,  we have that
\,\mathcal{R}=\rho\,
is a polar
R
–matrix of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
if and only if it is a polar
\varrho
–comatrix of
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.

Observations 5.1.3
.

(a)
With assumptions as in Definition
5.1.1
(a)

above, let
\mathcal{R}
be any polar
R
–matrix for a QFSHA
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
:  since

\;\mathcal{R}\,\equiv\,1^{\otimes 2}\;\;\text{mod}\,\hbar\;
,  we can write
\mathcal{R}

in the form
\;\mathcal{R}\,=\,\exp\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\,\theta\big)\;
for some

\,\theta\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
.

Similarly, if
\rho
is any polar
\varrho
–comatrix for a QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
then we can write it in the form

\;\rho\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{+1}\,\varsigma\big)\;
for some

\,\varsigma\in{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\,
.

(b)
Note that in the very definition of “polar
R
–matrix”,
resp. of “polar
\varrho
–comatrix”, we assume a condition which is quite close,
yet
weaker
, than the one demanded for the definition of
“polar twist”, resp. of “polar 2–cocycle”, in Definition
3.3.3
,
resp. in Definition
3.4.3
. In fact, our choice for these definitions about

R
–matrices and
\varrho
–comatrices is motivated by
Proposition
5.1.4
below,
which eventually implies that
when the two setups overlap, the stronger
condition for twists/2–cocycles actually holds true

— cf. Theorem
4.3.2
and
Theorem
4.3.4
.

The key result about polar
R
–matrices and polar
\varrho
–comatrices is the following:

Proposition 5.1.4
.

(a)  Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, and let
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}
be the QFSHA associated to it by the Quantum Duality Principle, as in Theorem
2.4.2
. Then for any
R
–matrix of
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
of the form
\,\mathcal{R}=\exp\!\big(\hbar\,\theta\big)\,
,  with
\,\theta=\hbar^{-1}\log\,(\mathcal{R})\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,
,  we have

\vartheta\,:=\,\hbar^{2}\,\theta\,=\,\hbar^{+1}\log\,(\mathcal{R})\;\in\;{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}

(b)  Let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and let
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
be the QUEA associated to it by the Quantum Duality Principle, as in Theorem
2.4.2
. Then for any
\varrho
–comatrix of
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
of the form
\,\rho=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar\,\varsigma\big)\,
,  with
\,\varsigma=\hbar^{-1}\log_{*}(\rho)\in{\Big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)}^{\!*}\,
,  we have

\zeta\,:=\,\hbar^{2}\,\varsigma\,=\,\hbar^{+1}\log_{*}(\rho)\;\in\;{\Big({\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)}^{\!*}

Proof.

(a)
This is proved in
[
EH
, Theorem 0.1]
. Indeed, the overall assumption there is that
\mathcal{R}
be an
R
–matrix, in the standard sense — so that
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is quasitriangular.
Nevertheless
,
all the arguments used there to prove the main result only apply the defining properties of an “
R
–matrix” in the sense of Definition
2.2.1
, namely (
2.8
) and the right-hand side of (
2.7
); the assumption (
2.10
), instead, is
never
used
. Therefore, the same arguments, and the whole proof, used in
[
EH
]
to prove Theorem 0.1 actually do prove also the present, stronger statement.

(b)
This follows from claim
(a)
,
by duality, using the duality relation (
2.18
), the fact that

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
is a QUEA when
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is a QFSHA, and
Proposition
2.2.7
.
∎

The previous result has the following, important consequence:

Theorem 5.1.5
.

Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA and
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA; let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}

be the QFSHA and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
be the QUEA provided by the
Quantum Duality Principle, as in Theorem
2.4.2
. Then the following holds:

(a)  every
R
–matrix for
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
which is congruent to
1^{\otimes 2}

modulo
\hbar
is a polar
R
–matrix for
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
;

(b)  every
\varrho
–comatrix for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
which is congruent to

\epsilon^{\otimes 2}
modulo
\hbar
is a polar
\varrho
–comatrix for

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
;

(c)  every
\varrho
–comatrix for
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}
which is congruent to

\epsilon^{\otimes 2}
modulo
\hbar
is a polar
\varrho
–comatrix for

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
itself;

(d)  every
R
–matrix for
\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
which is congruent to

1^{\otimes 2}
modulo
\hbar
is a polar
R
–matrix for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
itself.

Proof.

Claims
(a)
and
(b)
follow directly from
Proposition
5.1.4
, claims
(a)
and
(b)
,
respectively. Now claim
(c)
follows from claim
(b)

applied to the QFSHA
\,F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{*}\big]\big]:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
,
since
\,{F_{\hbar}\big[\big[G^{*}\big]\big]}^{\vee}\!={\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\vee}\!={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

by Theorem
2.4.2
(a)
. Similarly, claim
(d)

follows from
(a)
applied to the QUEA

\,U_{\hbar}\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big):={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,  since

\,{U_{\hbar}\big({\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\big)}^{\prime}={\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{\prime}={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,

by Theorem
2.4.2
(a)
again.
∎

5.2.
Morphisms from
R\,
–matrices and
\varrho\,
–comatrices

We shall now explore what happens if we apply the general constructions leading to
Proposition
2.2.9
, resp. to
Proposition
2.2.10
,
is (tentatively) applied to a QUEA, resp. a QFSHA, as the Hopf algebra
H
to start with,
and a suitable
R
–matrix, resp.
\varrho
–comatrix, for it.
To begin with, we check that Proposition
2.2.9

still makes sense when
\,H:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
is a QUEA:

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
be a QUEA, and
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
be its dual QFSHA, as in
§
2.3.4
. Let
\;\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{R}_{2}\,
(in Sweedler’s notation) be
an
R
–matrix for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Then there exist two morphisms of topological Hopf algebras

\hfil\displaystyle\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;,\qquad\eta\,\mapsto\,\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta):=\eta(\mathcal{R}_{1})\,\mathcal{R}_{2}

\hfil\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\text{\rm op}}\;\;,\qquad\eta\,\mapsto\,\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta):=\mathcal{R}_{1}\,\eta(\mathcal{R}_{2})

Proof.

This is straightforward. Let us write the given
R
–matrix as an

\hbar
–adically convergent series

\;\mathcal{R}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\hbar^{n}\,\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\;

(where each
\,\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\,
is written in Sweedler’s notation). Then

\,\;\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,:=\,\eta(\mathcal{R}_{1})\,\mathcal{R}_{2}\,=\,\sum_{n\geq 0}\hbar^{n}\,\eta\big(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{n}\big)\otimes\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{n}\;\,

for every
\,\eta\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
, where the last term is a well-defined,
\hbar
–adically
convergent series in
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  so that
\,\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,

and the map
\,\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}\,
is well-defined.
The properties making this map
\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}

into a (topological) Hopf morphism then are proved via a formal check, just like for
Proposition
2.2.9
. The proof for
\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}

is pretty similar.
∎

Dually, Proposition
2.2.10

still makes sense when
\,H:={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
is a QFSHA:

Proposition 5.2.2
.

Let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
be a QFSHA, and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,

be its dual QUEA, as in §
2.3.4
.
Let
\,\rho\,
be a
\varrho
–comatrix for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Then there exist two morphisms of topological Hopf algebras

\hfil\displaystyle\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;,\qquad\ell\,\mapsto\,\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}(\ell):=\rho(\ell\,,-)

\hfil\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\text{\rm op}}\;\;,\qquad\ell\,\mapsto\,\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}(\ell):=\rho(-,\ell\,)

Proof.

Again, this is straightforward, the main point being to show that the given maps
are indeed well-defined. We consider
\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}\,
,
the case of
\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}
is similar.

By definition,
\,\rho\in{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\big)}^{\star}\,
,
the latter being the space of all continuous
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear maps from

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
to
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
.
Then for any
\,\ell\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
the “left-hand evaluation” of
\rho
in
\ell

yields clearly a continuous
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear map

\;\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}:=\rho(\ell\,,-):{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,,\,f\mapsto\rho(\ell\,,f)\;
;  thus
\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}

is well defined, q.e.d.

The Hopf properties of this map
\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}

are then proved by direct check.
∎

Note that
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{cop}}\,
is obviously a QUEA for the Lie bialgebra

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\text{cop}}
(the “co-opposite” to
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
, i.e. one reverses the
Lie cobracket), and similarly
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{op}}\,
is a QUEA for the Lie bialgebra

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\text{op}}
(the “opposite” to
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
, using reversed Lie bracket).
Then both previous results provide morphisms between quantum groups of

different
nature, namely a QFSHA as domain and a QUEA as codomain.

Nevertheless, we shall now show that both previous results can be refined,
eventually yielding morphisms that connect quantum groups of the
same

nature, namely both QFSHA’a in one case and both QUEA’s in the other case.

Theorem 5.2.3
.

Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
be a QUEA, let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
be its dual QFSHA, as in
§
2.3.4
, and let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
, resp.
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
, be the QFSHA, resp. the QUEA, introduced in §
2.4
. Let
\;\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{i}\otimes\mathcal{R}_{i}\,
(sum, possibly infinite, over repeated indices) be an
R
–matrix for
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
which is congruent to
1^{\otimes 2}
modulo
\hbar\,
.

Then, for the two morphisms

\;{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;

and

\;{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\;

in Proposition
5.2.1
, the following holds:

(a)  they take values inside
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
,
and so they corestrict to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups;

(b)  they uniquely extend to

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}={\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)}^{\vee}\,
,  i.e. they extend to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras.

Proof.

(a)
Recall that

\,\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\big)=:J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\subseteq\big(J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}+{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,1_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\big)=:I_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\,
,  and
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is a topological
Hopf algebra with respect to the
I_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}
–adic topology.

Recall also that, by Proposition
5.1.4
(a)
,
we can write
\;\mathcal{R}\,:=\,\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta\big)\;
with

\,\vartheta\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
;  we write the latter as

\,\vartheta=\vartheta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\,
(sum over repeated indices),
where
\,\vartheta^{i},\vartheta_{i}\in J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\,
. Then we have

\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta\;=\;\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\;=\;\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta^{i}\big)\otimes\vartheta_{i}\;=\;\theta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}

with
\,\theta^{i}:=\hbar^{-1}\vartheta^{i}\in\hbar^{-1}J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\subseteq J_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}\big)\,
,  where the latter inclusion follows by
the basic properties of
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}
, cf.
[
Ga1
]
. Now writing

\,{\big(\theta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\big)}^{n}=\theta^{j}_{[n]}\otimes\vartheta_{[n],j}\,

for each
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
, we have in particular
\,\theta^{j}_{[n]}\in J_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\;n}\,

and
\,\vartheta_{[n],j}\in J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}\,
, for every
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
.
When we expand
\mathcal{R}\,
, by all this we find

\mathcal{R}\;=\;\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta\big)\,=\,\exp\big(\theta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\big)\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,{\big(\theta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\big)}^{n}\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\big(\theta^{j}_{[n]}\otimes\vartheta_{[n],j}\big)

Therefore, for every
\,\eta\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
we have

\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\;=\;\eta\big(\mathcal{R}^{s}\big)\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\;=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\eta\big(\theta^{j}_{[n]}\big)\,\vartheta_{[n],j}

which describes a well-defined element (a convergent series, in the relevant topology!) of

{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{cop}}
— equal to
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}
as a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module
— exactly because
\,\vartheta_{[n],j}\in J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}\,
for each

\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
. Thus
\Phi_{\!\mathcal{R}}^{\leftarrow}
corestricts to

\,{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{cop}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{cop}}\,
as claimed, q.e.d.

The proof for
\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}
goes exactly the same,
just switching left and right.

(b)
We begin acting as in the proof of
(a)
above,
but switching the roles of left and right hand sides. Namely, we write

\;\hbar\,\vartheta\,=\,\hbar\,\big(\vartheta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\big)=\,\theta^{i}\otimes\vartheta_{i}\;

where
\,\theta_{i}:=\hbar^{-1}\vartheta_{i}\in\hbar^{-1}J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\subseteq J_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}:=\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}\big)\,
, and also

\,{\big(\vartheta^{i}\otimes\theta_{i}\big)}^{n}=\vartheta^{j}_{[n]}\otimes\theta_{[n],j}\,
,
with
\,\vartheta^{j}_{[n]}\in J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}\,
and
\,\theta_{[n],j}\in J_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\;n}\,
,
for all
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
. Then expanding
\mathcal{R}
yields

\mathcal{R}\;=\;\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\vartheta\big)\,=\,\exp\big(\vartheta^{i}\otimes\theta_{i}\big)\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,{\big(\vartheta^{i}\otimes\theta_{i}\big)}^{n}\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\big(\vartheta^{j}_{[n]}\otimes\theta_{[n],j}\big)

hence for every
\,\mu\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
we have

\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\mu)\;:=\;\mu\big(\mathcal{R}^{s}\big)\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\;=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}}{{\,1\,}\over{\,n!\,}}\,\mu\big(\vartheta^{j}_{[n]}\big)\,\theta_{[n],j}

(5.1)

Now, recall that
\,{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)}^{\vee}={\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\,
,  by (
2.18
). Then we consider the formula (
5.1
) for any
\,\mu\in{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)}^{\vee}={\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\,
— which contains
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
. As all coefficients
\mu\big(\vartheta^{\prime}_{n})
belong to
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
, every partial sum in the right-hand side formal series is a well-defined element in
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{cop}}
— equal to
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
as a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module. In addition, since
\,\vartheta^{j}_{[n]}\in J_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}\subseteq I_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}\,
— for each
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
— and
\,\mu:{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
is
continuous
(with respect to the
I_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}^{\;n}
–adic topology on the left and the
\hbar
–adic topology on the right), for every
\,s\in\mathbb{N}\,
there exist
\,n_{s}\,
such that
\,\mu\big(\vartheta^{j}_{[n]})\in\hbar^{n_{s}}{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
for all
\,n\geq n_{s}\,
. This ensures that the formal series in (
5.1
) is actually convergent in the
\hbar
–adic topology of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, thus describing a well-defined element in
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Letting
\mu
range freely inside
{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)}^{\vee}\,
, this proves that
\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}
does indeed extend from
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}
to
\,{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)}^{\vee}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\vee}=:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,
,  q.e.d.

Switching left and right in the arguments above we get the proof for

\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}
too.
∎

Remark 5.2.4
.

Claim
(a)
of Theorem
5.2.3

above appears also in
[
EK
]
, §4.5.

In the dual framework, the parallel result holds true as well:

Theorem 5.2.5
.

Let
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
be a QFSHA, let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
be its dual QUEA, as in
§
2.3.4
, and let also
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
, resp.
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
, be the QUEA, resp. the QFSHA, introduced in
§
2.4
. Let
\,\rho\,
be a
\varrho
–comatrix for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
which is congruent to
\epsilon^{\otimes 2}
modulo
\hbar\,
.

Then, for the two morphisms

\;{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;

and

\;{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\;

in Proposition
5.2.2
, the following holds:

(a)  they take values inside
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
,  so they corestrict to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}:{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}={{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups.

(b)  they uniquely extend to
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,  i.e. they
extend to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras.

Proof.

(a)
By the assumption
\,\rho\equiv\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\pmod{\hbar}\,
and by
Proposition
5.1.4
(b)
, we can write
\rho
in the form

\;\rho\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\,\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,\big)\;

for some
\;\zeta\in{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,\Big)}^{\!*}\,
.
Then

\hfil\displaystyle\zeta\,\in\,{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,\Big)}^{\!*}\,=\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{*}\,=\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{*}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{*}\,=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,\big)}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,\big)}^{\prime}\,=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}

— thanks to (
2.18
) — hence

\;\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,\in\,\hbar^{-1}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\prime}\;
.
Now, the right-hand side of (
2.12
) for
\,\sigma:=\rho\,
implies

\;\zeta(1\,,a)=0\;
for all
\,a\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  hence

\,\zeta(-,a)\in\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\big)\,
and so

\;\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta(-,a)\,\in\,{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\big)}^{\vee}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\;
for all

\,a\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
. This implies that

\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta(-\,,-)\;\in\;{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\vee}\!\otimes{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,=\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\otimes{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}

(5.2)

where hereafter we are being temporarily sloppy with the tensor product
— we fix this later on. Clearly, (
5.2
) implies

\;\rho\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\big)\,\in\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\otimes{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\;

as well. Therefore we get at once
\;\Psi_{\rho}^{\leftarrow}(\ell):=\rho(\ell\,,-)\,\in\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\;

for all
\,\ell\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  q.e.d. This proves the claim about
\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}\,
,
and that concerning
\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}
is entirely similar.

It remains to “dot your
i
’s” about the tensor product in
(
5.2
). In fact,
a priori

we have
\,\rho\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  hence also

\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\;\in\;{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}=\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

— where the (completed, topological) tensor product
“
\,\widehat{\otimes}\,
” is considered.
On the other hand, we have found that

\zeta\;\in\;{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\big)}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\big)}^{\prime}\,=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}

— where the (completed, topological) tensor product
“
\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,
” is used. Then the critical point is:
what kind of tensor product “
\,\otimes\,
” is taken in
(
5.2
)?

Instead of giving a direct, formal answer to this question, we point out the following.
First observe that
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\widetilde{\otimes}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)}^{\prime}\,
naturally embeds into
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
Then, when
\;\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,\in\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;

is expanded into some (suitably convergent) series

\,\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta=\beta^{i}\otimes\beta_{i}\,

(summing over repeated indices) with
\,\beta^{i},\beta_{i}\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
for all
i\,
,
what we proved above is that we actually have

\,\beta_{i}\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\;\big(\!\subseteq{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\big)\,
for all indices
i\,
.
This is what we loosely wrote as

\;\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,\in\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\otimes{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,=\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\otimes{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\;

in (
5.2
) above.

(b)
Acting as in part
(a)
, we find

\,\rho=\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,\big)\,
with

\;\zeta\in{\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\;
and

\;\zeta\in{\big(\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}\big)\big)}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\,

too, so
\;\zeta\in{\big(\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\big)\big)}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\;
. Since
\,\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}}\big)\subseteq\hbar\,\textsl{Ker}\big(\epsilon_{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}\big)\,
,  this implies that, expanding

\,\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,
as a (convergent) series

\;\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,=\,\beta^{i}\otimes\beta_{i}\,
,  we can assume

\,\beta^{i}\in{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
. As
\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}={\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\big)}^{\prime}={\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{*}\,
, we end up with

\hbar^{-1}\,\zeta\,=\,\beta^{i}\otimes\beta_{i}\;\in\;{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,\big)}^{*}\otimes{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

(5.3)

where again the meaning of the tensor product “
\,\otimes\,
”
considered in this formula (along with the corresponding convergence issues) is
handled just as in part
(a)
. Finally, from (
5.3
)
it follows at once that
\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}
extends from
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
to

{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}
as claimed. This proves our statement for

\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}\,
, and the case of
\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}

is entirely similar.
∎

5.2.6
.

Duality properties.
When we deal with a QUEA and a QFSHA which are dual to each other, it makes sense to compare the previous results. The outcome is that Proposition
2.2.12
turns to an enhanced version (with trivial proof), as follows:

Theorem 5.2.7
.

Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA,
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
a QFSHA, which are dual to each other, i.e.
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
. Let
\,\mathcal{R}=\rho\,
be an
R
–matrix for
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and a
\varrho
–comatrix for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
, which is trivial modulo
\hbar\,
, i.e. congruent to
1^{\otimes 2}
, resp. to
\epsilon^{\otimes 2}
, modulo
\hbar\,
.
Then, for the morphisms in Proposition
5.2.1
, Proposition
5.2.2
, Theorem
5.2.3
and Theorem
5.2.5
we have the following identifications

\overleftarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}\;,\,\;\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}\;,\,\;\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}\quad\text{and}\quad\overrightarrow{\Phi}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\Psi}_{\!\rho}\;,\,\;\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}\;,\,\;\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}

[formula]

5.2.8
.

Comparing morphisms (1).
Let us fix assumptions as in
Theorem
5.2.3
:
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
is a given QUEA,

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
its dual
QFSHA, and
\,\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}^{s}\otimes\mathcal{R}_{s}\,
is a (quantum)

R
–matrix of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
Then from Theorem
5.2.3
we have Hopf algebra
morphisms

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;,\;\;\qquad{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}

(5.4)

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups, and

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;\;,\quad\qquad{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}

(5.5)

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras, which we re-write in the form

{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;\;\;,\quad\qquad{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

(5.6)

that is entirely equivalent. We now go and compare (
5.4
) and (
5.6
).

Recall that
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\,
,
which are in duality because
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
are in duality (by construction)
and we can apply (
2.18
). Then also
{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}

and
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}
are in duality, as well as
{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}

and
{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\,
.

We are now ready to compare the morphisms in (
5.4
)
with those in (
5.6
).
Namely, we have a couple of diagrams

\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}
\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}

(5.7)

where the vertical, twisting lines denote a relationship of mutual (Hopf) duality.
Next result tells us that the link between the morphisms on top row and those underneath
is indeed “the best possible one”:

Theorem 5.2.9
.

The two morphisms in left-hand side, resp. in right-hand side, of
(
5.7
) are adjoint to each other, that is for all

\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,
and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
