we have

\Big\langle\,\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(f)\Big\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Big\langle\,\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(f)\Big\rangle

where by “
\,\big\langle\,\ ,\ \big\rangle
”
we denote the pairing between any two Hopf algebras in duality.

Proof.

It is enough to prove half of the claim — the other one being entirely similar — say the right-hand side. Direct computation yields

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\Big\langle\,\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\big\langle\eta\,,\mathcal{R}^{s}\big\rangle\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\big\langle\eta\,,\mathcal{R}^{s}\big\rangle\,\big\langle\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\,,f\,\big\rangle\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\big\langle\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\,,f\,\big\rangle\,\big\langle\eta\,,\mathcal{R}^{s}\big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\mathcal{R}^{s}\big\langle\,\mathcal{R}_{s}\,,f\,\big\rangle\!\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}(f)\Big\rangle\quad

for all
\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,
and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
hence we are done.
∎

As a second step, let now start with assumptions as in
Theorem
5.2.5
:
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
is a given QFSHA,

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
its dual QUEA, and
\,\rho\,
is a (quantum)

\varrho
–comatrix of
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
. Then
Theorem
5.2.5
provides Hopf algebra morphisms

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;,\;\;\qquad{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}

(5.8)

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups, and

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;\;\;,\quad\qquad{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}

(5.9)

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras;
we re-write the latter as

{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;\;\;,\quad\qquad{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}}}}\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

(5.10)

that is entirely equivalent. We now go and compare
(
5.8
) and (
5.10
).

Acting as before (for the morphisms induced by an
R
–matrix),
we find diagrams

\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}
\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}

(5.11)

where the vertical, twisting lines denote a relationship of mutual (Hopf) duality.
Again, the link between the morphisms on top row and those underneath turns
out to be “the best possible one”, as the following result claims:

Theorem 5.2.10
.

The two morphisms in left-hand side, resp. in right-hand side, of
(
5.7
) are adjoint to each other,
that is for all
\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,

and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
we have

\Big\langle\,\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}(f)\Big\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Big\langle\,\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}(f)\Big\rangle

where by “
\,\big\langle\,\ ,\ \big\rangle
”
we denote the pairing between any two Hopf algebras in duality.

Proof.

We prove the left-hand side of the claim, the other side being similar.
By direct computation we find

\Big\langle\,\overrightarrow{\Psi}^{\vee}_{\!\rho}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\big\langle\,\rho\,(-,\eta)\,,f\,\big\rangle\;=\;\rho\big(\,f,\eta\big)\;=\;\big\langle\,\eta\,,\rho\,(\,f,-)\,\big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overleftarrow{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\!\rho}(f)\Big\rangle

for all
\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,
and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  as requested.
∎

5.3.
Morphisms from polar
R\,
–matrices and polar
\varrho\,
–comatrices

We shall now explore what happens when the constructions leading to
Proposition
2.2.9
or
Proposition
2.2.10
, respectively, is (tentatively)
applied to a QFSHA and a polar
R
–comatrix for it, or to a QUEA and a polar
\varrho
–comatrix for it, respectively. Much like for deformations by polar twists or polar
2
–cocycles,
we eventually achieve a nice result by “watching through the lens” of the QDP.

As a first result, we find that the construction of Hopf morphisms as in
Proposition
2.2.9
can be applied again
(though it might not be done through direct application of the abstract,
general recipe) when the Hopf algebra under scrutiny is a QFSHA and its

R
–matrix is indeed only (!) a polar
R
–matrix.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}
be a QFSHA, and
\mathcal{R}
a polar
R
–matrix for it.
Then the recipes in Proposition
2.2.9

provide two well-defined morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\big)}^{*}={\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{*}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}\!={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\big)}^{*}={\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{*}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}\!={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

Proof.

This follows from a direct application of Proposition
5.2.1

to the QUEA
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
and its
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}\,
.
∎

The previous result provide morphisms from a QFSHA to a QUEA, just like
Proposition
5.2.1
did. Following a similar path,
we shall now improve such a result — much like we did in
§
5.2
—
finding a couple of morphisms between QFSHA’s and another couple
between QUEA’s.

Theorem 5.3.2
.

Assume that
\,\mathcal{R}
is a
polar
R
–matrix
for the QFSHA

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  i.e. an
R
–matrix for the QUEA

\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}=:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,
,  of the form

\;\mathcal{R}\,=\,\exp\big(\hbar^{-1}r\big)\;
for some

\,r\in{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}\,2}\,
. Then, for the two morphisms

\,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{R}}\,
and

\,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\mathcal{R}}\,
in
Proposition
5.3.1
above, the following holds:

(a)  they corestrict to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}\!:{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}=\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{\!*}\!\longrightarrow\!{\Big(\!\hskip-1.0pt{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}\Big)}^{\!\prime}\!=\!{\Big(\!\hskip-1.0pt{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\prime}\Big)}^{\!\text{\rm cop}}\!\!={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}:{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}=\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{\!*}\!\longrightarrow\!{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}\Big)}^{\!\prime}\!=\!{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\prime}\Big)}^{\!\text{\rm op}}\!={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups;

(b)  they extend to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}:\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}={\Big(\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{*}\Big)}^{\!\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}:\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}={\Big(\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{*}\Big)}^{\!\vee}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm op}}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras.

Proof.

First of all, note that the chain of identities

{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\text{\rm cop}}\Big)}^{\prime}\,=\,{\Big(\!{\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}\big)}^{\prime}\Big)}^{\!\text{\rm cop}}\,=\,{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}

— and similarly with superscript “op” instead of “cop” throughout —
is obvious from definitions along with the fact that Drinfeld’s functors

\,{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
and
\,{(\ )}^{\vee}\,
are inverse to each other.
Similarly, it is also obviously true the chain of identities

{\Big(\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{*}\Big)}^{\!\vee}\,=\;{\Big(\!{\big({F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\big)}^{\prime}\Big)}^{\!\star}\,=\;{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,=\;{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}

As to the rest of the claim, everything follows from Theorem
5.2.3
applied to the QUEA
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}:={F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}^{\vee}\,
along with its
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}\,
.
∎

Now we go for the dual constructions, concerning polar
\rho
–comatrices for a QUEA:

Proposition 5.3.3
.

Assume that
\rho
is a
polar
\varrho
–comatrix

for the QUEA
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  i.e. an element of the form

\;\rho\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varrho\big)\;
for some

\,\varrho\in\Big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{*}\,

— taking into account Lemma
3.3.2
—
which obeys (
2.13
).

Then the recipes in Proposition
2.2.10

provide two well-defined morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\,\rho}\;:\;{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\star}\big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\,\rho}\;:\;{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm op}}=\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\star}\big)^{\text{\rm op}}=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

Proof.

Everything follows from definitions once we apply
Proposition
5.2.2
to the QFSHA

\,F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]:={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,
,
also taking into the account the chain of (obvious) identities

\;{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}={{F[[G^{*}\hskip 0.5pt]]}}^{\star}={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\;
.
∎

Once again, the previous result provides morphisms from a QFSHA to a QUEA,
and now we “enhance” it — like we did with
Theorem
5.3.2
—
finding morphisms between QFSHA’s and morphisms between QUEA’s:

Theorem 5.3.4
.

Assume that
\rho
is a
polar
\varrho
–comatrix
for the QUEA

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  i.e. an element of the form

\;\rho\,=\,\exp_{*}\!\big(\hbar^{-1}\varrho\big)\;
for some

\,\varrho\in\Big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\widehat{\otimes}\,2}\Big)^{*}\,

— taking into account Lemma
3.3.2
—
which obeys (
2.13
).
Then, for the two morphisms
\,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\,\rho}\,
and

\,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\,\rho}\,

in Proposition
5.3.3
above,
the following holds:

(a)  they corestrict to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\,\rho}\;:\;F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Big(\!\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm cop\,}}\Big)}^{\prime}=\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=:{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\,\rho}\;:\;F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]={U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Big(\!\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm op\,}}\Big)}^{\prime}=\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)^{\text{\rm op}}=:{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QFSHA’s for mutually dual (formal) Poisson groups;

(b)  they extend to morphisms

\begin{matrix}\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\,\rho}\;:\;{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\vee}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\star}\big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\phantom{{}_{\big|}}\\
\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\,\rho}\;:\;{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\vee}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\text{\rm op}}=\big({{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}}^{\star}\big)^{\text{\rm op}}=\,{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\phantom{{}^{\big|}}\end{matrix}

and

between QUEA’s for mutually dual Lie bialgebras.

Proof.

As the functors
\,{(\ )}^{\prime}\,
and
\,{(\ )}^{\vee}\,

are inverse to each other, definitions yield

{\Big(\!\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\star}\Big)^{\!\!\text{\rm cop\,}}\Big)}^{\!\prime}\!=\!{\Big(\!\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\star}\Big)^{\prime}\,\Big)}^{\!\!\text{\rm cop}}\!\!={\Big(\!\Big(\!{\big({U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}^{\prime}\,\big)}^{\!\vee}\Big)^{\!*}\,\Big)}^{\!\!\text{\rm cop}}\!\!=\big({{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\big)^{\text{\rm cop}}=:{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}

— and similarly with superscript “op” instead of “cop” throughout —
also thanks to
\,{(\ )}^{\prime}\circ{(\ )}^{\star}={(\ )}^{*}\circ{(\ )}^{\vee}\,
.
Basing on this, the entire claim follows at once from
Theorem
5.2.5
applied to the QFSHA

\,{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\prime}\,
and to its
\varrho
–comatrix
\rho\,
.
∎

5.3.5
.

Duality properties.
If we consider a QUEA and a QFSHA which
are dual to each other, we can compare the previous results:
thus we find the following “polar analogue”
— whose proof is trivial again — of
Theorem
5.2.7
:

Theorem 5.3.6
.

Let
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA,
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,

a QFSHA, which are dual to each other, i.e.
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
and
\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}={{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\star}\,
.
Let
\,\rho=\mathcal{R}\,
be a polar
\rho
–comatrix for

\,{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and a polar
R
–matrix for
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.

Then, for the morphisms in Proposition
5.3.1
,
Proposition
5.3.3
,
Theorem
5.3.2
and
Theorem
5.3.4

we have the following identifications

\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\rho}\;,\,\;\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}\;,\,\;\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}\hskip 9.0pt\text{and}\hskip 9.0pt\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}_{\rho}\;,\,\;\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}\;,\,\;\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}=\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}

[formula]

5.3.7
.

Comparing morphisms (2).
We shall now compare
morphisms among quantum groups provided by a polar
R
–matrix
as above; the analysis is pretty similar to what we did with
R
–matrices,
so we can be quicker.

Let us start with a QFSHA
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  with dual QUEA denoted by

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,  and a polar
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}
for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.
Then Theorem
5.3.2

gives a couple of diagrams

\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}
\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}

(5.12)

where the vertical, twisting lines denote a relationship of mutual (Hopf)
duality while the horizontal arrows are Hopf algebra morphisms.
Next result is the “polar analogue” of Theorem
5.2.9
,
telling us that the morphisms on top row and those underneath are
“as close as possible”:

Theorem 5.3.8
.

The two morphisms in left-hand side, resp. in right-hand side, of
(
5.12
) are adjoint to each other,
that is for all
\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\,
we have

\Big\langle\,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}(f)\Big\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Big\langle\,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{R}}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Phi}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{R}}(f)\Big\rangle

where by “
\,\big\langle\,\ ,\ \big\rangle
”
we denote the pairing between any two Hopf algebras in duality.

Proof.

The proof follows from Theorem
5.2.9

along with Theorem
5.1.5
.
∎

Similarly, let
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
be a QUEA, with dual QFSHA denoted by

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  and let
\rho
be a polar
\varrho
–comatrix

{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
. Then Theorem
5.3.4

yields a couple of diagrams

\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}
\textstyle{{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-1.0pt\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm op}}}
\textstyle{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.0pt\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}}
\textstyle{{{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}}

(5.13)

where the vertical, twisting lines denote a relationship of mutual (Hopf) duality
while the horizontal arrows are Hopf algebra morphisms. We get now the
“polar analogue” of Theorem
5.2.10
,
which claims that the morphisms on top row of
(
5.13
) and those underneath are
“as close as possible”:

Theorem 5.3.9
.

The two morphisms in left-hand side, resp. in right-hand side, of
(
5.13
) are adjoint to each other,
that is for all
\,\eta\in{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
and
\,f\in{F_{\hbar}[[G^{*}]]}\,
we have

\Big\langle\,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}(f)\Big\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Big\langle\,\overleftarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\vee}_{\rho}(\eta)\,,f\Big\rangle\;=\;\Big\langle\eta\,,\overrightarrow{\underline{\Psi}}^{\prime}_{\rho}(f)\Big\rangle

where “
\,\big\langle\,\ ,\ \big\rangle
” denotes the pairing
between any two Hopf algebras in duality.

Proof.

Here again, the proof follows from Theorem
5.2.9

and Theorem
5.1.5
.
∎

5.4.
Semiclassical morphisms induced by specialization

We will now go and study the semiclassical limit of the various morphisms
among quantum groups, considered in §§
5.2

and
5.3
above, induced by
R
–matrices,

\varrho
–comatrices and their “
polar
counterparts”.

First we consider the case of an
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}
for a given QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
whose dual QFSHA is
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
. With this assumptions, we recall the existence of
the Hopf algebra morphisms in (
5.7
),
which by Theorem
5.2.9
are pairwise mutually adjoint.

Specialising
\hbar
to 0, the left-hand side of
(
5.7
) provides two mutually adjoint morphisms

\;{F[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}}}}\,{{F[[G^{*}\hskip 0.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\;

and

\;{{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}}}}\,{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
the first being a morphism of
Poisson
Hopf algebras, the second one of

co-Poisson
Hopf algebras. As they are mutually adjoint,
each one of them defines one and the same morphism of formal Poisson groups

\;\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}:G^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow G\;

where
\,G^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}\,
is the
opposite

(i.e., with opposite product) formal Poisson group to
G^{*}\,
. Note that

\,\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}:G^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow G\,

is directly defined by
\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}\,
,
while the morphism of Lie bialgebras

\;d\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}:{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}\;

can be deduced directly from
\,\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}\,
,
by restriction to
{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}
and corestriction to
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
.

Similarly, specialising
\hbar
to 0 the right-hand side of
(
5.7
) yields two mutually adjoint morphisms

\;{F[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overrightarrow{\Phi}^{\prime}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}}}}\,{{F[[G^{*}\hskip 0.5pt]]}}^{\text{\rm op}}\;

and

\;{{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}}^{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\overleftarrow{\Phi}^{\vee}_{\!\mathcal{R}}{\big|}_{\hbar=0}}}}\,{U(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\;

which in turn defines one single morphism of formal Poisson groups

\;\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}:G^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow G\;

where now
\,G^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}\,
denotes the
co-opposite

formal Poisson group to
G^{*}\,

— i.e., with same product but opposite Poisson structure.
This goes along with its associated morphism of Lie bialgebras

\;d\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}:{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}\;
.
In short, we have pairs of morphisms

G^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,G\;,\quad G^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,G\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\;,\quad{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}

(5.14)

of formal Poisson groups and of Lie bialgebras,
respectively.

Second, we consider the case of a
\varrho
–comatrix
\rho

for a given QFSHA
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  with dual QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
.
In this case, there exist Hopf algebra morphisms as in
(
5.11
),
which are pairwise mutually adjoint due to Theorem
5.2.10
.

Acting as before, specialising
\hbar
to 0 we find that the
semiclassical limits of these (quantum) morphisms eventually
define two pairs of morphisms

G^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\psi^{+}_{\rho}}}}\,G\;,\quad G^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\psi^{-}_{\rho}}}}\,G\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\psi^{+}_{\rho}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\;,\quad{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\psi^{-}_{\rho}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}

(5.15)

of formal Poisson groups and of Lie bialgebras, respectively.

Third, to compare the two constructions, assume that,
given mutually dual quantum groups
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,
we pick a single element
\,\mathcal{R}=\rho\,
,  thought of simultaneously
as an
R
–matrix for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and as a
\varrho
–comatrix for

{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  much in the spirit of Proposition
2.2.7

and Theorem
2.2.12
.
Then morphisms as in (
5.14
) and
(
5.15
) are defined: but in addition,
directly by Theorem
5.2.7

we get at once that

\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\,=\,\psi^{+}_{\rho}\;\;,\quad\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\,=\,\psi^{-}_{\rho}\quad\qquad\text{and}\qquad\quad d\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\,=\,d\psi^{+}_{\rho}\;\;,\quad d\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\,=\,d\psi^{-}_{\rho}

If one works instead with polar
R
–matrices and polar
\varrho
–comatrices,
the roles of
G
and
G^{*}
are reversed, but for the rest the analysis is
entirely similar (so we may be more sketchy). Therefore, assume we have dual
quantum groups
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}
and
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
.

Given a polar
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}
for
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  the Hopf algebra
morphisms in Theorem
5.3.2
give rise
(through their semiclassical limit) to two pairs of morphisms

G_{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\underline{\phi}^{+}_{\,\mathcal{R}}}}}\,G^{*}\;,\quad G_{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\underline{\phi}^{-}_{\,\mathcal{R}}}}}\,G^{*}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\underline{\phi}^{+}_{\,\mathcal{R}}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\;,\quad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\underline{\phi}^{-}_{\,\mathcal{R}}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}

(5.16)

of formal Poisson groups and of Lie bialgebras, respectively.

Similarly, if
\rho
is a polar
\varrho
–comatrix for
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
the Hopf algebra morphisms in
Theorem
5.3.4
define
(via their semiclassical limit) two pairs of morphisms

G_{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\underline{\psi}^{+}_{\,\rho}}}}\,G^{*}\;,\quad G_{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\underline{\psi}^{-}_{\,\rho}}}}\,G^{*}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm op}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\underline{\psi}^{+}_{\,\rho}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\;,\quad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\text{\rm cop}}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d\underline{\psi}^{-}_{\,\rho}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}

(5.17)

of formal Poisson groups and of Lie bialgebras, respectively.

Finally, if
\,\mathcal{R}=\rho\,
— in the spirit of
Proposition
2.2.7
, more precisely like in
Theorem
5.3.6

— then Theorem
5.3.6

gives at once

\underline{\phi}^{+}_{\,\mathcal{R}}\,=\,\underline{\psi}^{+}_{\,\rho}\;\;,\quad\underline{\phi}^{-}_{\,\mathcal{R}}\,=\,\underline{\psi}^{-}_{\,\rho}\quad\qquad\text{and}\qquad\quad d\underline{\phi}^{+}_{\,\mathcal{R}}\,=\,d\underline{\psi}^{+}_{\,\rho}\;\;,\quad d\underline{\phi}^{-}_{\,\mathcal{R}}\,=\,d\underline{\psi}^{-}_{\,\rho}

Studying in depth all the morphisms introduced above seems to be quite an
interesting problem; we cannot, however, cope with in the present paper
— we just finish with a comparison with previous results.

Assume we have an
R
–matrix
\mathcal{R}
for a given QUEA
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
,
whose dual QFSHA is
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}:={{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}}^{*}\,
. It is well-known that
the “semiclassical limit” of
\mathcal{R}\,
, that is

\,\displaystyle{r:={{\,\mathcal{R}-1^{\otimes 2}\,}\over\hbar}\pmod{\hbar}}\,
,
is in turn a “classical
r
–matrix” for the Lie bialgebra
{\mathfrak{g}}\,
.
Then Lie bialgebra morphisms

\;{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm op}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi^{+}_{r}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\;
and

\;{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{\text{\rm cop}}{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi^{-}_{r}}}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\;
are
defined directly through
r
itself — with no need of
\mathcal{R}\,
,
nor of
{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}})}\,
, nor
{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\,
,  cf.
[
CP
]
, §2.1, or

[
Mj
]
, §8.1. Tracking the various constructions involved
— in particular, the functor
\,{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}\mapsto{{F_{\hbar}[[G\hskip 1.5pt]]}}^{\vee}=:{U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt{\mathfrak{g}}^{*})}\,

— by direct comparison one immediately sees that

\varphi^{+}_{r}\,=\,d\phi^{+}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\quad\qquad\text{and}\qquad\quad\varphi^{-}_{r}\,=\,d\phi^{-}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}

In particular, we get that
the morphisms
d\phi^{\pm}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}

depend on
r
alone, rather than on
\mathcal{R}\,
,
hence the same is true for the morphisms
\phi^{\pm}_{{}_{\mathcal{R}}}\,
;
indeed, both facts can also be easily proved by direct inspection.
Similarly, one can prove, via direct analysis again, or by a duality argument
from the previous result, that
the morphisms
\psi^{\pm}_{\rho}
and

d\psi^{\pm}_{\rho}
depend only on the
“classical
\varrho
–comatrix”

\displaystyle{\rho_{0}:={{\,\rho-\epsilon^{\otimes 2}\,}\over\hbar}\pmod{\hbar}}

alone
, rather than on
\rho\,
.
